# Character Creation

**Variant:** INTERNAL-FROZEN-DESIGN / EXPLICIT-RENDER-CHECKPOINT / CONCEPT-INTEGRATED-INTRINSIC-COVERAGE / THREE-SHEET-PHASE-1 / RECURSIVE-SELECTOR / TLCR / DESIGN-DIRECTION-COMPANION / DIRECT-FINAL-RENDER-CONTEXT

**Deterministic Character Creation / Broad Population Workflow**

**STATUS:** AUTHORITATIVE / CANONICAL / EXTENSIVE / PREMIUM-GACHA-ANIME-MANHWA-DESIGNER / CHARACTER-IDENTITY-AND-FUSION-COEQUAL / BODY-FIRST-IN-PHASE-1 / COMPLETE-CONCEPT-FUSION / MAXIMAL-INTENSITY-FIRST / PRE-GENERATION-VALIDATED / INTERNAL-FROZEN-SPECIFICATION / EXPLICIT-RENDER-ONLY / RENDERER-CAPABILITY-GATED / 2D-NATIVE / FEMALE-CHARACTER / INDEPENDENT-CHARACTER-AUTHORSHIP / FIFTEEN-CHARACTER-BROAD-PHASE-1 / THREE-SHEETS-OF-FIVE / MANDATORY-THREE-VIEW / RECURSIVE-SELECTOR / TLCR / DESIGN-DIRECTION-ROUTED / NO-MANDATORY-CAPSULE-FILE-HANDOFF

**AUTHORITY:** `Character_Creation.md` is the sole execution authority. `Concepts.md` is the canonical concept / concept-knowledge / selector-data authority. `Design_Direction.md` is the Design Direction authority. Neither companion document may override execution, phase routing, candidate/output counts, state, validation, freezing, renderer preflight, Renderer Capability Gate, READY TO RENDER, image authorization, retries/failures, or presentation ownership defined here.


## 0.0 EXECUTION LOCK — HIGHEST PRECEDENCE / HARD / ABSOLUTE

This block governs routing, state, presentation, and final renderer handoff. It does **not** replace, compress, or weaken the stricter character, concept-materialization, outfit, weapon, fusion, differentiation, quality, final-refinement, robustness, validation, or failure rules elsewhere in this document.

**AUTHORITY BOUNDARY — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** `Character_Creation.md` owns commands, phases, counts, state, revision semantics, concept-resolution execution, construction methodology, fusion, differentiation, validation, freezing, serialization, renderer preflight, Renderer Capability Gate, READY TO RENDER, image authorization, retries/failures, presentation geometry, and final execution. `Concepts.md` owns canonical SC identities/IDs, canonical leaf contracts, recursive BC/SF hierarchy, aliases/views, selectable-leaf membership, materialization knowledge, mechanism/topology knowledge, and concept-specific boundaries. `Design_Direction.md` owns its registered Design Direction entries and phase-specific aesthetic grammars. Companion data is consumed by this execution authority and cannot weaken it.

**DIRECT PHASE-1 ROUTING — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** A supplied concept combination used as the active creation task, including a bare form such as `A × B × C`, is Phase-1 character creation unless the user explicitly requests analysis, text-only design, another phase, or another operation.

**CHARACTER-FIRST AUTHORSHIP LOCK — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Phase 1 creates premium female anime/manhwa characters. Concept fusion is the causal construction method, not a replacement for character authorship. Before image action, every candidate is a complete independent female character with resolved proportions, silhouette, face, eyes, hair, body regime, materials, colour/value architecture, concept-fusion architecture, topology/appendages where applicable, asymmetry where useful, intrinsic body-owned structures, and flagship-level finishing.

**DESIGN BEFORE IMAGE — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** The raw concept string is never the operative visual instruction. Complete slot resolution/TLCR intake where applicable, literal extraction, physical decomposition, maximal-intensity complete fusion, independent character construction, differentiation proof, alternative-design search, subordinate influences, Design Direction routing, final identity authorship, final refinement, image-generation robustness, Frozen Character Specification, Clean Renderer-Facing Description, FINAL RENDER CONTEXT, renderer preflight, and Renderer Capability Gate before READY TO RENDER.

**MAXIMAL-INTENSITY-FIRST CONCEPT LOCK — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Every primary concept begins from its strongest coherent character-level embodiment, not a minimal cue. Preserve the complete major recognizable vocabulary; give every primary concept unmistakable macro or strong-meso physical presence; resolve every required pairwise interaction and the complete-set higher-order interaction before aesthetic simplification. If mentally removing one primary concept leaves substantially the same body architecture, the concept is under-materialized and the character is not render-eligible. The complete strict materialization rules in §5.3 and §5.4 remain protected and take precedence over any weaker interpretation of companion data.

**FIFTEEN-CHARACTER BROAD PHASE-1 POPULATION LOCK — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Every fresh Broad Phase-1 CREATE authors exactly **fifteen** independent female characters as one population. Sheet 1 = Characters 01–05; Sheet 2 = Characters 06–10; Sheet 3 = Characters 11–15. The sheets are presentation/output partitions only. Characters 06–15 are not derived from Characters 01–05, and no later sheet is designed from pixels produced by an earlier sheet. All fifteen designs are fully resolved, refined, frozen, certified, and sheet-context-compiled before the first initial-sheet render is allowed.

**WITHIN-SHEET DIFFERENTIATION + GLOBAL COLLISION LOCK — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Apply the checkpoint-strength five-way divergence/fusion matrix and all ten pairwise structural comparisons independently to Characters 01–05, 06–10, and 11–15, including macro-body divergence floor, independent Concept-Fusion Architectures, anti-template checks, material-discriminability, face/head/hair divergence, silhouette divergence, proportion/body-regime independence, and topology/appendage independence where applicable. Then perform a global fifteen-character collision review for literal duplicates, mirror-equivalent duplicates, repeated body chassis, repeated complete fusion architecture, and identity construction severe enough to make characters interchangeable. Repeated resolved input leaves or Design Directions are not themselves diversity failures; repeated character construction is.

**FIVE-REFERENCE SCOPE LOCK — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** The inherited checkpoint contains detailed five-candidate differentiation, comparison, quality, and sheet-audit clauses. In the integrated fifteen-character runtime, any such Phase-1 statement that refers to `the five characters`, `five bodies`, or a `five-character population` without explicitly declaring a total CREATE count is scoped independently to the applicable initial sheet group (01–05, 06–10, or 11–15). It never reduces the total Broad Phase-1 CREATE cardinality below fifteen. Explicit fifteen-character rules govern total-population state; explicit five-character rules govern each sheet-local proof/presentation group; the global collision review governs cross-sheet reuse.


**PRIMARY SLOT / SELECTOR LOCK — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Primary slots are canonical fixed atomic, open literal fixed, selector-family, unrestricted wildcard `X`, or constrained wildcard `X{query}`; lowercase `x` normalizes to `X`. Autonomous selection uses canonical selectable SC leaves only, uniformly across the resolved eligible pool, with replacement across candidates. BC/SF nodes, aliases, mechanism/topology records, and local variants/reference forms never materialize as autonomous selected concepts. Freeze each candidate's complete Resolved Primary Concept Set before decomposition/body construction and never reroll merely because the combination is difficult. Primary wildcard randomness is distinct from Sub-concept-2 randomness.

**OPEN-LITERAL / TLCR LOCK — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** A user-fixed concept absent from `Concepts.md` remains valid and fixed. Create a conversation-local Temporary Literal Concept Record; do not invent an SC ID, silently replace it with a nearest canonical leaf, modify `Concepts.md`, or make it wildcard-eligible. Canonical promotion is a separate explicit database operation.

**DESIGN-DIRECTION LOCK — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Every Phase-1 character receives an eligible ACTIVE / COMPLETE Design Direction unless the user explicitly specifies one for a named scope. Selection is reasoned, per character, after mandatory primary consequences/provisional body architecture are understood and before final Phase-1 freeze. No quota applies; repeats are allowed. The selected direction freezes through Phases 1–3; Phase 4 introduces no new Design Direction. Design Direction works only inside concept-valid solution space and never becomes a primary concept, sub-concept, rendering style, presentation controller, or substitute for fusion.

**MANDATORY THREE-VIEW PHASE-1 PRESENTATION — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Every identity on every initial sheet has exactly three depictions of the same frozen character: (1) one dominant uncropped full-body true-front neutral-standing view; (2) one smaller full-body exact 90-degree profile; (3) one smaller full-body direct-rear view. Each sheet therefore contains exactly five identities and fifteen full-body figure depictions. Only viewpoint changes. No duplicate front, three-quarter profile, redesigned side/rear body, action pose, fashion pose, contrapposto, walking, crouching, or combat stance.

**PHASE-1 INTRINSIC-COVERAGE LOCK — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Every Phase-1 figure resolves its chest and lower-torso presentation surface solely through intrinsic concept-derived body materialization. No shared studio/reference layer, neutral underlayer, or fallback garment-analogue is used. Any visible coverage must remain body-owned constituent matter: concept-valid, non-garment-like, non-propagating into Phase 2, and non-obscuring of appendage roots, topology, intrinsic material transitions, or defining anatomy. **RENDERER-FACING COVERAGE LANGUAGE FIREWALL:** renderer-facing wording states only the positive intrinsic material/surface construction actually present on the body. It never frames the presentation through a negative-state description of what is missing or absent and never asks the renderer to infer omitted presentation content. Internal exclusion rules remain internal.

**FINAL RENDER CONTEXT — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** The renderer derives visual intent only from one self-contained sheet-local FINAL RENDER CONTEXT bound to the exact frozen revision set. For each initial sheet, serialize in this order: **(1) immutable presentation envelope; (2) one concise shared intrinsic-coverage paragraph; (3) active illustrated 2D drawing/material grammar; (4) exactly five complete shortest-lossless current character payloads in left-to-right order; (5) cross-view consistency lock; (6) terminal layout lock.** Each sheet context contains only its five characters. Selector/random bookkeeping, SC/DD IDs, Era/Culture/Civilization labels, Sub-concept labels, raw concept formulae, validation prose, rejected alternatives, reasoning, changelog data, and unrelated history remain outside. Preserve every macro/strong-meso visible decision needed for faithful reconstruction.

**NO MANDATORY CAPSULE/HYDRATION HANDOFF — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Renderer Capsule `.txt`/`.md` files are never an execution dependency. Do not require file creation, delivery, downloading, re-reading, dispatch hydration, file attachment ingestion, file-as-render-source semantics, or any second renderer handoff authority. Optional text/markdown export may be produced only on explicit user request for inspection/copying; frozen runtime state and its FINAL RENDER CONTEXT remain authoritative.

**SEMANTIC-LABEL ISOLATION — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Exact user concept words remain in internal traceability. Renderer-facing text states the resolved visible construction rather than raw semantic labels as thematic instructions. Do not import roles, biography, scenario, environment, symbolic props, or adjacent identities.

**FINAL-CONTEXT SELF-SUFFICIENCY — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** No renderer context says `same as above`, `use previous design`, `make them unique`, or another contextual shortcut. Every active character payload independently preserves its render-critical visible identity.

**TARGETED PHASE-1 ALTER / REFINE / FUSE PRESENTATION — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** A targeted single-character Phase-1 revision uses one exact 9:16 vertical white analytical field: one dominant uncropped full-body true front, one smaller exact 90-degree profile, one smaller direct rear, same frozen identity, neutral standing. It remains a targeted revision, not a new fifteen-character CREATE, and proceeds through resolve → final refinement → freeze → serialize → preflight → capability gate → READY TO RENDER.

**RENDERER CAPABILITY GATE — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Before any image call, determine whether the active renderer/handoff can receive the complete certified FINAL RENDER CONTEXT as one operative self-contained instruction through any documented operative instruction channel available to the current image-generation interface, including the active conversation context, without truncation, omission, unresolved macro inference, or dependence on unsupported hidden capabilities. A dedicated prompt field, file-hydration mechanism, hidden payload field, or native exact-aspect control is not required when the documented interface can consume the complete operative instruction through another available channel. Never pretend support for hidden prompt fields, file hydration, prompt isolation, exact pixel dimensions, multiple-image semantics, hidden payload priority, attachment ingestion, or any other capability that is not actually available. If the renderer cannot natively produce the certified exact canvas ratio, request the closest supported orientation/ratio and permit deterministic non-generative white-canvas fitting or padding after the single authorized image-generation call, provided no designed character content is cropped, regenerated, redesigned, rescaled non-uniformly, or materially altered. The certified exact ratio remains a delivered-sheet requirement, not a mandatory native renderer-control requirement. If the complete operative context still cannot be consumed faithfully, or required presentation priority cannot be preserved, mark **HANDOFF UNSUPPORTED**, preserve state, and make no image call.

**STRICT RENDER CHECKPOINT — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** CREATE / CONCEPT / ALTER / REFINE / FUSE may perform all internal design, validation, freezing, serialization, and preflight work. They **must not call image generation**. Only explicit user `RENDER` authorizes an image call. For the initial Broad Phase-1 three-sheet ready state, one explicit bare `RENDER` authorizes the full unattempted READY initial-sheet set in numerical order (Sheet 1 → Sheet 2 → Sheet 3), with exactly one image-generation call per targeted sheet. `RENDER SHEET 2` or another explicit targeted form authorizes only that targeted sheet. Outside that initial three-sheet batch behavior, one `RENDER` authorizes exactly one image-generation call. No automatic image submission, no implicit continuation beyond the explicitly authorized target set, no `n=3`, no one-call request for three alternatives for the same sheet, and no automatic retry.

**INITIAL THREE-SHEET READY STATE — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** After all fifteen characters are designed: freeze Characters 01–05 as Sheet-1 revision set, 06–10 as Sheet-2 revision set, and 11–15 as Sheet-3 revision set; compile/preflight/capability-check one sheet-local FINAL RENDER CONTEXT for each; mark each passing sheet independently READY TO RENDER. No image call occurs at certification. When several initial sheets are READY, a bare `RENDER` targets all unattempted READY initial sheets in numerical order. `RENDER SHEET 2` targets only Sheet 2 when ready. There is no separate implicit `RENDER ALL`; the bare explicit `RENDER` command already serves as the initial all-ready-sheet render command.

**RENDER-ATTEMPT PRECOMMIT + POST-CALL STATE — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Immediately before the authorized image call, create an immutable attempt reservation bound to the explicit `RENDER` authorization, target sheet/revision set or explicitly authorized initial-sheet set, exact FINAL RENDER CONTEXT, and generation settings. After each call, the first post-call action is preservation: preserve any accessible returned image/reference before visual audit or status narration, then inspect the actual result and record success/failure while keeping frozen design separate from image outcome. If the runtime exposes image bytes or a stable file/artifact path, preserve that exact returned asset and, when a tool-supported copy/materialize/export action is actually available, immediately create a durable conversation-owned copy before audit. If it exposes only an opaque image reference, preserve the reference and do not claim a durable pixel copy. If no copy/export capability exists, record that limitation. If execution is interrupted before any returned image/reference becomes accessible to the assistant, do not claim pixel persistence or reconstruct the missing output from memory; retain the precommitted attempt record and classify the output state honestly under §10.3–§10.4. A failure on one targeted sheet does not redesign or retry that sheet and does not invalidate the other frozen sheets. Under a bare initial Broad Phase-1 `RENDER`, the remaining sheets in the already authorized target set may still proceed in numerical order. Any retry after the authorized target set is exhausted requires another explicit `RENDER`.

**REVISION RENDER-SOURCE LOCK — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** ALTER/REFINE/FUSE creates a newly resolved complete character revision from certified state and genuine dependencies. Previous rendered pixels never become the operative rendering canvas/source or a substitute for the new revision. Observational evidence from prior images may be inspected where useful, but design state remains authoritative.

**FAILURE / RETRY LOCK — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** No automatic retry and no silent redesign after renderer failure. A failed image is immutable attempt evidence, not canonical character state and not permission to weaken specifications. If failure is purely serialization/handoff and frozen design remains valid, repair only that layer, rerun preflight/capability checks, return the exact design to READY TO RENDER, and require another explicit `RENDER`. Never hide a retry behind a new ID.

**INTERNAL/PUBLIC SEPARATION — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** TLCRs, selector ledgers, random pools/mechanisms, Sub-concept rationale, Design Direction routing, alternative searches, fusion ledgers, five-way matrices, pairwise comparisons, global collision review, Frozen Character Specifications, validation matrices, renderer preflight/capability bookkeeping, and internal FINAL RENDER CONTEXT text remain internal unless the user explicitly requests inspection.

**PHASE OWNERSHIP LOCK — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Phase 2 owns outfit only and preserves the exact frozen character; Phase 3 owns weapon only and preserves frozen character + outfit; Phase 4 assembles frozen body/outfit/weapon without redesign. Deterministic blueprint improvements may increase completeness inside the owning phase but may not cross ownership boundaries.

Before every initial Phase-1 image action, reconstruct this presentation contract:

> **ONE CONTINUOUS EXACT 16:9 LANDSCAPE IMAGE FOR THE TARGET SHEET**
>
> **EXACTLY FIVE LEFT-TO-RIGHT CHARACTER REGIONS**
>
> **EXACTLY THREE FULL-BODY VIEWS PER IDENTITY: DOMINANT TRUE FRONT + SMALLER EXACT PROFILE + SMALLER DIRECT REAR**
>
> **EXACTLY FIFTEEN FULL-BODY FIGURE DEPICTIONS TOTAL**
>
> **ALL VIEWS USE STRICT NEUTRAL ANALYTICAL STANDING GEOMETRY**
>
> **COMPLETE DESIGNED EXTENTS REMAIN VISIBLE**
>
> **PLAIN UNINTERRUPTED WHITE FIELD**
>
> **NATIVE PREMIUM 2D ANIME / MANHWA DRAWING WITH LINE-LED CONTOURS, SHAPE-FIRST FORM READING, GROUPED ILLUSTRATED SHADOWS, AND MATERIAL-SPECIFIC DRAWN BEHAVIOR**

If anything is unresolved, return to its owning design layer before image action. Never ask the renderer to complete character design.

## 0 PURPOSE

The document is the sole active Character Creation execution specification. It is a deterministic design-and-validation system, not a collection of suggestions.

Its role is not merely to materialize concepts into bodies. It must operate as an **elite gacha / anime / manhwa character designer** responsible for creating complete premium characters whose body, face, hair, identity, clothing, weapon, materials, colour architecture, silhouette, and final assembly are authored as one coherent franchise-grade design. The internal quality target is deliberately extreme: every completed character should be designed as though she were a flagship character expected to create exceptional attachment, recognition, collectability, merchandising potential, and commercial value on the scale of a major successful release. This is a design-quality benchmark, not a claim or guarantee of revenue.

The standard is not merely **“can these concepts be physically represented?”** The standard is **“can these concepts become an extraordinary premium character?”** Concept materialization remains mandatory and exact, but it serves the creation of a memorable character rather than replacing character-design judgment.

The initial Broad Phase-1 workflow produces exactly fifteen genuinely different female visual identities as one population, partitioned for presentation into three analytical sheets of five (Characters 01–05, 06–10, and 11–15). Fixed primary slots remain fixed across all fifteen; autonomous selector/wildcard slots resolve candidate-specifically before construction. Each character's complete frozen Resolved Primary Concept Set is the origin of that independently authored body: the body is physically authored as a consequence of that complete resolved combination, not as a pre-existing mannequin subsequently decorated by individual concept features.

The complete character is the actual long-range design target:

> **CONCEPTS → PREMIUM CHARACTER IDENTITY → BODY → CLOTHING → WEAPON → COMPLETE ICONIC CHARACTER**

Phase 1 establishes a premium foundational character body and identity. Phase 2 must elevate that exact character through a dedicated outfit. Phase 3 must complete her with a signature weapon. Phase 4 assembles the already-resolved systems without replacing them. Body, clothing, and weapon are not three unrelated outputs stacked together; they are successive design layers of one character.

The renderer receives a fully resolved and fully validated design description and realizes it. It does not complete unresolved design decisions.

The complete transaction is:

> **READ → RESOLVE → PROVE → AESTHETIC SEARCH → CONCEPT-INTEGRATED INTRINSIC COVERAGE → FINAL REFINEMENT → CERTIFY RENDER-READY BLUEPRINT → COMPILE SHEET-LOCAL FINAL RENDER CONTEXT → VALIDATE HANDOFF → READY TO RENDER → USER `RENDER` → EXACTLY ONE IMAGE SUBMISSION → AUDIT → RECORD OUTCOME → NO FURTHER CALL WITHOUT ANOTHER USER `RENDER`**

No failed pre-generation gate may be bypassed. No aesthetically weak but technically valid construction may be certified merely because its checklist is complete. No failed render becomes accepted state. Recording failure fulfils reporting only, never the failed visual requirement.

> **PRIMARY INPUT SPECIFICATION → FIFTEEN INDEPENDENT RESOLVED PRIMARY SETS / BODY-SOLUTION SEARCHES → COMPLETE FUSION PER BODY → AESTHETIC/TASTE SELECTION → THREE CHECKPOINT-STRENGTH FIVE-WAY SHEET PROOFS → GLOBAL FIFTEEN-CHARACTER COLLISION REVIEW → THREE ANALYTICAL SHEETS**

Fifteen characters are fifteen independently authored physical and aesthetic solutions governed by the same Primary Input Specification. They are not sequential variations of the first solution or of earlier sheets.

> **SUPPLIED CONCEPT COMBINATION → PHYSICAL-PROPERTY RESOLUTION → COMPLETE CONCEPT ARCHITECTURE → PREMIUM FEMALE CHARACTER BODY + IDENTITY**

The concepts are the origin of the Phase-1 bodies. The body is the sole Phase-1 visual object, but it must already read as the foundation of a premium named character rather than a concept experiment. Every concept must be unmistakably and materially complete, all required interactions must genuinely exist, and visual emphasis must remain selective enough to preserve aesthetic economy, hierarchy, and desirability.

---

## 1 GENERAL RULES

### 1.1 Input

A bare primary expression such as:

`CONCEPT A + CONCEPT B + CONCEPT C`

or `Elf × x × X{gemstone}` defines one **Primary Input Specification** containing fixed and/or autonomous primary slots. User-fixed canonical or open-literal concepts remain fixed in every initial character; selector-family and wildcard slots resolve independently per candidate under §1.7, §4.8, and §12. Each candidate's resulting complete Resolved Primary Concept Set freezes before decomposition and body construction.

A concept is a visual-design input. It is not permission to invent lore, narrative, psychology, symbolism, events, titles, occupations, relationships, environment, or semantic descriptions.

### 1.2 State

Maintain separately:

- active concept set;
- current fifteen-character Broad Phase-1 population and three initial sheet groups;
- each character blueprint;
- concept-causality/fusion records;
- structural fingerprint;
- appendage map;
- material/substrate map;
- sub-concepts;
- per-character Design Direction assignment;
- selector/TLCR resolution ledger where applicable;
- per-sheet FINAL RENDER CONTEXT and READY TO RENDER state;
- phase state;
- frozen body/clothing/weapon state;
- accepted image/reference;
- current design-space occupancy.

Temporary task data does not become permanent state until the corresponding gate passes.

### 1.3 Command classes

- **CREATE / CONCEPT:** creates the initial Broad Phase-1 population.
- **SELECT:** selects an existing character.
- **FREEZE:** freezes the resolved system.
- **UNFREEZE:** reopens the explicitly named system and dependent systems.
- **ALTER:** changes only the explicitly targeted property of an existing character.
- **REFINE:** changes an explicitly targeted property while preserving all other identity.
- **FUSE:** combines existing resolved character constructions when explicitly requested.
- **RESET:** clears task state while preserving this specification.

ALTER/REFINE never become fresh concept generation. Phase does not advance implicitly.

### 1.4 No trope substitution — HARD / ABSOLUTE

A supplied concept retains its literal identity throughout the workflow.

Resolve it through:

> **DIRECT CONCEPT PROPERTIES → BODY-RELEVANT PHYSICAL CONSEQUENCES**

Adjacent cultural, narrative, symbolic, stylistic, or conventional associations are not design authority. They may not replace the supplied concept, introduce a different identity, or carry the concept through an unrelated object, role, pose, environment, or species substitution.

The renderer receives only the already-resolved physical result. If the same design would remain after replacing the supplied concept with a familiar association, the interpretation is not sufficiently direct and must be rebuilt.
### 1.5 NO UNREQUESTED ASSOCIATED-IDENTITY IMPORT — HARD / ABSOLUTE

A supplied concept authorizes only itself and the physical consequences directly derived from it.

The compiler preserves the distinction between the supplied concept and any familiar association that might normally accompany it. Conventional shorthand never has authority to add a new species identity, costume identity, object identity, setting identity, narrative identity, or other adjacent category.

The governing route is:

> **CONCEPT PROPERTY → BODY-RELEVANT PHYSICAL CONSEQUENCE**

The supplied primary combination governs primary body construction. Authorized subordinate selections govern only their assigned later decisions.

For morphology- or creature-associated inputs, the default Phase-1 target remains a premium female character with an independently authored female/human-readable anatomical foundation. Resolve deliberately which systems transform, how strongly they transform, and which systems remain recognizably human/female in the selected stylized anatomy. A full nonhuman species body plan is used only when the user explicitly requests that transformation.

Before certification, every visually introduced major feature must resolve to one or more authorized sources: complete primary fusion, authorized subordinate influence, necessary anatomy, necessary character-identity construction, necessary material consequence, necessary drawing consequence, or required presentation geometry. Any feature without an authorized source is removed before serialization.

A fused structure may satisfy several sources simultaneously. Source bookkeeping never authorizes separate concept-to-body-part partitioning.
### 1.6 Standalone authority and complete-document use — HARD / ABSOLUTE

**Rule.** `Character_Creation.md` is the sole execution methodology for this workflow. `Concepts.md` and `Design_Direction.md` are companion data/grammar authorities only within their explicit boundaries. Read all three active files completely when their content is required; do not substitute a summary, remembered fragment, historical checkpoint, or prior render.

**Explanation.** Current task concepts and explicitly selected candidate state are task inputs, not competing methodologies. Generalized construction examples explain transferable principles only and never introduce their content into a new concept set.

**Application.** Preserve each supplied word exactly in task state and resolve the literal combination through the procedures in this document. Similar spelling, prior examples, or familiar associations do not authorize silent substitution.

**Failure rule.** Historical workflow state, unavailable references, prior retry behavior, or previous character pixels cannot become a hidden dependency of the active methodology.

**Validation.** Every execution-method requirement used for a generation resolves to this document; canonical concept facts/selector membership resolve to `Concepts.md`; active Design Direction facts resolve to `Design_Direction.md`. Every task-specific decision resolves to the current command or an authorized design decision recorded in the current blueprint.
### 1.7 Primary input cardinality, slot types, and design authority — HARD / ABSOLUTE

**Rule.** An initial Primary Input Specification contains two or more **primary slots**. There is no fixed upper limit imposed by this document. `+`, `×`, and an explicit list can delimit slots.

The runtime distinguishes **user-fixed literal concepts** from **autonomously resolved concepts**.

Every primary slot is exactly one of five kinds:

1. **Canonical fixed atomic slot** — a user-supplied concept that exactly matches a canonical selectable leaf in `Concepts.md`. Its SC identity and canonical materialization knowledge apply directly. Example: `Dragon`.
2. **Open literal fixed slot** — a user-supplied concept that does **not** currently exist as a canonical selectable leaf in `Concepts.md`. The literal user concept is still valid input and remains fixed exactly as supplied. It is materialized through the universal concept-to-body translation rules in this document using a Temporary Literal Concept Record. Example: `Chronoweave` when no canonical Chronoweave leaf exists.
3. **Selector-family slot** — a canonical branch/sub-branch name or registered branch view from `Concepts.md` §4. It resolves independently per candidate to one eligible descendant canonical selectable leaf under Section 12.
4. **Untyped wildcard slot `X`** — the workflow draws one eligible canonical selectable leaf uniformly from the complete current canonical leaf registry for that candidate.
5. **Typed wildcard slot `X{TYPE}`** — the workflow resolves the brace query under Section 12, builds the resulting eligible canonical leaf pool, then draws one leaf uniformly from that pool for that candidate.

**User-fixed concepts are open-vocabulary. Autonomous concepts are canonical-vocabulary.**

A concept supplied explicitly by the user MUST NOT be rejected merely because it is absent from `Concepts.md`. Absence from the canonical database means only that the concept lacks a pre-authored canonical SC entry; it does not make the literal user input invalid.

`Concepts.md` therefore has two distinct roles:
- it is the authoritative source for canonical selectable leaves, SC identities, family membership, wildcard eligibility, and pre-authored materialization knowledge;
- it is **not** a whitelist limiting what the user may explicitly type as a fixed concept.
### 1.8 Visual invention without narrative inference — HARD / ABSOLUTE

**Rule.** Concrete visual authorship is mandatory. Unrequested narrative, biography, role, setting, moral framing, title, epithet, or symbolic story is outside the design authority.

The assistant may choose anatomy, topology, materials, geometry, palette, and authorized subordinate influences because original character creation requires those decisions. Abstract or nonmaterial concepts receive explicitly authored physical interpretations; those interpretations are recorded as design choices rather than asserted as a unique literal anatomy.

Concept eligibility is open-ended. Material, structural, abstract, behavioural, social, contextual, cultural, emotional, symbolic, invented, and other concept classes may be valid when a faithful phase-appropriate visual construction can be resolved.

A visually authored mark, scar, cavity, material boundary, asymmetry, or similar feature is defined through visible geometry and physical integration only. No explanatory story is needed to make it valid.

**Validation.** Maintain a feature-origin record for every major visible decision. If a particular input is materially ambiguous and no faithful physical interpretation can be resolved, ask only the narrow meaning question required before certification.
### 1.9 Task state, commands, and user phase control — HARD / ABSOLUTE

**Rule.** Only the user starts or ends a phase. SELECT, FREEZE, completion of a catalogue, a successful audit, or a failed image does not itself change the phase.

**State records.** Keep separate the literal primary set; current phase and command scope; candidate identities; per-candidate blueprint revisions; the two subordinate selections; pre-generation certificates; Frozen Character Specifications; Clean Renderer-Facing Descriptions; Renderer Capability Gate status; READY TO RENDER status and its bound Revision-ID; submission records; immutable attempt outcomes; selected candidates; frozen systems; dependent fit certificates; comparison-space records; and preserved source candidates for alteration or fusion.

**Identity.** A Character-ID identifies a character; a Candidate-ID identifies an outfit or weapon candidate; a Revision-ID identifies one resolved design state. Revision identifiers track task state only and are not document version numbers. A sheet position is not an identity. Changing position does not create a new character.

**Commands.** CREATE/CONCEPT explicitly starts the requested design-authoring operation and authorizes the complete internal design cycle through final refinement, Frozen Specifications, serialization, renderer preflight, Renderer Capability Gate, and READY TO RENDER state. SELECT identifies existing candidates without changing them. FREEZE adopts the selected passing system for downstream use. UNFREEZE reopens only the named system and marks affected dependencies stale. ALTER targets stated properties. REFINE resolves or improves the stated target while preserving the remaining identity. FUSE combines explicitly selected constructions into one new result. ALTER, REFINE, and FUSE first resolve the new complete revision internally and then proceed through the same freeze → serialize → preflight → capability-gate → READY TO RENDER sequence. `RENDER` is the sole command that authorizes one image-generation call for the current READY TO RENDER Revision-ID; it changes no design state by itself. Any design-changing command clears READY TO RENDER until recertification. These commands remain inside the active phase.

**Application.** The user can select Characters 02 and 09, request a fusion, alter its waist architecture, compare it with another fusion, and then freeze the chosen result, all within Phase 1. The assistant must not start clothing until the user explicitly starts Phase 2. The same selection/fusion/alteration cycle applies to outfit candidates in Phase 2 and weapon candidates in Phase 3.

**Failure.** Starting the next phase because one character looks finished, assuming that only one candidate may be selected, or interpreting “move on” after a failed image as “start the next phase” violates phase control.

**Validation.** Each operation records the active phase, source IDs, requested targets, protected fields, candidate count, authorized output scope, and required prerequisites before any design work is committed to renderer-facing description/handoff.
#### 1.9.1 Certified execution and render checkpoint — HARD / ABSOLUTE

**Rule.** Design execution and image submission are separate authorization stages. A creation or development command resolves the complete design internally, freezes the exact current revision, compiles the renderer-facing description, passes renderer preflight, passes the Renderer Capability Gate, and enters READY TO RENDER. It does not submit an image.

For initial Broad Phase 1:

> **USER CONCEPT / CREATE INPUT → INTERNAL COMPLETE DESIGN RESOLUTION → CONCEPT-INTEGRATED INTRINSIC COVERAGE → FIFTEEN FROZEN CHARACTER SPECIFICATIONS + THREE SHEET REVISION SETS → CLEAN RENDERER-FACING DESCRIPTION → RENDER PREFLIGHT → RENDERER CAPABILITY GATE → READY TO RENDER → USER `RENDER` → FINAL CONTEXT INTEGRITY RECHECK → CAPABILITY RECHECK → ONE IMAGE SUBMISSION → ACTUAL-IMAGE INSPECTION → OUTCOME RECORD**

For targeted `ALTER`, `REFINE`, and `FUSE`, rebuild only the targeted current revision and its genuine dependencies, then complete the same freeze → serialize → preflight → capability-gate → READY TO RENDER path. These operations never imply `RENDER`.

Written specifications remain an optional inspection surface. If the user asks to see the design, provide the requested frozen specification. Otherwise keep internal records internal. In either case, READY TO RENDER is a state record, not an image call.

**Global renderer-language boundary.** Across Phases 1–4, internal design terminology and renderer terminology are separate. Internal specifications may be exhaustive and analytical. The FINAL RENDER CONTEXT contains only directly renderable physical construction and required camera/layout/rendering facts. Internal proof vocabulary, workflow terminology, design-audit vocabulary, source-concept titles/lists, narrative language, nonvisual identifiers, and other non-renderable metadata are excluded. Phase-specific abstract goals are translated into concrete visible geometry before handoff.

**Renderer authority boundary.** The renderer performs pixel realization only. It does not choose unresolved anatomy, proportions, appendage count/origin, concept-fusion mechanism, topology, materials, colours, hair, face geometry, intrinsic-coverage geometry, or view layout. Every identity-bearing decision must already exist in the frozen specification and survive into the FINAL RENDER CONTEXT at the scale where it can affect the image.

**Renderer capability boundary.** A passing internal design is not sufficient to justify submission. If the active renderer/handoff cannot carry the complete render-critical specification as one operative self-contained input, or the status is unknown, mark HANDOFF UNSUPPORTED and STOP without an image call.

**Validation.** Before entering READY TO RENDER confirm: (1) final-refined design state exists; (2) every identity-bearing structure is resolved; (3) concept-integrated intrinsic coverage has been resolved before freeze and follows the character's form without becoming a separate silhouette authority; (4) required view and canvas geometry are explicit; (5) the Clean Renderer-Facing Description contains every render-critical visible decision without proof bookkeeping; (6) renderer preflight finds no unresolved design, contradictory serialization, or capacity loss; (7) the Renderer Capability Gate is SUPPORTED. Before executing an explicit `RENDER`, repeat items (5)–(7) and confirm the READY TO RENDER Revision-ID still matches the frozen current revision. Any failure blocks submission.
### 1.10 Scope of repeated rules, recognizable vocabularies, and conditional systems — HARD / ABSOLUTE

**Rule.** Every repetition has an owning phase, operation, and evaluation stage. The detailed Phase-1 body, concept, differentiation, identity, aesthetic, and presentation rules apply to Phase-1 work. Checkpoint-strength five-body independence applies separately to each of the three initial sheet groups, while global collision review applies across the complete fifteen-character CREATE population; an explicitly requested ALTER or FUSE instead uses the source-preservation and synthesis checks in Section 4.

**Complete recognizable concept vocabulary.** Concept decomposition must distinguish three classes:

1. **major recognizable characteristics** — the smallest set of visually important characteristics whose omission would materially weaken immediate recognition of the supplied concept in the current character-design domain;
2. **supporting characteristics** — useful secondary manifestations that reinforce recognition but are not individually mandatory in every character;
3. **optional possibilities** — valid design directions that may or may not be used.

When a concept possesses several major recognizable characteristics, **every initial character must preserve that complete major set**. Differentiation is never obtained by deleting one major characteristic from Character 02 because Character 01 already used it. Differentiation is obtained by radically changing the physical implementation of the retained characteristic: number, pairing, origin, attachment depth, size, proportion, length, width, thickness, curvature, branching, segmentation, articulation, jointure, orientation, spread, symmetry/asymmetry, material, visible texture, internal construction, surface organization, relation to neighboring anatomy, interaction with topology, interaction with the other concepts, and silhouette role.

A characteristic is not declared “major” merely because it is culturally associated with the word. The decomposition must justify why it is necessary to immediate visual identity. Conversely, once a characteristic has been classified as major for the active concept interpretation, it cannot later be removed merely to create population variety or visual economy.

**Major-set anti-downgrade rule — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Major/supporting/optional classification is an identity decision, never a diversity lever. A characteristic may be placed below the major tier only because the supplied concept genuinely remains immediately recognizable without it for the active interpretation—not because keeping that characteristic in all fifteen bodies would be inconvenient, repetitive, difficult to compose, or thought to narrow the search space. A required characteristic class can and must still diverge radically through count, origin, scale, construction, material, topology, articulation, silhouette role, and interaction with the other concepts. “Making it optional creates more variety” is therefore an invalid classification rationale.

When the user supplies an unqualified iconic morphology concept, begin from the strongest commonly recognizable composite morphology in the target character-design domain and preserve all characteristics classified as major. A narrower interpretation may change that classification only when the task wording or explicit user direction establishes it; population variety alone never authorizes omission.

**Conditional systems.** A list of body systems is a completeness checklist, not an instruction to invent every possible appendage or opening. Wings, horns, tails, apertures, cavities, unusual joints, particular materials, markings, makeup, and similar systems remain conditional **unless the active concept decomposition classifies them as part of the major recognizable vocabulary or the resolved fusion physically requires them**. When present, their applicable parameters are mandatory to resolve. When legitimately absent, record why the active concepts and fusion do not require them.

**Concept completeness versus saturation.** Whole-body authorship does not mean equal visible treatment over every body region. A quiet region is allowed when the complete concept vocabulary and all required interactions are already materially and unmistakably present. Quietness cannot be used to erase a mandatory major characteristic. Likewise, unused anatomical space is never a reason to add another manifestation once the design is complete and visually strong.

**Character identity.** Two sub-concepts, female readability, complete primary fusion, structural independence for initial candidates, character-identity completion, intelligently authored colour/material architecture, aesthetic/taste approval, white background, and 2D rendering remain mandatory. Conventional cosmetics are optional identity-finishing tools rather than a mandatory checklist of makeup features.

**Evaluation timing.** Design and blueprint checks use planned geometry and written evidence. Image audits use actual inspected pixels. Any instruction to revise or rebuild a character design applies before image submission or inside an explicit user-requested new development operation. A usable but nonconforming image receives a fidelity-failure record and does not authorize automatic regeneration. If a submission produces no usable image, preserve the frozen design, correct only the responsible non-design handoff layer when appropriate, return to READY TO RENDER after recertification, and require a new explicit user `RENDER` command for any further image call under §10.2.

**Concept visibility.** Abstract inputs are not excluded, and their physical mappings cannot remain vague. Use concrete design evidence and the authored visual interpretation; do not fabricate empirical recognizability or treat a label as a carrier. Identity-finishing suppression for structural comparison means ignoring makeup, markings, colour-only cues, and other finishing evidence while retaining constituent anatomy, facial geometry, hair roots and masses, materials, topology, and all structural systems. No test deletes the fusion to reveal an invented pre-concept body.

**Externalization.** Phase 1 prohibits independent symbolic objects, environmental carriers, emitted phenomena, and surrounding effects from carrying a supplied concept. The only spatially separated exception is the body-bound detached identity-structure rule in §0.0: a finite, stable, character-owned structure that is itself genuine major morphology or required by the fusion, remains fixed in body-local relation, and cannot be understood as an independent prop/effect. Concept-combination materialization still belongs to the character's body architecture; the exception never allows the complete fusion to migrate into surrounding imagery.

**Generalized example.** If a creature-derived concept has been decomposed and wings are only an optional possibility, a valid body may omit wings and still fulfil the concept through its complete major recognizable vocabulary. If wings have been identified as one of that concept's major recognizable characteristics, every character must retain wings, but their entire architecture must be independently authored. The same logic applies to horns, tails, scales, cavities, shells, fins, claws, visible internal structures, or any other major system.

**Validation.** Each gate states its scope and applicability. Before accepting an absent major system, verify that it was not classified as part of the active concept's major recognizable vocabulary and is not required by the fusion. A fixed user input that genuinely conflicts with a standing default must be resolved as an explicit narrow override or a focused question, never a fabricated pass. Different required properties cannot be silently traded against one another.
### 1.11 Deterministic execution and authored choices

**Rule.** Deterministic control means explicit phase routing, stable task IDs, recorded choices, consistent gates, and faithful serialization. It does not mean identical generated pixels or certainty that every render will comply.

**Explanation.** The assistant actively chooses original visual constructions. A roulette is only a selection method and must be recorded honestly. A reasoned choice is not called random. The renderer remains responsible only for realization of a fully resolved certified description/handoff.

**Application and validation.** The same frozen blueprint must produce the same specified anatomy and constraints in the Frozen Character Specification and Clean Renderer-Facing Description, but a stochastic rendering may still fail. Record that failure without claiming the gate proved unseen pixels. Track actual choices and submitted generation state; never certify unobserved pixels before inspection.

### 1.12 Automatic per-character Design Direction routing — HARD / ABSOLUTE

This runtime package includes `Design_Direction.md`. Every Phase-1 character receives an automatically selected Design Direction unless the user supplies an explicit direction for that character, subset, sheet, or population. A missing Design Direction registry is unresolved required input, not permission to skip direction assignment.

**Eligibility.** The automatic pool contains every registry entry marked `ACTIVE / COMPLETE` for the phases required by the active workflow.

**Selection timing.** Direction selection occurs after mandatory concept consequences and provisional body architecture are understood, and before final Phase-1 aesthetic/body freeze.

**Candidate-specific selection.** Route each character independently according to compatibility with the complete concept fusion, assigned body regime, face/head/hair opportunity, transformed-anatomy treatment, individual identity, and downstream outfit/weapon coherence. Population contrast may inform judgment but never becomes a quota.

**Population authorship.** Select the eligible COMPLETE direction that best supports each candidate's concept-valid body and individual identity. Explore different eligible orientations where useful, but impose no direction-use quota or distribution ratio. Repeating a direction is legal, including across an entire sheet or population; repeating character constructions or identities is not. A direction that fits less well cannot be forced onto a candidate merely to use every registry entry. Explicit user routing and per-character direction freezes remain binding.

**Concept-first aesthetic authorship.** Character creation always starts from supplied/resolved concepts. The causal order is **SUPPLIED CONCEPTS → CANONICAL IDENTITIES / ANCHORS → SYMBIOTIC PHYSICAL CONSTRAINTS → VALID BODY-SOLUTION SPACE → DESIGN-DIRECTION AESTHETIC AUTHORSHIP → PREMIUM FEMALE BODY + IDENTITY**. Direction authorship works inside that valid space: body-regime refinement, proportion/mass, face/eyes/hair, transformed anatomy, silhouette, hard/soft relationships, shape grouping, rhythm, asymmetry, material presentation, colour, focal hierarchy and desirability. It is neither a replacement concept nor an independent generator, theme source or post-hoc cosmetic filter. It cannot change frozen inputs, remove anchors, add an unsupported species, substitute franchise tokens for symbiosis or normalize unusual anatomy. A direction-only request supplies no concept set; request the missing concept specification before creation. Any random/no-theme language means no additional theme supplied, with concepts already present.

**Per-character freeze.** The selected direction freezes with that character through Phase 2 and Phase 3. Phase 4 preserves the frozen direction-derived decisions.

**Explicit user routing.** A user-specified Design Direction takes precedence for the scope named by the user. All unspecified characters continue through automatic routing.

Record the selected DD ID/name and automatic-or-user-directed status in internal state before Phase-1 freeze. This routing state is orchestration-only and never enters renderer-facing text unless a visible design consequence itself must be described.

## 2 REQUIREMENTS / CONSTRAINTS

### 2.1 Embedded quality calibration — HARD

The following **quality and construction benchmark** is mandatory for every candidate and is fully defined within this document.

The benchmark applies to:

- main-character-grade visual finish;
- coherent, deliberate anatomy;
- strong macro silhouette;
- integrated large-scale structures;
- deliberate material hierarchy;
- controlled high-frequency detail;
- crisp 2D line and shape construction;
- readable full-body presentation;
- specific, non-generic character identity.

Quality is achieved through independently resolved body construction, proportions, palette, appendage arrangement, and finish. A polished existing character does not become the template for another character. **Construction discipline and rendering finish** apply independently to every design.

A technically compliant population that is visibly below the embedded benchmark in character construction, 2D finish, macro readability, or material control is rejected.

### 2.2 Quality benchmark is population-wide — HARD

The quality floor applies independently to **all fifteen initial characters**. No sheet or later-numbered character may become simplified, generic, low-detail, or otherwise inferior because the population is larger. Deliberately broad, muscular, powerful, soft, heavy-framed, full-figured, compact, or extreme transformed regimes remain valid when intentionally authored and aesthetically successful.

Every one of the fifteen initial characters must independently reach the same quality class before population certification and before any initial sheet becomes render-eligible.

### 2.3 Quality is not an identity template — HARD

The renderer must realize each independently certified character. The certified population remains the sole identity-design authority.

The renderer-facing package must express the benchmark through concrete instructions for line, shape, anatomy, materials, hierarchy, and finish. An unspecified quality comparison does not resolve those instructions.

The final image must be unmistakably drawn 2D anime/manhwa artwork.

The 2D language is actively selected and enforced. It is not one candidate among several visually attractive rendering families.

### 2.3.1 Elite character-designer mandate — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The workflow must exercise design judgment, not merely conformance. Every phase is evaluated as the work of an exceptional gacha/anime/manhwa designer.

- **Phase 1:** the body, face, hair, materials, colour, concept structures, proportions, and silhouette must already create a premium character foundation.
- **Phase 2:** the outfit must elevate that exact character and become recognizable as belonging specifically to her rather than functioning as transferable clothing.
- **Phase 3:** the weapon must operate as a signature design that completes and amplifies the character rather than as generic inventory equipment.
- **Phase 4:** assembly must preserve and present the strongest qualities of the frozen body, outfit, and weapon as one coherent iconic character.

The designer must prefer the strongest aesthetically successful valid solution over the first technically valid solution. Main-character-grade means flagship-level identity, memorability, desirability, silhouette recognition, face/hair strength, material control, colour control, and the capacity to remain recognizable across splash art, portraits, small UI views, alternate outfits, merchandise, and other franchise representations. These are design-quality criteria, not narrative or commercial guarantees.

### 2.3.2 FINAL-REFINEMENT BARRIER — HARD / ABSOLUTE

No character, outfit, weapon, or final assembly becomes renderer-eligible while it is still merely structurally valid, exploratory, provisionally attractive, or awaiting polish. The first coherent solution is an internal candidate only. It must be refined until the complete design is deliberately resolved at premium main-character quality.

For Phase 1, the fifteen internal frozen character specifications are **render-ready final design blueprints**, not rough drafts, raw decompositions, placeholder bodies, or unpolished intermediate candidates. Internal exploration may compare and discard weaker alternatives, but the workflow presents a character as ready only after the complete fusion, proportions, silhouette, face/head/eyes/hair, material architecture, colour/value architecture, focal hierarchy, density rhythm, asymmetry where useful, and identity-defining detail have all been resolved together.

A render-ready blueprint contains no `TBD`, generic fallback, unresolved aesthetic choice, stock face, placeholder hair, default body, generic material treatment, or instruction for the renderer to improve the design. The renderer realizes a finished design; it is never asked to perform the final character-design pass.

Before the refinement passes, every Phase-1 character must undergo an **internal alternative-design search**. Explore at least three materially different macro-and-identity directions for that character using that character's same frozen complete Resolved Primary Concept Set and assigned sub-concepts. The alternatives must differ in whole-character construction rather than palette or decoration. Select the strongest direction by premium appeal, silhouette, face/hair potential, fusion elegance, material clarity, roster distinctness, and render robustness; discard the weaker directions internally. The user is shown only the selected, refined design unless they explicitly ask to inspect alternatives.

A character may not reach Frozen Character Specification certification merely because it is the first complete solution. The selected direction must then be deliberately improved rather than copied verbatim from the exploratory candidate.

Before blueprint presentation, every Phase-1 character must pass four refinement passes:

1. **Macro refinement:** body regime, proportions, dominant silhouette, concept-fusion architecture, appendage/topology placement where applicable, and first-read hook are coherent and immediately legible.
2. **Identity refinement:** face/head geometry, eyes, hair architecture, framing, and the character's specific appeal mode are memorable enough to carry the design independently of colour and micro-detail.
3. **Material/composition refinement:** material boundaries, colour/value hierarchy, focal emphasis, density rhythm, quiet regions, controlled contrast, asymmetry, and subordinate influences strengthen rather than clutter the character.
4. **Final polish and weakest-character comparison:** remove procedural residue, generic filler, redundant structures, awkward transitions, unresolved joins, weak silhouette decisions, weak face/hair decisions, and any visible quality drop relative to the strongest candidate in the group.

If any candidate is visibly less resolved, less attractive/compelling, less memorable, or more procedural than the others, it is rebuilt **before** the fifteen Frozen Character Specifications and three sheet revision sets are certified for handoff. Quality may not be averaged across the population.

This barrier applies again after any ALTER, REFINE, or FUSE operation and after any change caused by a sub-concept or dependency. A changed candidate must return to render-ready final refinement before its revised blueprint is presented as eligible for later rendering.

Passing this barrier is necessary but not sufficient for image submission. The §1.9.1 certified execution and render checkpoint additionally requires concept-integrated intrinsic coverage resolved before freeze, frozen design state, clean renderer serialization, renderer preflight, and a SUPPORTED Renderer Capability Gate result. Completion enters READY TO RENDER only; image submission still requires an explicit user `RENDER` command.
### 2.3.3 IMAGE-GENERATION ROBUSTNESS GATE — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Premium design quality includes **render robustness**. A design is not render-ready merely because it is sophisticated on paper; its defining identity must survive the actual five-character analytical sheet scale, especially in the five principal true-front views, without depending on tiny text, microscopic differences, or fragile implied geometry; the mandatory profile/rear views support reconstruction but do not rescue weak front-view identity.

Before Frozen Character Specification certification, verify that each character's identity is carried primarily by clear macro and strong meso decisions: silhouette, body regime, face/head/hair, dominant fusion structures, major appendage/topology architecture where applicable, material/value separation, and controlled colour placement. Micro-detail enriches these systems but does not carry the identity by itself.

The compiler must actively improve generation robustness by:

- consolidating redundant structures that compete for the same visual read;
- enlarging or clarifying identity-critical structures that would otherwise disappear at sheet scale;
- resolving attachment points, overlaps, silhouette tangencies, and material boundaries before rendering;
- ensuring each dominant front view remains large enough to carry character identity, primary-concept recognition, fusion architecture, body differentiation, and material discrimination by itself;
- keeping the face and hair sufficiently distinct to survive the five-character sheet layout;
- describing complex topology through a few clear geometric relationships rather than metaphor or analytical jargon;
- resolving stable three-dimensional anchors internally for the features most likely to drift: body regime, face/hair, appendage count/origin, major apertures/topology, dominant material masses, and colour/value architecture;
- removing prompt repetition that competes with the actual visual priorities.

This gate does not authorize generic simplification. Complete concept vocabulary, required fusion, unusual anatomy, and premium individuality remain mandatory. The objective is a **stronger and more legible version of the same complete design**, never a generic substitute.

For the later Clean Renderer-Facing Description, use the **shortest lossless visual formulation** that preserves these render-critical decisions. Use one compact coherent block per character, stable terminology across all views, concrete geometry instead of metaphor, and a clear first-read → supporting-structure → material/colour hierarchy. Do not repeat internal proof language, alternative candidates, rejection logic, or the same anatomy fact under several synonyms. Prompt density is a rendering-quality constraint: excess wording that competes with the actual design must be compiled away before the image call.

A candidate fails this gate when its identity depends mainly on micro-detail, when major structures collide or become ambiguous at expected sheet scale, when too many equal-priority features compete for attention, when the face becomes visually secondary without deliberate reason, or when the clean renderer description cannot state the design hierarchy clearly. Correct these problems before Frozen Character Specification certification.

### 2.4 Required 2D rendering architecture

The renderer must produce:

- clean drawn lineart;
- deliberate contour hierarchy;
- graphic 2D shape language;
- controlled illustrated anatomy;
- coherent colour fields;
- controlled illustrated shadow grouping, including cel-shaded or soft/painterly transitions within the resolved 2D treatment;
- deliberate illustrated gradients consistent with the resolved drawing treatment;
- drawn highlights;
- drawn reflections;
- stylized transparency and translucency;
- crisp illustrated edges;
- deliberate material boundaries;
- clear anatomical planes;
- high-resolution professional line and shape control;
- consistent rendering quality across all five characters on each initial sheet, with equal quality across all fifteen designs.

### 2.5 2D-first serialization rule

The renderer-facing package must describe the positive 2D construction before any validator-level drift controls.

The image must look drawn, designed, and illustrated rather than physically simulated.

### 2.6 Material rendering remains illustrated

All concept-derived and ordinary character materials—such as skin, scales, crystal, feathers, bone, keratin, metal, glass, membrane, liquid-like matter, fibrous matter, mineral structures, appendages, hair, eyes, and surface systems—must be rendered through 2D shape design, linework, material-specific shadow behavior, material-specific highlight behavior, value grouping, controlled gradients, edge treatment, granularity, internal texture organization, and illustrated reflections where applicable.

Material complexity must never force a change of rendering family.

### 2.7 Quality floor

The 2D finish must remain main-character grade for every character.

The fifth character is not permitted to become a simplified rendering because of sheet density.

The rendering must preserve:

- facial clarity;
- body clarity;
- silhouette clarity;
- appendage clarity;
- aperture clarity;
- material separation;
- line control;
- value hierarchy;
- colour hierarchy;
- micro-detail quality.

### 2.8 Defined 2D style is authoritative

The embedded 2D rendering grammar in this section is mandatory, without any accompanying style document.

The workflow must not compare:

> 2D style versus 3D style

as aesthetic alternatives.

There is no such choice once the 2D style is defined.

### 2.9 2D style must be visible in structural decisions

The style is not only a post-process.

It must influence:

- line contours;
- face shapes;
- eye construction;
- hair grouping;
- shape simplification;
- shadow grouping;
- highlight design;
- colour blocks;
- material depiction;
- surface texture;
- detail density.

The output should appear to have been designed in the defined 2D visual language from the beginning.

The embedded 2D grammar is always active. It defines how the resolved character must be drawn. The compiler must explicitly resolve its applicable visual parameters:

- line quality;
- line weight;
- contour control;
- face construction;
- eye construction;
- hair rendering;
- anatomical simplification;
- body rendering;
- shadow grouping;
- highlight design;
- material illustration;
- colour behaviour;
- texture frequency;
- painterly or cel-shaded behaviour;
- softness;
- edge control;
- contrast;
- saturation;
- visual noise;
- background simplicity.

### 2.10 2D style is selected, not negotiated

If a defined 2D style is available, the renderer must use that defined style.

The renderer must not choose a more physically simulated, volumetric, realistic, or three-dimensional treatment merely because it can represent complex anatomy more easily. Broad soft modeling, plastic or metallic CGI-like sheen, excessive continuous gradient volume, and other simulation-heavy surface treatment are drift indicators rather than acceptable shortcuts.

Complexity must be solved inside the embedded 2D language.
### 2.11 Material complexity remains illustrated

Feathers, scales, fire-derived structures, apertures, unusual substrates, reflective surfaces, translucent regions, layered materials, liquid-like matter, bone, keratin, glass, membrane, skin, and complex concept-derived anatomy must all be drawn using the active 2D rendering grammar. Different materials must remain distinguishable through their illustrated response—shadow softness/hardness, highlight shape and intensity, edge sharpness/softness, granularity, roughness/smoothness cues, opacity/translucency, internal pattern, fracture or fiber logic, layering, porosity, flow, thickness, and local contrast—without switching to PBR or 3D simulation.

Complexity is not a license to change rendering families.

### 2.12 Body construction and drawing grammar have different ownership

The body-construction rules establish **concept-materialization methodology**.

The embedded 2D grammar establishes **rendering methodology**.

Neither method permits copying a previously designed identity or importing an unrequested concept.

The renderer-facing package must use neutral, concrete, visual construction language.

The workflow must never sacrifice a resolved concept consequence merely because a semantic label may be interpreted incorrectly.

The correct transformation is:

> **SEMANTIC LABEL → NEUTRAL PHYSICAL DESCRIPTION → STRUCTURAL VALIDATION → POSITIVE SERIALIZATION**.

The wrong transformation is:

> **SEMANTIC LABEL → WORDING CONCERN → DELETE STRUCTURE → WEAKEN CHARACTER**.

The Clean Renderer-Facing Description should contain the positive visual construction that actually matters.

Internal audit language remains separate.

### 2.13 Do not overload renderer-facing text with prohibitions

Long chains of negative terms are not the primary control mechanism.

The primary control mechanism is a precise positive construction description.

The renderer should receive:

- exact format;
- exact view geometry;
- neutral standing state;
- female anime/manhwa identity;
- resolved body architecture;
- resolved concept fusion;
- resolved appendages;
- resolved internal topology;
- resolved face;
- resolved character identity and any selected cosmetic finishing;
- resolved material system;
- resolved colour system;
- embedded 2D rendering language.

Internal validators perform the rejection logic.

### 2.14 RENDER-SCALE LOSSLESS COMPILATION — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The complete certified blueprint remains the authoritative design specification. The Clean Renderer-Facing Description is **not** a verbatim dump of that blueprint and is **not** required to repeat every internal proof, rationale, validator, parameter ledger, or hidden construction detail.

Renderer compilation must preserve every decision that is necessary to reproduce the character's visible identity at the intended sheet scale: dominant silhouette, body regime and proportions, face/head/eyes/hair identity, major concept-fusion structures, major topology and appendage architecture where applicable, material boundaries, colour/value architecture, defining asymmetry, subordinate visual consequences, view geometry, presentation, and active 2D drawing treatment.

The compiler may compress repeated facts, combine mutually supporting clauses, replace analytical bookkeeping with the resulting visible construction, and omit internal causal proof that has already been resolved. Pairwise/higher-order fusion **proof** remains in the blueprint; the renderer receives the concrete fused structures produced by that proof. Details that are genuinely invisible at the intended sheet scale remain preserved in the blueprint for future close views rather than being allowed to dilute the immediate generation instructions.

Renderer-facing priority is:

1. exact canvas/count/view/presentation and active 2D medium;
2. each character's dominant silhouette, body regime, face/head/hair identity, and strongest visual hook;
3. the major complete concept-fusion architecture and its unmistakable visible carriers;
4. topology/appendage architecture that materially changes silhouette or anatomy;
5. material boundaries and colour/value hierarchy;
6. subordinate influences that visibly matter at sheet scale;
7. selected identity-defining micro-detail;
8. cross-view consistency.

No quality-critical visible decision may be dropped merely to shorten text. Conversely, no internal bookkeeping may be repeated merely because it exists in the blueprint. The clean description must be dense, concrete, visually ordered, and easy for an image generator to follow.

If the description becomes so long or repetitive that the primary visual hierarchy is diluted, recompile it more efficiently while preserving the visible design. If an essential visible requirement still cannot be expressed clearly and unambiguously in the final renderer context, block the handoff rather than silently omitting it.

### 2.14A NO CONDENSATION OF THE CERTIFIED DESIGN — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The complete certified blueprint remains the authoritative design specification. The need for a concise Clean Renderer-Facing Description or sheet-local FINAL RENDER CONTEXT never authorizes shortening the certified design itself.

Renderer compilation may reorganize information, remove redundant prose, and replace internal labels with concrete physical descriptors. It may not remove certified structural content or any render-critical visible consequence. Every material body decision, concept-fusion decision, differentiation-critical identity decision, format decision, active drawing-grammar decision, and quality-critical visible decision remains preserved either in the complete frozen specification or, where it affects the intended image scale, in shortest-lossless renderer serialization.

Conciseness is a serialization property, not a design-reduction mechanism. If capacity cannot carry the render-critical result without loss, the Renderer Capability Gate returns HANDOFF UNSUPPORTED rather than weakening the character.

### 2.15 NO LINE-COUNT CEILING — HARD / ABSOLUTE

There is no maximum document length.

Document length is subordinate to precision.

If an additional rule, example, test, table, gate, serialization schema, audit stage, or explicit requirement reduces ambiguity and improves deterministic execution, it MUST be retained.

Do not remove a useful rule merely because it makes the document longer.

Do not merge distinct rules merely to reduce line count.

Do not replace explicit operational examples with vague summaries.

Do not compress separate failure modes into broad statements when the separation improves execution reliability.

The document is allowed to become extremely long.

Precision takes priority over brevity.

Completeness takes priority over compactness.

Deterministic execution takes priority over elegance of document size.

### 2.16 UNIVERSALIZATION REQUIREMENT — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Every rule in this section applies across concept categories.

A new concept must not require a new fundamental exception to:

- anatomical body materialization;
- concept fusion;
- useful differentiation;
- independent character construction;
- extreme body range;
- appendage divergence;
- cavity/topology divergence;
- material divergence;
- colour divergence;
- face quality;
- character-identity completion;
- 2D rendering;
- macro/micro hierarchy;
- female-character identity;
- exact format;
- neutral standing.

When a new concept category appears, the compiler must map it through the universal concept-to-body translation layer rather than creating an external exception.

The implementation changes.

The governing principle does not.

### 2.17 Embedded drawing grammar is always active

This section supplies the complete rendering source for every phase.

The renderer must actively use the defined visual language.

The style is not selected by subjective attractiveness.

The style is not negotiated against a more realistic rendering method.

The style defines:

- line quality;
- line weight;
- contour behaviour;
- facial rendering;
- eye rendering;
- hair rendering;
- body rendering;
- shadow organization;
- highlight organization;
- material illustration;
- colour behaviour;
- texture frequency;
- edge control;
- softness;
- contrast;
- saturation;
- visual noise;
- background treatment.

### 2.18 Drawing-treatment resolution

The compiler must resolve one coherent drawing treatment from the embedded 2D grammar before rendering. Shared invariants apply throughout each sheet: illustrated contours, intentional shadow and highlight shapes, stylized readable forms, controlled material separation, and a plain white field.

The permitted envelope includes clean cel/soft shading, polished drawn gloss, delicate painterly transitions, and controlled visible brushwork. Choose their density, edge behavior, and contrast coherently; do not indiscriminately combine incompatible extremes.

Drawing method must not import a stock body, costume, pose, expression, scene, palette, or identity. Structural features stay intact when translated into the drawing language.

### 2.19 2D-native execution

The required output is drawn 2D anime/manhwa artwork.

The renderer-facing package must positively encode:

- drawn lineart;
- deliberate contour hierarchy;
- graphic shape construction;
- illustrated anatomical planes;
- controlled 2D shading groups;
- drawn gradients where supported by the style;
- designed highlight shapes;
- deliberate reflective accents;
- stylized material representation;
- line-led contour authority and shape-first form reading;
- clearly grouped illustrated shadows rather than generalized airbrushed volumetric modeling;
- crisp illustrated edges;
- controlled value masses;
- coherent anime/manhwa facial construction;
- deliberate illustrated surface detail;
- high-resolution clean finish.
### 2.20 Material complexity remains 2D

Complexity is solved within the active 2D language.

Feathers, scales, fire-derived anatomy, cavities, unusual substrates, reflective surfaces, translucent regions, layered structures, and intricate appendages remain illustrated through the selected 2D grammar.

The renderer must not change rendering families merely because the body is complex.

### 2.21 2D quality applies equally to all fifteen initial characters

Characters 01–15 must share the same professional rendering class; no later sheet receives a lower quality tier.

The final characters must not become progressively rougher because of sheet crowding.

They must not become less detailed.

They must not become more volumetric.

They must not become less facially developed.

They must not lose their concept structures.

They must not lose their resolved character-identity finishing.

### 2.22 Rendering is not judged by prettiness alone

A rendering passes only when:

- the correct 2D language is visible;
- the linework reflects the embedded 2D grammar;
- the anatomy reflects the embedded 2D grammar;
- the material treatment reflects the embedded 2D grammar;
- the face reflects the embedded 2D grammar;
- the shading reflects the embedded 2D grammar;
- the output remains readable;
- the five characters remain individually resolved.

Attractive but stylistically incorrect rendering fails.

Highly detailed but three-dimensional rendering fails.

### 2.23 Negative vocabulary stays outside primary serialization

Internal validation may classify failure conditions.

The primary generation package should not reproduce long chains of internal rejection terminology or unrelated negative vocabulary.

The renderer should be given the desired visual state.

This reduces lexical interference while preserving the actual specification.

### 2.24 Structural equivalence is mandatory

Any renderer-facing wording adjustment must preserve the certified design exactly.

The sequence is:

> **IDENTIFY AMBIGUOUS WORDING → SUBSTITUTE NEUTRAL CONCRETE VISUAL LANGUAGE → VERIFY STRUCTURAL EQUIVALENCE → SERIALIZE THE SAME DESIGN**

Never:
