> **IDENTIFY AMBIGUOUS WORDING → REMOVE THE DESIGN CONSEQUENCE**

### 2.25 Concrete visual language

The renderer package must describe what is visibly constructed.

Examples:

- body topology;
- integrated appendage roots;
- layered material transitions;
- structural cavities;
- specific hair architecture;
- specific eye geometry;
- defined character-identity finishing, including any selected cosmetic details;
- defined palette architecture;
- defined silhouette;
- defined line and shading language.

Abstract labels alone are insufficient.

### 2.26 Concept labels remain metadata

Supplied concept names may remain in internal metadata.

They must not be the sole renderer instruction.

The physical consequences must be serialized explicitly.

### 2.27 Submission and wording integrity

A non-executed or unavailable image attempt is recorded accurately without inventing a hidden cause. Preserve the frozen design state and the exact submitted package.

Before READY TO RENDER, resolve renderer-facing wording through faithful concrete visual descriptions and revalidate equivalence to the frozen design. If a submission does not execute or clearly fails at the serialization layer, §10.2 permits correction of the renderer-facing wording while preserving the frozen character architecture and intrinsic coverage exactly; any subsequent image call still requires a new explicit user `RENDER` command.
### 2.28 EMBEDDED 2D GRAMMAR ACTIVATION — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The 2D grammar written in this document is the active rendering specification for all phases.

It is not a suggestion.

It is not one option among multiple possible rendering approaches.

The renderer MUST actively use the defined 2D style language.

The workflow MUST explicitly encode the applicable drawing properties defined here:

- line quality;
- line weight;
- contour behaviour;
- shape language;
- face grammar;
- eye grammar;
- hair grammar;
- body grammar;
- hand grammar;
- foot grammar;
- colour behaviour;
- shadow behaviour;
- highlight behaviour;
- material illustration;
- texture frequency;
- rendering density;
- softness;
- edge control;
- contrast;
- saturation;
- stylistic brush behaviour;
- visual-noise level;
- background simplification;
- composition tendencies.

The renderer must not choose a different rendering method because it appears more visually impressive.

The embedded 2D grammar outranks generic rendering preference.

### 2.29 2D MEANS DRAWN CONSTRUCTION, NOT 3D MATERIAL SIMULATION — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The target rendering must look intentionally drawn.

The material information must be communicated through:

- drawn shape boundaries;
- drawn highlights;
- drawn shadows;
- controlled gradients;
- deliberate linework;
- stylized transparency;
- stylized reflectivity;
- simplified anatomical planes;
- graphic value grouping;
- controlled painterly or cel-shaded transitions where present in the embedded 2D grammar.

The renderer MUST NOT reconstruct the body as a 3D object and then apply a token anime filter.

A technically attractive 3D-looking render is still a failure.

PBR-like highlights, physically simulated reflections, volumetric shading, realistic optical scattering, or physically rendered material response do not satisfy the defined 2D style requirement.

The body must look authored as a 2D illustration.

### 2.30 EMBEDDED 2D PARAMETERS ARE ACTIVE SOURCE MATERIAL — HARD

The compiler must use the following embedded parameter families to resolve a consistent 2D treatment for the current sheet.

Rendering resolution must remain focused on visual drawing properties.

The compiler must not import a stock character through its choice of drawing treatment.

The compiler must preserve independently resolved character identities.

The compiler must not substitute a generic anime rendering style.

The compiler must explicitly establish:

- line treatment;
- contour treatment;
- face and eye construction language;
- hair rendering language;
- anatomical simplification level;
- colour-field organisation;
- shadow structure;
- highlight structure;
- material illustration;
- detail hierarchy;
- edge behaviour;
- background behaviour.

The embedded drawing grammar determines how the resolved character is drawn.

It does not determine who the character is.

### 2.31 MAIN-CHARACTER QUALITY IS PER-CHARACTER — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Every one of the fifteen generated characters must independently be sufficiently resolved to serve as the main character of a high-quality anime/manhwa work.

This requirement applies to every character equally.

It includes:

- face quality;
- eye quality;
- hair quality;
- body quality;
- body proportions;
- silhouette;
- concept integration;
- appendages;
- materials;
- colour;
- asymmetry;
- character-identity finishing, including optional cosmetic detail where selected;
- linework;
- rendering finish;
- readability;
- aesthetic coherence.

The fifth character must not receive less design attention than the first.

The smallest character must not receive less design attention than the tallest.

The most structurally unusual character must not receive less polish than the simplest.

The most complex concept-fusion solution must not receive degraded face quality.

Each column must reach the same professional quality floor.

Every Broad Phase-1 request defines **one complete fifteen-character population presented as three five-character analytical sheets**. All fifteen characters are independently authored, final-refined, frozen internally, and certified; each five-character sheet group is then serialized and preflighted independently, and rendered only after the entire population passes certification. Written specifications remain internal unless the user requests inspection.

Characters 01–15 form the complete initial Broad Phase-1 population; Characters 01–05, 06–10, and 11–15 are the three sheet groups. Every identity has one certified dominant true-front neutral-standing full-body design plus one mandatory exact 90-degree profile and one mandatory direct-rear full-body design of the same frozen identity. Compose subordinate views compactly without reducing the five principal fronts below useful analytical scale. If a submitted call produces no usable image, same-design serializer correction may return the unchanged frozen revision to READY TO RENDER under §10.2, but no further image call occurs without a new explicit user `RENDER` command.
### 2.32 Complete embedded 2D drawing grammar — HARD / ABSOLUTE

**Rule.** All phases use intentionally illustrated 2D anime/manhwa or controlled painterly anime drawing. The default is clean, precise contour construction with controlled soft/cel shadow groups and drawn highlights. Painterly and polished glossy variants remain inside the same illustrated family. Every sheet uses one coherent resolved drawing treatment.

**Explanation.** “2D” describes the visible construction of the image, not merely its file format or an anime face placed on a simulated body. A raster image can still look like CGI. Conversely, drawn gradients, glossy hair, reflected accents, and painterly soft edges do not by themselves make an illustration 3D. Evaluate how contours, values, highlights, textures, and materials work together.

#### 2.32.1 Line and edge construction

**Rule.** Use deliberate thin-to-medium illustrated contours, with selective weight variation to separate silhouette, overlaps, and interior anatomy. Large forms remain graphically readable. Interior lines are finer or less numerous than dominant boundaries.

**Explanation.** Soft or light-facing edges may lose part of a contour without losing construction. Painterly breaks may be used deliberately; random scratch noise cannot become anatomical structure. Dark outlines are not a mandatory uniform border around every material.

**Application.** A translucent forearm may use a thin tinted exterior contour, a stronger joint overlap, and sparse internal structural lines. Its cavity rim must remain readable even if some illuminated edge transitions are soft.

**Failure and validation.** Reject melted boundaries, uniformly fuzzy anatomy, unexplained edge thickening, or noisy texture that prevents reconstruction. Check silhouette, overlaps, joint boundaries, aperture rims, garment layers, and weapon edges independently.

#### 2.32.2 Shape, anatomy, and morphology ownership

**Rule.** Use illustrated shape simplification without changing the certified body. No drawing preference authorizes a common waist, chest, hip/lower-torso region, musculature, face, or limb template across candidates.

**Explanation.** The drawing grammar controls how a defined volume is depicted; the body blueprint controls what volume exists. Reduced surface shading is compatible with unusual internal topology only if every required boundary and connection remains legible.

**Application.** A muscular candidate retains its authored shoulder mass and limb construction even when muscles are grouped into a few broad drawn planes. A soft-bodied candidate retains its different mass distribution. The same rendering method does not normalize them into one body.

**Failure and validation.** Reject a style translation that removes a cavity, rounds away a structural ridge, reduces a selected chest or hip form, or standardizes distinct faces. Compare geometry before and after serialization.

#### 2.32.3 Face, eyes, hair, hands, and feet

**Rule.** Resolve crisp anime/manhwa facial geometry, deliberately drawn lashes and brows, clear iris/pupil construction, and controlled eye highlights. Hair uses organized masses and strand groups with subordinate individual strands. Hands and feet remain complete, anatomically coherent with the chosen unusual body, and legible in the required views.

**Explanation.** A small indicated nose, simplified mouth, almond or other authored eye shape, soft facial planes, ribbon-like hair grouping, and selective small highlights are available drawing methods, not compulsory identical features. Nail finish and digit detail belong to the blueprint where visible. Long or short hair, unusual eyes, nonstandard digits, and ethereal hair remain eligible.

**Application.** One character may have angular narrow eyes with separate iris colours and fine lashes; another may have rounded eyes with concentric intrinsic iris structures. Both use the same controlled line and highlight method without becoming the same face.

**Failure and validation.** Reject interchangeable faces, unreadable eyes in later columns, generic hands, missing feet, or hair rendered as unstructured photoreal strands. Face detail must be visible within the main view and cannot demand unauthorized portrait inserts.

#### 2.32.4 Shadow and highlight construction

**Rule.** Define large intentional shadow groups and subordinate transitions. Permit clean cel boundaries, soft gradients, painterly blending, and selected hard accents according to the resolved treatment. Highlights are drawn shapes that explain material and hierarchy.

**Explanation.** Polished illustrated skin may use broad soft value transitions and small bright accents. Hair may use ribbon highlights. Metal may use crisp angular reflection shapes. Controlled local gloss is compatible with 2D. Reproducing a simulated environment in every reflective surface, volumetric skin scattering, noisy ray-traced reflections, or a sculpted plastic appearance is not.

Different materials must not receive one universal highlight/shadow treatment. Within the same 2D style, the renderer deliberately changes shadow edges, highlight geometry, line emphasis, granularity, edge breakup, opacity, internal texture, reflected accent structure, and transition behavior so that skin, scale, crystal, feather, bone, keratin, metal, membrane, glass, liquid-like matter, and other resolved substances remain visually distinct even when their colours are similar.

**Application.** A dark metal blade can use one broad cool face, a sharply drawn pale bevel, restrained reflected bands, and a clear inset border. It does not need photoreal roughness, bloom, or volumetric reflections to communicate metal.

**Failure and validation.** Reject CGI-like modeled material response, photographic optical effects, semi-3D or mannequin-like body drawing, plastic-looking bodies, and highlights that erase structural edges. Do not reject a correctly drawn image solely because it has gloss or gradients.

#### 2.32.5 Palette and colour behavior

**Rule.** Colour treatment must support the independently resolved palettes. Warm or cool shadows, saturated accents, pale fields, deep neutrals, and high or restrained contrast are available. No fixed hue family applies to every character.

**Explanation.** Harmony concerns the relationships within a design, not making all candidates blue, gold, white, or purple. Global consistency of drawing can coexist with radically different colour counts and placements. Warm skin is not mandatory for a body made from another material.

**Application.** One candidate can be nearly monochrome with carefully separated values; another can use four coordinated hues; another can use ordered spectral transitions. Their shadow methods remain coherent while their colour architecture stays distinct.

**Failure and validation.** Reject palette convergence where not imposed by explicit input, highlight bleaching that destroys material colours, or importing a style's sample palette as a population-wide restriction.

#### 2.32.6 Illustrated material library

**Rule.** Resolve each material through contour, thickness, internal construction, value grouping, highlight shape, and controlled surface information.

| Material family | Required drawn evidence | Failure to prevent |
|---|---|---|
| Skin or soft substrate | Coherent planes, defined anatomy, controlled value transitions | Plastic modeling or erased body differences |
| Hair or fibrous matter | Grouped direction, roots, coherent masses, local strand separation | Random strand noise or detached atmospheric wisps |
| Cloth and leather | Edges, folds, thickness, drape/tension, closures | Texture pretending to be garment construction |
| Metal and rigid composite | Clear faces, bevels, joins, drawn reflected accents | PBR simulation replacing specified geometry |
| Crystal, glass-like or translucent matter | Drawn edges, layered transparency, visible thickness and internal boundaries | Background effects pretending to be material |
| Bone-like, stone-like or ceramic matter | Actual structural volumes, clear joints, chosen grain or pore detail | A coating over a generic body or weapon |
| Feather, scale, plate or petal systems | Organized units grouped into larger coherent structures | Undirected detail soup |
| Liquid-like intrinsic matter | Stable designed volumes, membranes or boundaries, coherent anatomical continuity | Drips, puddles or splashes detached from the subject |
| Fire-like, luminous or ethereal intrinsic matter | Defined body/garment/weapon volume, illustrated internal gradients and edges | A surrounding aura or emitted effect |

**Application.** A flame-constituted hair system has resolved roots, grouped flowing masses, termination shapes, colour layers, and consistent side/rear geometry. It does not shed separate flames into the white field. An energy-like garment still has wearable edges, attachment, layering, and coverage.

**Validation.** Every material must remain readable without a label and must preserve the owning system: body in Phase 1, clothing in Phase 2, weapon in Phase 3, all three separately in Phase 4.

#### 2.32.7 Permitted drawing envelope and positive serialization

**Rule.** Resolve line weight, edge softness, shadow-group count and complexity, gradient strength, highlight size, brush visibility, texture frequency, local contrast, saturation behavior, and detail density before the call.

**Explanation.** The available envelope extends from sparse clean anime drawing through glossy manhwa polish to deliberate painterly anime. It does not impose every endpoint simultaneously. Painterly texture stays controlled; clean drawing does not mean removing required micro-construction. Strong directional painted lighting is permitted only when it preserves analytical visibility and the white background.

**Worked renderer-description application.** “Draw the certified designs with precise thin contours, strengthened overlap lines, broad controlled shadow shapes, soft drawn transitions on rounded planes, crisp angular highlights on rigid materials, grouped hair masses with ribbon highlights, sharp aperture rims, and subordinate etched detail; maintain uniform illustrated finish across all candidates on plain white.” Resolve the actual design-specific values and geometry in the surrounding package; this sentence is not a substitute for the character blocks.

**Failure and validation.** A bare “anime, 2D, high quality” token set fails serialization completeness. Check the actual Clean Renderer-Facing Description for the resolved drawing properties. Do not import composition, body proportions, expressions, costumes, backgrounds, or semantic adjectives from drawing examples.

## 3 TASK ATTRIBUTION / BOUNDARIES

### 3.1 Layer ownership is explicit — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Each layer has one defined job. A layer may consume the certified output of a previous layer, but it may not silently create, reinterpret, repair, or replace a decision owned by another layer.

#### 3.1.1 Layer 1 — supplied input

Owns the literal concepts, explicit commands, task-specific constraints, and selected existing candidate identities provided for the current operation. This document supplies all standing methodology, quality, presentation, and rendering rules.

It does not author anatomy, concept meaning, fusion, differentiation, sub-concepts, phase progression, or presentation beyond what the user explicitly supplied.

#### 3.1.2 Layer 2 — zero-inference interpretation

Owns literal extraction of supplied visual/concept information and separation of explicit requirements from unsupported assumptions.

It does not invent trope associations, lore, species, environments, props, narrative roles, psychological stories, or culturally associated objects.

#### 3.1.3 Layer 3 — universal physical-property decomposition

Owns conversion of every supplied concept into concrete visual properties capable of becoming physical character construction.

It does not select the final fifteen character bodies.

#### 3.1.4 Layer 4 — complete concept fusion

Owns the combined physical interaction of all supplied concepts before individual body construction.

It must determine how the concepts can jointly alter body geometry, proportions, body substrate, topology, material architecture, appendage architecture, apertures, silhouette, asymmetry, and other applicable body systems.

It does not delegate unresolved fusion to the renderer.

#### 3.1.5 Layer 5 — independent character authorship

Owns fifteen distinct, fully designed body solutions, partitioned into three sheet groups of five, to their frozen candidate-specific complete concept-fusion problems.

It must actively author each character as a coherent aesthetic whole rather than mutating isolated parameters.

#### 3.1.6 Layer 6 — sub-concept resolution

Owns exactly two subordinate visual sub-concepts per character and their concrete physical consequences.

Sub-concept 1 is one assistant-selected **actual real-world Era / Culture / Civilization source**. Mythology may inform that source only where historically/culturally embedded; mythology labels, genre labels, invented worlds, art-direction labels, design movements, and aesthetic adjective bundles are ineligible as the final Sub-concept-1 identity. Sub-concept 2 is one genuinely randomly selected additional concept drawn from a freshly constructed broad/open-ended temporary eligible reservoir. Both enrich the already-resolved primary fusion from a subordinate position. Neither replaces it or becomes a co-equal primary concept.

#### 3.1.7 Layer 7 — phase progression

Owns when body, clothing, weapon, assembly, and later presentation systems become eligible.

It does not redesign the body merely because a later phase is easier to render.

#### 3.1.8 Layer 8 — presentation composition

Owns the analytical sheet geometry: three targetable 16:9 canvases, each with five character regions and exactly three full-body views per identity (dominant true front, exact profile, direct rear) on a plain white field.

It does not invent anatomy or concept embodiment.

#### 3.1.9 Layer 9 — art-style execution

Owns the rendering grammar used to draw the already-resolved characters.

The embedded 2D grammar is active rendering methodology. It controls line, shape, face/eye/hair grammar, colour-field behavior, shadow/highlight behavior, material illustration, edge treatment, texture frequency, and related rendering properties.

It does not redesign identity, body architecture, concept fusion, differentiation, or sub-concepts.

#### 3.1.10 Layer 10 — description materialization and renderer serialization

Owns the mandatory intermediate conversion of the certified internal Generation Package into two distinct artifacts:

1. an **internal Frozen Character Specification**, describing the complete certified characters in exhaustive concrete visual language and retained in task state whether or not the user asks to see it; and
2. a **Clean Renderer-Facing Description** derived from that frozen specification, containing only the render-critical visual design, format, camera, 2D rendering, and sheet-presentation information needed for realization.

The Frozen Character Specification is shown to the user only when explicitly requested for inspection, comparison, editing, or archival use. This layer may reorganize wording for renderer clarity and phase-local precision, but it may not change the certified design. The clean renderer description is a scale-aware visual compilation, not permission to omit identity-bearing or concept-critical visible decisions.

**Active-phase context firewall.** The immediate Frozen Character Specification and Clean Renderer-Facing Description are phase-local. They contain the active phase's resolved visual design plus only the frozen upstream dependencies required to preserve it. Inactive-phase methodology, examples, preference vocabulary, failure atlases, future-phase options, and unrelated task history are not copied, summarized, quoted, or restated into the immediate renderer-facing handoff. Phase 1 therefore materializes Phase-1 character construction only; Phase 2 adds the frozen character plus the resolved outfit; Phase 3 adds only the frozen dependencies required for the weapon task; Phase 4 contains the selected frozen assembly. This firewall controls handoff composition without deleting any rule from the governing document.

#### 3.1.11 Layer 11 — renderer

Owns only visual realization of the certified package using the supplied rendering language.

The renderer is not permitted to invent anatomy, concept meaning, concept fusion, differentiation, sub-concepts, phase progression, alteration targeting, omitted architecture, or presentation modules.

#### 3.1.12 Layer 12 — post-render audit

Owns comparison of the actual image against the certified package and identification of failed requirements.

The audit does not silently redesign the character while declaring the existing render successful.

#### 3.1.13 Layer 13 — pre-generation correction and requested revision

Owns targeted pre-generation correction of unresolved or failing design and serialization decisions. Identify the failed layer, preserve unrelated passing decisions, invalidate affected certificates, revise, and recheck until every applicable gate passes.

After a usable image exists, this layer performs no automatic correction, editing, or regeneration. If a submission produces no usable image, the unchanged frozen design may undergo same-design serializer correction and recertification under §10.2, then returns to READY TO RENDER; another image call requires a new explicit user `RENDER` command. Explicit user-requested ALTER, REFINE, or FUSE opens a new candidate revision under the current phase.
#### 3.1.14 Layer 14 — population state

Owns separately the resolved-candidate comparison record, immutable attempt history, inspected outcome record, selected identities, and accepted frozen state. A failed generation never becomes a canonical character or body template. Prior resolved solutions remain comparison evidence so later sheets do not repeat their construction.

### 3.2 No silent cross-layer substitution — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The following substitutions are explicitly invalid:

- renderer invents anatomy that the blueprint omitted;
- renderer invents concept meaning from a semantic label;
- renderer invents concept fusion because the fusion architecture was not serialized;
- renderer chooses differentiation because the five body solutions were not independently authored;
- renderer invents sub-concepts because the sub-concept layer was unresolved;
- renderer changes phase because clothing or presentation appears visually convenient;
- renderer alters a certified character because the requested format is crowded;
- art-style layer changes the character's body because a different body renders more easily;
- presentation layer uses pose to solve differentiation;
- colour layer uses palette changes to solve structural convergence;
- identity-finishing layer uses makeup or surface finishing alone to solve face convergence;
- micro-detail layer uses texture density to conceal weak macro architecture;
- post-render audit silently accepts a structural failure because the image is attractive;
- renderer-language translation removes a certified physical consequence instead of changing only renderer-facing wording.

Each of these failures is a layer-ownership violation.

### 3.3 Internal specification and renderer package are separate layers

The internal workflow may contain detailed validation terminology.

The renderer-facing package should contain positive concrete visual construction.

The renderer does not need the internal history of failed generations.

Because rendering intent may be influenced by conversational context, the final pre-call materialization must be self-sufficient and semantically closed. It contains only the current renderable visual construction, required camera/layout facts, active 2D rendering grammar, and necessary visible frozen dependencies. All other contextual material remains non-operative and outside the FINAL RENDER CONTEXT.
### 3.4 Renderer receives resolved design only

The renderer must realize an already-resolved design. It is never a design-authoring stage.

The certified Generation Package remains the authoritative internal specification. The Frozen Character Specification and Clean Renderer-Facing Description are fidelity-preserving views of that package for generation.

The internal Frozen Character Specification remains authoritative task state. After certification, renderer preflight, and a SUPPORTED capability result, preserve the certified Clean Renderer-Facing Description and enter READY TO RENDER. Only an explicit `RENDER` command permits materializing that description as one self-contained FINAL RENDER CONTEXT for submission. That final context must carry the complete current visible target without depending on earlier conversational shorthand or nonvisual metadata.

For every handoff:

- every renderer-visible instruction traces to certified state;
- no unresolved anatomy, concept interaction, material, identity, format, or style decision is delegated to rendering;
- no creative rewriting, embellishment, simplification, renaming, or reinterpretation occurs between certification and final context materialization;
- prior rendered images, failed images, exploratory drafts, rejected alternatives, and inactive phases are explicitly non-operative as render sources; prior images may be inspected only as observational evidence and never replace the current frozen specification.

The renderer realizes the certified design. It does not complete it.
### 3.5 Character blocks — HARD

The renderer-facing character blocks for the **active Phase-1 count** describe **final refined designs only**. Internal blueprints may contain exhaustive analytical fields, but the Clean Renderer-Facing Description uses a visual-priority order so the generator receives the character as a coherent design rather than as a checklist.

Within each character block use this order:

1. character identity, body regime, dominant silhouette, and primary visual hook;
2. face/head/eyes/hair identity and framing;
3. major body proportions and silhouette relationships needed to preserve the exact body regime;
4. the complete primary-fusion architecture expressed through its resulting visible body structures;
5. topology, cavities/apertures, appendages, and unusual joints where they materially affect anatomy or silhouette;
6. body substrate, material boundaries, texture behavior, and colour/value hierarchy;
7. the two subordinate influences through their actual visible consequences;
8. selected identity-defining secondary and micro detail that remains readable at the intended scale;
9. exact consistency of the same construction across the required views.

The renderer block does not repeat internal comparison matrices, rejected alternatives, random-pool records, pairwise bookkeeping, certification terminology, or hidden reasoning. It serializes the **physical result** of those processes.

Every visible identity-bearing system is resolved before serialization. Conditional absent systems remain internal N/A records rather than filler. A generic uniqueness instruction is not a structural specification, and a renderer-facing block containing unresolved aesthetic choices is not eligible for generation.

### 3.6 Isolated FINAL RENDER CONTEXT — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The certified Generation Package remains the authoritative design. Before READY TO RENDER, fully materialize the visible result internally as the Frozen Character Specification and derive the Clean Renderer-Facing Description from that exact state. Public display of the exhaustive frozen specification remains optional.

The FINAL RENDER CONTEXT is not a summary of the workflow or the source concepts. It is an operative visual payload with **three ordered blocks**. The order is mandatory and may not be inverted, blended, or omitted.

**Block 1 — IMMUTABLE PRESENTATION ENVELOPE.** State the already-certified presentation facts first and in concrete positive language: exact canvas/background, active figure count and spatial order, complete full-body framing, camera/view, head and torso orientation, shoulder and hip/lower-torso orientation, arm position, hand state, leg support, stance, and permitted visible object classes. For Phase 1, the visible object classes are limited to the certified character body, hair, body-owned anatomy/surface matter, certified appendages, and certified body-bound detached identity morphology where actually present. The background remains the certified plain analytical field. The envelope is immutable during rendering and character payload language has no authority to alter it.

**Block 2 — FROZEN CHARACTER PAYLOAD(S).** Serialize each active candidate independently through already-resolved visible construction only: complete body/proportion geometry; face/head/eyes/hair construction; morphology, topology, appendages, cavities/channels and unusual joints; material regions, boundaries, transitions and surface behavior; intrinsic body-owned structures; concrete colour/value placement; and the cross-view consistency anchors required by the active presentation contract.

**Block 3 — ACTIVE 2D DRAWING GRAMMAR.** State only the already-certified 2D rendering facts required to realize the frozen design faithfully.

Abstract aesthetic, temperament, status, desirability, or character-direction language is internal-only at handoff. If such a design property mattered during authorship, serialize only its frozen visible consequence in geometry, proportion, hair, face, material, colour, topology, appendage organization, or silhouette. Do not pass the abstract descriptor itself.

Everything outside these three blocks remains internal. Source-concept titles/lists, workflow terminology, validation results, design rationale, rejected alternatives, randomization records, narrative or thematic language, nonvisual identifiers, prior-image discussion, and inactive-phase material are not serialized into the operative renderer context.

Every active candidate is restated independently through its already-resolved visible construction. No renderer-facing clause asks the renderer to infer concept meaning, character anatomy, population differentiation, outfit architecture, weapon architecture, phase progression, presentation geometry, or another unresolved design decision.

Immediately before an explicit `RENDER` call, verify that Block 1 is complete and first, every later clause is compatible with Block 1, the full FINAL RENDER CONTEXT contains only allowed renderer-operative categories, the context fits the active renderer without truncation or omission, and it remains bound to the same frozen Revision-ID. Rerun the Renderer Capability Gate. Capability is SUPPORTED only if the handoff can preserve Block-1 priority over the character payload. If any check fails or support is UNKNOWN, status becomes HANDOFF UNSUPPORTED and no image call occurs.

Nothing may appear between the certified FINAL RENDER CONTEXT and the authorized image action that changes, dilutes, reframes, or competes with the certified target.
### 3.7 Intrinsic body-membership and analytical-presentation serialization — HARD

Phase-1 renderer serialization begins from the frozen body itself. Describe the actual concept-derived body surfaces directly through **anatomical continuity**: exact body region of origin, contiguous path across the body, depth or thickness relative to neighboring anatomy, transition into the adjacent substrate, attachment/root logic, termination boundary, segmentation where present, texture, and material behavior. Every complex intrinsic surface must read from the description as physically continuous with, grown from, replacing, penetrating, or structurally integrated into the body rather than as a separately worn layer.

Renderer-facing wording for intrinsic body matter uses anatomy/material terminology rather than garment or adornment terminology. Serialization introduces no independently attached decorative construction that is absent from the frozen body design. A spatially separated structure appears only when it is already frozen as certified body-bound detached identity morphology under §0.0, in which case its body-local anchor and invariant relation are stated explicitly.

Do not serialize any neutral analytical underlayer in Phase 1. If a design cannot preserve a clean reference presentation while leaving the body's proportions, joints, appendage roots, topology, and concept structures easy to inspect through intrinsic concept-derived body materialization alone, return to body-materialization/design resolution instead of introducing a separate layer.
### 3.8 Externalization and body-bound detached morphology serialization — HARD

For every supplied concept, serialize the certified character-level physical manifestation before any secondary styling. Ordinary concept materialization remains inside anatomy, topology, substrate, joints, appendage roots, appendages, proportions, face/head/hair, and silhouette.

A certified body-bound detached identity structure may be included only when it passed §0.0. Serialize it as a precise character-owned morphology: exact body-local anchor, count, size, geometry, orientation, distance from the anchor, material, symmetry/asymmetry, and invariant relation to the body. Do not describe it as an aura, ambient effect, floating decoration, atmospheric phenomenon, or free object.

### 3.9 Female-character identity serialization lock — HARD

Every character block MUST explicitly serialize the certified **female anime/manhwa character identity** before and alongside concept deformation. The renderer-facing package targets a complete female character rather than a generic humanoid, creature, mascot, object, or abstract form.

The exhaustive internal Frozen Character Specification retains the exact face/head construction, torso/chest architecture, waist/hip/lower-torso architecture, limbs, hands, feet, proportions, mass distribution, and silhouette. The Clean Renderer-Facing Description compiles those facts into concise visual construction: the character's female identity, body regime, major proportions, silhouette, face/head/hair identity, and any body relationships that materially determine the image at current scale.

Concept-derived creature, material, dimensional, temporal, behavioural-state, or abstract morphology is serialized as transformation of the already-resolved female character. The renderer is never asked to choose whether the result remains female or to infer a body regime from the concept labels.

**Internal rejection conditions include:**

- animal/creature body replacing the female character;
- object or abstract-form body replacing the female character;
- mascot/species-avatar construction;
- amorphous mass replacing readable character form;
- gender-ambiguous construction resulting from concept deformation;
- face-only female identity attached to a non-character body;
- concept label being the only mechanism intended to preserve female identity.

These rejection conditions remain internal validation language. The renderer receives the positive resolved female character construction.

### 3.10 External-phenomenon and semantic-concept translation lock — HARD

When a supplied concept conventionally suggests a surrounding phenomenon, symbolic object, narrative situation, or other non-body referent, the renderer-facing serialization MUST state the already-resolved character-level physical translation explicitly rather than asking rendering to interpret the word.

Weather, force, elemental, fluid, spatial, material-state, behavioural, social, contextual, and abstract concepts are carried through certified anatomy, topology, material, proportion, tension, repetition, branching, density, direction, surface behavior, appendage architecture, or another concrete design consequence. Emitted or environmental imagery is not a substitute.

The only detached exception is a certified body-bound identity structure under §0.0. Even then, the complete concept combination remains materially embodied in the character and the detached structure never becomes the sole carrier.

### 3.11 Population anti-collapse serialization — HARD

The renderer-facing package MUST serialize every character as a complete independent physical specification.

Each character block MUST state its unique stature/body regime, the proportion relationships necessary to preserve that regime, body substrate/material architecture, colour/value architecture, face/head/hair identity, dominant concept-fusion structures, major interaction consequences, defining topology/appendages where applicable, asymmetry where used, and silhouette. Pairwise and higher-order interaction bookkeeping remains internal; renderer-facing text describes the resulting fused physical structures directly. Justified absence of conditional systems is recorded internally rather than filled with invented anatomy. The active-count blocks MUST serialize materially different complete body constructions. Where candidates share the same fixed primary set, they must be materially different ways of turning that same set into one body; where selector-resolved sets differ, each block remains independently complete for its own frozen resolved set.

The renderer package serializes every active character as a complete independent physical specification. Each character block contains its own certified body, proportions, material system, appendage system, and structural architecture.

### 3.12 Embedded-quality serialization — HARD

Always serialize the embedded quality benchmark as concrete rendering requirements: premium 2D anime/manhwa construction, deliberate main-character-grade anatomy, crisp drawn structural edges, controlled value grouping, deliberate material separation, high but subordinate micro-detail, strong macro silhouette, and readable full-body construction.

Never use a character-copy instruction or rely on “high detail” alone.

### 3.13 Clean renderer-description lexical contract — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The Clean Renderer-Facing Description is a positive construction package. Internal audit terminology is not copied into it.

The true-front camera serialization explicitly encodes a direct front view: zero intentional yaw relative to the frozen frontal body plane, face and torso directed straight toward the camera, relaxed neutral arms, naturally supporting legs, and physical asymmetry preserved as body construction rather than created by pose or camera rotation.

The exact 90-degree profile and direct-rear views are mandatory for every initial Phase-1 identity and are serialized as the same frozen body in neutral standing.

The Clean Renderer-Facing Description is serialized as:

> **PHASE + TARGET-SHEET 16:9 FIVE-REGION GEOMETRY → PREMIUM 2D GRAMMAR → MANDATORY TRUE-FRONT/EXACT-PROFILE/DIRECT-REAR NEUTRAL-VIEW CONTRACT → FIVE COMPLETE INDEPENDENT CHARACTER BLOCKS → MANDATORY-MORPHOLOGY + MATERIAL + FUSION PRESERVATION IF USED**

The renderer-facing package begins with:

> **16:9 LANDSCAPE → EXACTLY FIVE LEFT-TO-RIGHT REGIONS → FIVE LARGE UNCROPPED FULL-BODY TRUE FRONTS + FIVE EXACT PROFILES + FIVE DIRECT REARS → STRICT NEUTRAL STANDING → PLAIN WHITE FIELD**

No thematic prose precedes this format contract. The serializer does not append generic rejection lists; structural rejection remains an internal certification and post-render function.

### 3.14 Concept labels and semantic isolation — HARD

Exact supplied concept labels remain in internal traceability, decomposition, fusion, and validation records.

The FINAL RENDER CONTEXT defaults to **label-free positive visual construction**. Once a concept has been physically resolved, describe what the character visibly is rather than repeating the source word and inviting the renderer to invent an association. A literal label is retained in renderer-facing text only when it names a directly visible morphology more precisely than its physical description and introduces no unresolved semantic interpretation.

For nonvisual or context-heavy concepts, do not import associated activities, social roles, locations, relationships, scenarios, biography, or symbolic props. Translate only the certified geometry, anatomy, material, topology, proportion, rhythm, colour/value, texture, appendage, and cross-concept consequences.

`Fuse the concepts`, `make them unique`, `make them extreme`, and similar phrases are supplementary only. The actual physical structures must be serialized.

### 3.15 Complete positive character serialization

Each character's positive renderer block must be sufficiently detailed that a renderer can reproduce the certified body without inventing missing structures.

The package must state the actual resolved design.

It must not defer to:

- concept labels;
- generic uniqueness instructions;
- generic diversity language;
- generic 2D language;
- generic quality language.

### 3.16 Concept-fusion proof precedes final character serialization — HARD

Internally, complete concept fusion is resolved and proved before subordinate finishing can be allowed to compensate for it. Renderer-facing order, however, follows the visual-priority structure in §3.5 so the generator receives each character as a coherent finished design rather than as a proof transcript.

The internal causal order is:

> **complete fused body architecture → required interaction proof → independent body identity → subordinate integration → face/head/hair and character-identity completion → materials/colour/hierarchy → final refinement**

The renderer-facing block then serializes the **resulting final physical design** in §3.5 order. It does not list pairwise equations, certification records, or a concept-by-concept feature inventory. Colour, cosmetics, or micro-detail can never serve as evidence for an otherwise unresolved fusion, but they may appear earlier in renderer-facing prose when that improves visual instruction clarity.

### 3.17 Independent blocks are literal

Character 02 must contain Character 02's actual physical specification.

Character 03 must contain Character 03's actual physical specification.

Character 04 must contain Character 04's actual physical specification.

Character 05 must contain Character 05's actual physical specification.

Do not serialize:

> "same as Character 01 but different."

> "variation of Character 02."

> "similar body with altered wings."

> "same fusion, new colour."

The complete **render-critical** physical specification must be present in each clean character block; the exhaustive analytical specification remains in the internal Frozen Character Specification.

Renderer-language translation is a fidelity-preserving translation layer. It uses concrete neutral physical wording, reduces accidental semantic ambiguity, and preserves the same resolved anatomy, topology, material, proportion, structure, phase, and presentation. The translation preserves the certified concept embodiment and presentation exactly while keeping the renderer-facing wording clear and neutral. Faithful neutral serialization is required before generation.

### 3.18 Submission handling — HARD

Renderer-facing wording preserves the exact resolved design. A non-executed submission does not establish a specific lexical cause; record the returned evidence and preserve state. Section 10 governs outcome classification, same-design serializer correction, READY TO RENDER recertification, and explicit resubmission authorization.
### 3.19 Neutral presentation without structural loss — HARD

Use professional character-reference terminology and positive physical construction while preserving the certified structural design. Translation must preserve the complete proportion regime, appendage roots, body materials, topology, asymmetry, silhouette, face/head identity, and every render-critical visible consequence of concept-integrated intrinsic coverage.

If an image attempt does not execute, the frozen design remains unchanged. Any §10.2 same-design serializer correction may re-order, compress, or clarify visual wording only; it may not change intrinsic coverage, body architecture, materials, or any other frozen design decision. After correction and recertification the revision returns to READY TO RENDER and requires a new explicit user `RENDER` command before another image call.
### 3.20 Renderer translation examples — HARD / OPERATIONAL

The following examples define the translation principle and are generalized operational guidance. They do not replace the certified blueprint; they demonstrate how the renderer-facing package must preserve it.

#### 3.20.1 Raw concept label is not sufficient

Invalid renderer instruction:

> **CONCEPT A + CONCEPT B + CONCEPT C**

The renderer cannot be expected to determine the correct physical embodiment from those labels alone.

Required renderer package:

> **CERTIFIED FEMALE BODY ARCHITECTURE + CERTIFIED A/B/C FUSED PHYSICAL IMPLEMENTATION + CERTIFIED POPULATION DIFFERENTIATION + CERTIFIED PHASE-1 FORMAT**

The renderer receives the resolved physical implementation rather than being asked to invent it.

#### 3.20.2 External presentation is not an acceptable substitute

Invalid renderer serialization:

> female character with one supplied concept represented by a surrounding phenomenon, another by an environmental display, and another by a free symbolic object.

Required renderer serialization:

> female character whose body substrate, anatomy, topology, appendages, internal structures, materials, silhouette, and any specifically certified body-bound identity morphology directly embody the resolved complete fusion.

#### 3.20.3 Feature-list serialization is insufficient when it conceals fusion

Insufficient serialization:

> A-derived wings, B-derived horns, C-derived markings.

Required serialization:

> independently resolved body architecture in which the geometry, origin, material, topology, proportions, attachment systems, and silhouette of the major structures are jointly determined by the complete concept fusion, while each concept remains highly visible.

#### 3.20.4 Differentiation must be serialized structurally

Insufficient population serialization:

> five female characters with different colours, appendage sizes, and surface details.

Required population serialization:

> five independent female body constructions per sheet group with materially different height, body regime, body architecture, fusion locus, pairwise interaction structures, higher-order fusion structures, substrate, topology, appendage-root architecture, silhouette mechanism, and asymmetry, while preserving every fixed slot and each candidate's complete frozen resolved primary set.

#### 3.20.5 Neutral standing must be explicit

The renderer-facing package must explicitly require the same neutral standing condition for every required view.

A generic “character sheet” instruction is insufficient because it permits the renderer to introduce posing, gesture, action, or alternative presentation.

#### 3.20.6 Format must be explicit

The renderer-facing package explicitly requests one continuous 16:9 landscape canvas with five left-to-right character regions and exactly five dominant uncropped full-body true-front neutral-standing characters on plain white.

Exact-profile and direct-rear views are mandatory. Layout must preserve all five principal fronts at useful analytical scale while also fitting the ten subordinate views without posing, cropping, or character simplification.

#### 3.20.7 Simple macro / intricate micro must be explicit

The renderer-facing package must explicitly require immediate macro readability before intricate micro-detail.

It must communicate that:

- silhouettes are primary;
- major body architecture is primary;
- major concept structures are primary;
- micro-detail is subordinate;
- detail density cannot obscure body readability;
- complex micro-detail must reinforce the resolved macro structure.

#### 3.20.8 Quality serialization must be concrete

A generic instruction to “make it high quality” is insufficient.

The renderer-facing quality package must explicitly encode:

- premium 2D anime/manhwa construction;
- crisp controlled 2D line and shape language;
- deliberate anatomy;
- strong macro silhouette;
- integrated large-scale body structures;
- controlled material hierarchy;
- high-frequency detail subordinate to macro structure;
- readable full-body anatomy;
- independent character identity;
- equal quality across all five characters.

#### 3.20.9 Renderer-language translation must preserve structure

When wording benefits from clarification for renderer precision, the translation must preserve the complete certified design.

It may change wording.

It may not change:

- body architecture;
- body proportions;
- chest architecture;
- concept-fusion architecture;
- concept interaction structures;
- appendage architecture;
- topology;
- substrate;
- material architecture;
- silhouette;
- asymmetry;
- female-character identity;
- neutral standing;
- Phase-1 clean character-reference sheet geometry and intrinsic-coverage fidelity;
- required format.

### 3.21 Character identity must be render-critically serialized — HARD

Every character block must encode the character-specific identity systems that are visible and important at the intended sheet scale: body regime, silhouette, face/head/hair, major material/value relationships, dominant concept-fusion structures, defining topology/appendages where applicable, and the specific visual hook that distinguishes the character.

A renderer-facing phrase such as “five unique female characters” is insufficient. The actual visible differences must already exist in the five blocks. Internal proof fields remain in the blueprint rather than being copied verbatim into the clean renderer description.

**ANTI-TEMPLATE SERIALIZATION PRIORITY — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Each character payload must state the body-core facts that distinguish her from the other four **before** optional surface detail: body regime/stature; shoulder-ribcage-torso relationships; chest/waist/hip-lower-torso architecture; limb proportions/mass distribution; face/head/hair architecture; dominant fusion locus; and major appendage root/support architecture where present. Do not spend renderer-context capacity on micro-ornament while any of those distinguishing macro/strong-meso facts are implicit. If shortening the payload would erase a chassis difference and leave the renderer with five similar generic female bases, compression fails and must be redone.

**Per-character macro structural anchor — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** Each initial-sheet character payload begins with one concise positive macro-geometry clause that makes the frozen body impossible to confuse with another payload at first read. That clause states the character's actual stature/body regime, central torso/ribcage/waist/hip-lower-torso construction, dominant limb/mass relationship, and the count/origin/support/form of any major appendage system that materially affects silhouette. It describes resolved geometry only; it does not say merely `different`, `unique`, or `varied`. Across the five payloads, these clauses must describe five materially different physical chassis. If two clauses could be swapped between characters without forcing a visible redesign, serialization has collapsed important differentiation and fails preflight.

### 3.22 Face serialization must be concrete — HARD

Each character block must carry enough face information to prevent generic face convergence.

At minimum serialize the certified differences in:

- head shape;
- eye geometry;
- iris structure;
- brow geometry;
- nose/mouth construction;
- jaw/chin geometry;
- hair silhouette;
- face-framing hair;
- facial line hierarchy;
- character-specific identity finishing, with cosmetic details only where deliberately selected.

### 3.23 Aperture serialization must identify its actual body locus — HARD

When an aperture/cavity/topology system is render-critical, do not serialize only a generic label. State the few geometric facts that determine its visible identity: anatomical locus, overall scale/shape, orientation/depth, connection to neighboring body structure, dominant material boundary, and its major role in the fused architecture.

More granular topology parameters remain in the full blueprint and Frozen Character Specification when they are not reliably visible at the current sheet scale. The clean renderer block prioritizes the relationships needed to prevent relocation, collapse, or generic repetition.

### 3.24 Appendage serialization must identify architecture — HARD

When appendages are present because they belong to major recognizable vocabulary or because the fusion creates them, the clean character block must state their render-critical architecture rather than only naming the appendage class.

At minimum, preserve the visible combination of class, count/pairing, anatomical origin, dominant direction, body-relative scale, primary form/curvature/branching, articulation or segmentation where visually important, material, asymmetry where used, and silhouette relationship. Include topology or neighboring-body interaction when it materially changes the read.

The exhaustive parameter record remains in the blueprint. Renderer-facing text groups correlated facts into concise visual clauses so the appendage is easy to realize. When the same appendage class is mandatory across characters, differentiation comes from the independently resolved architecture, not omission.

### 3.25 Fusion serialization must precede feature serialization — HARD

The character block must first describe the fused body architecture and then identify the visible component structures that prove it.

The renderer must receive:

> complete fused body architecture → major shared structures → individual concept visibility → secondary structures → micro-detail.

It must not receive:

> Concept A feature → Concept B feature → Concept C feature → generic body.

### 3.26 Neutral standing must be serialized without ambiguity — HARD

Every required view uses the same balanced neutral standing reference stance: torso aligned to the relevant camera, shoulders relaxed, arms resting with readable separation from the torso, weight evenly supported, and legs placed stably for anatomical inspection. Static asymmetry belongs to the body itself.

The clean renderer description states this positive standing geometry directly. Pose-rejection vocabulary remains an internal validation function rather than the main renderer instruction.

### 3.27 Phase-1 view architecture serialization — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Every initial five-character sheet serializes **exactly three full-body depictions per identity**: one dominant true-front neutral-standing view, one smaller exact 90-degree profile, and one smaller direct-rear view of the same frozen character. The five fronts remain the principal analytical views and must stay large enough to inspect body architecture, mandatory morphology, fusion structures, material boundaries, face/hair identity, and proportion regime.

Side/rear geometry is fully resolved in the Frozen Character Specification and serialized as mandatory reconstruction evidence for all five identities. The ten subordinate views exist for exact cross-view reconstruction, not page decoration, and never replace or redesign front-view character space.

### 3.28 Layout serialization must remain analytical — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The renderer-facing layout is one continuous 16:9 landscape image with exactly five left-to-right character regions on one coherent plain white field. Each region is owned by one independently authored character and is dominated by her complete uncropped true-front neutral-standing body. The field remains visually quiet so body silhouette, proportions, major morphology, materials, topology, and fusion architecture carry the image.

No portrait-card framing, decorative dividers, non-analytical composition, raised-arm posing, prop display, scenery, symbolic object, emitted concept phenomenon, or Phase-2 outfit architecture may consume the analytical character space. Certified body-bound detached identity morphology under §0.0 remains character structure, not page decoration.

### 3.29 Macro/micro serialization must protect hierarchy — HARD

The renderer-facing package must state that:

- the five silhouettes are immediately readable;
- major body masses are immediately readable;
- major concept-fusion structures are immediately readable;
- major appendages are individually readable;
- major apertures remain visually legible;
- intricate detail is subordinate;
- dense micro-structure does not become the macro silhouette.

### 3.30 Main-character quality must be serialized positively — HARD

The renderer-facing package must request:

- polished anime/manhwa character-design quality;
- fully developed faces;
- deliberate eye design;
- deliberate hair design;
- coherent anatomy;
- refined body architecture;
- refined appendages;
- refined materials;
- refined surface details;
- deliberate asymmetry;
- controlled silhouette;
- high-resolution linework;
- consistent professional finish across all five characters.

The quality requirement applies independently to every character.

### 3.31 Positive rendering language takes precedence over negative lists — HARD

The final generation call should primarily consist of positive physical and rendering instructions.

Internal rejection criteria remain active, but they should not dominate the renderer-facing serialization.

This is a renderer-context hygiene measure, not a relaxation of any validation requirement.

### 3.32 Renderer-control wording must remain low-noise

Internal control wording must not dominate the renderer-facing package.

Internal protection remains extensive.

The final renderer package should use the most direct neutral physical terminology available.

The aim is:

> **MAXIMUM STRUCTURAL FIDELITY + MINIMUM UNNECESSARY LEXICAL AMBIGUITY**

The renderer-language layer must not weaken:

- character identity;
- body differentiation;
- concept materialization;
- fusion;
- style;
- quality;
- format.

### 3.33 No renderer-facing concept metaphor

Avoid metaphoric or narrative concept language when a physical description is available.

Prefer:

> altered body topology, integrated branching appendage roots, structured cavity systems, controlled material transitions

over abstract narrative descriptions.

The renderer receives what to draw, not an invitation to interpret.

### 3.34 Three-sheet Broad Phase-1 output lock — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Every initial Broad Phase-1 CREATE defines **one fifteen-character population** and **three separate exact 16:9 landscape analytical sheet outputs**:

- Sheet 1 = Characters 01–05;
- Sheet 2 = Characters 06–10;
- Sheet 3 = Characters 11–15.

Each sheet contains exactly five identities and exactly fifteen full-body depictions: one dominant true-front, one exact profile, and one direct rear per identity. All fifteen characters are completely authored, differentiated, refined, frozen, and certified before the first initial-sheet render is allowed. The three sheets are not separate CREATE operations and no later character derives from an earlier rendered image.

Each sheet receives its own sheet-local FINAL RENDER CONTEXT and may independently become READY TO RENDER after preflight and Renderer Capability Gate. No renderer capsule file is required or authoritative. Rendering remains controlled by the explicit-render state machine in §0.0, §1.9.1, and §10.2: one explicit `RENDER` authorizes one image-generation call for one READY sheet only.

The complete presentation formula is:

> **ONE FIFTEEN-CHARACTER POPULATION → THREE 16:9 SHEETS → FIVE IDENTITIES PER SHEET → THREE VIEWS PER IDENTITY → FIFTEEN FULL-BODY DEPICTIONS PER SHEET → PLAIN WHITE FIELD**

Global fifteen-character state is orchestration/design state only. The renderer receives only the target sheet's five certified visible character payloads plus the immutable presentation envelope, shared studio layer, active 2D grammar, cross-view lock, and terminal layout lock.
### 3.35 True-front geometry is a camera property — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The dominant front is a geometric analytical front elevation, not a perspective hero view. The camera has zero intentional yaw relative to the certified frontal body plane. Physical asymmetry remains present because it belongs to body design, not camera or pose.

The front provides the principal uncropped inspection of face, eyes, shoulders, ribcage, chest, torso, waist, hip/lower-torso region, limbs, mandatory morphology, materials, asymmetry, topology, appendage roots, body-bound identity structures, and silhouette. Structures naturally hidden from the front remain resolved internally and are inspected through the profile/rear views required by the active presentation contract where applicable, rather than by rotating or posing the principal front.

### 3.36 Analytical supplementary-view purity — HARD / ABSOLUTE WHEN REQUIRED

Profile/rear support is analytical only. For initial Phase 1, targeted single-character Phase-1 revisions, and the default Phase-4 analytical assembly, the required support views are an exact 90-degree profile and a direct rear of the same frozen body, kept smaller than the principal front. In another presentation contract, any explicitly required support view follows the same identity-purity rule. No extra front, close-up, crop, material study, palette study, concept icon, label module, or auxiliary block is inserted.

If required subordinate views threaten front readability, reallocate composition/scale while preserving complete extents; do not omit a required profile or rear view.

### 3.37 Simple macro means deliberate large-form hierarchy — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Simple macro can be visually straightforward while the character remains fully authored. The requirement is therefore not to make the entire character simple. The requirement is to make the major forms immediately legible.

Macro design owns:

- dominant silhouette;
- overall stature and scale;
- body regime;
- major torso architecture;
- chest architecture;
- hip/lower-torso architecture;
- major limb proportions;
- major appendage architecture;
- major aperture/topology architecture;
- major concept-fusion structures;
- primary asymmetry;
- major material regions.

Micro design owns fine segmentation, surface transitions, hair strands, small topology, fine appendage articulation, subtle material transitions, small facial detail, and similar subordinate information.

Micro-detail must reinforce the macro rather than create competing silhouettes or visual saturation.

### 3.38 Petite is a body regime, not a miniature render — HARD / ABSOLUTE

A petite character may be substantially shorter and more compact than the other characters while remaining a fully developed principal-character design.

The compact-body target is an attractive, fully proportioned character rather than a chibi or caricature. Resolve a distinct head/body ratio, torso depth, hip/lower-torso region, and limb construction instead of shrinking a tall design.

A petite solution must deliberately resolve its own head/body relationship, torso length, ribcage, chest, waist, hip/lower-torsos, limb lengths, hands, feet, face, hair, appendages, materials, silhouette, character identity, and any optional cosmetic finishing selected for that design.

Petite does not mean uniformly shrinking another character. It means authoring a coherent compact body regime.

### 3.39 Character independence is multi-system aesthetic authorship — HARD / ABSOLUTE
Independence is a whole-character authorship problem. Height alone, chest alone, colour alone, appendage scale alone, or aperture relocation alone cannot establish a new identity.

Within each initial sheet group, the five characters' major design decisions must form five different coherent systems. They may share fixed supplied concepts and the same rendering methodology, but they must not read as siblings, alternate forms, recolours, costume variants, transformation stages, or one template rendered five times. The full CREATE population remains fifteen characters and is additionally subject to the global collision review.

Each character therefore requires simultaneous divergence across body architecture, concept-fusion architecture, topology/aperture architecture where applicable, appendage architecture where applicable, face architecture, hair architecture, material architecture, colour architecture, asymmetry, and silhouette.

The five characters within a sheet group are not required to share a family resemblance merely because they use fixed concepts and the same rendering language.

Commonality belongs primarily to the supplied concept set and the active 2D rendering methodology. Character identity must arise from independent design decisions across multiple mutually reinforcing systems.

For each character actively author a unique combination of:

- stature;
- body regime/build;
- head/body relationship;
- shoulders;
- ribcage;
- torso length and geometry;
- chest volume, projection and placement;
- waist;
- hip/lower-torso region;
- hips;
- limb length and taper;
- hand/foot architecture;
- skeletal/topological architecture;
- musculature/body mass;
- aperture/cavity system;
- appendage count and pair structure;
- appendage origin;
- appendage form;
- appendage length/thickness;
- appendage articulation/branching;
- appendage material;
- asymmetry;
- fusion locus;
- concept-carrier distribution;
- body substrate;
- material architecture;
- colour architecture, with hue-count recorded descriptively;
- colour distribution;
- face architecture;
- eye architecture;
- hair architecture;
- character-identity finishing;
- macro silhouette.

These are not random sliders. Each must be selected because it contributes to a specific, coherent, aesthetically resolved character.

The result should remain recognizably different when colour-only cues, optional cosmetics, hairstyle styling, expression, pose, lighting, markings, and micro-detail are conceptually stripped away while structural face/head geometry, actual hair mass, materials, and body construction remain.

### 3.40 Concept prominence is a body-scale requirement — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Concept prominence is measured at the same visual scale at which the character's major anatomy, materials, and identity are read. A concept that becomes legible only through micro-detail, labels, or external presentation is under-resolved.

Every supplied primary concept must be unmistakably present inside the character itself, but **no fixed carrier count or mandatory anatomical spread is imposed**. A concept may be deliberately concentrated into one or several selected body regions when that produces a stronger identity; completeness is established by the strength, physical specificity, and interactions of its carriers rather than by uniform coverage of the body.

For every active concept, the blueprint must identify:

1. its complete major recognizable vocabulary for the active interpretation;
2. one or more unmistakable macro or strong meso-scale physical/identity-bearing carriers;
3. its genuine physical interaction with every other active primary concept;
4. its complete-set higher-order participation when three or more concepts are supplied;
5. the reason the concept remains recognizable after labels, external presentation, colour-only cues, and optional cosmetic finishing are removed as evidence.

A tiny marking, isolated colour accent, minor decorative appendage, or micro-detail is insufficient as the sole carrier. Conversely, an exceptional integrated system may carry several of these obligations without being repeated across the body.

The concepts must be materially visible without requiring surrounding effects, environmental objects, symbols, props, or explanatory labels, while aesthetic economy controls any additional manifestation.
### 3.41 Concept fusion must be recalculated independently for every character — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The complete concept set is constant across the population. The physical solution is not.

For each character independently resolve:

- principal fusion locus or dominant shared construction;
- secondary fusion loci/systems where useful;
- the body systems most strongly transformed;
- **every distinct pairwise interaction for the actual number of supplied concepts**;
- the complete-set higher-order interaction for three or more concepts;
- meaningful intermediate higher-order interactions only when they materially determine the construction;
- shared structures caused by several concepts;
- topology/material interaction where applicable;
- appendage/topology interaction where applicable;
- cavity/aperture/body interaction where applicable;
- asymmetry generated by the fusion where useful;
- silhouette and focal-hierarchy consequence.

For a two-concept command, there is one pair and no invented third concept. For a three-concept command A × B × C, resolve A×B, A×C, B×C, and A×B×C. For larger sets, enumerate all n(n−1)/2 pairs and the complete-set fusion as defined in Section 1.7.

No character may inherit the fusion architecture of another character and merely alter colour, height, or surface detail. For every initial character, the Frozen Character Specification must resolve a distinct principal causal construction for the complete concept set, including the dominant shared structure, the body systems it transforms, every required pairwise consequence, and the complete-set higher-order consequence. The supplied concepts must remain physically distinguishable within those shared structures rather than disappearing into one generic undifferentiated substrate.
### 3.42 Aperture and cavity placement must be authored as part of the whole design — HARD / ABSOLUTE

When the concept set produces apertures, cavities, openings, channels, missing-volume topology, or related internal structures, their anatomical locus is an active design decision.

The population must not repeatedly place the same aperture in the same convenient limb location.

Across characters, vary the relevant locus, count, scale, depth, geometry, orientation, branching, distribution, asymmetry, surrounding material, and interaction with other concept-derived structures according to the independently resolved body design.

The aperture system must be aesthetically integrated with the body regime rather than pasted onto it.

### 3.43 Appendage architecture must be independently authored — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Where the concepts produce appendages, the compiler must independently resolve their count, pair count where relevant, symmetry/asymmetry, origin, attachment depth, orientation, length, thickness, branching, segmentation, articulation, material, internal construction, relationship to the skeleton and torso, interaction with apertures/topology, and silhouette.

Repeated default wing pairs, repeated horn placement, repeated tail placement, or repeated appendage silhouettes across the population constitute population convergence unless independently justified by the complete body architecture. When a morphology is mandatory in all five characters of an applicable initial sheet group, each character's Frozen Character Specification must give it a structurally distinct implementation through its origin, attachment, support, topology, articulation, internal construction, interaction role, or another comparably consequential property; size, colour, curvature, or surface detail alone cannot establish that distinction. Across the full fifteen-character population, the additional global collision review separately rejects repeated construction severe enough to make characters interchangeable.
### 3.44 Colour architecture is internally differentiated per character and independently authored across the population — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Each initial character must receive substantial internal body-scale colour differentiation. Resolve at least two meaningfully distinct colour/value systems across major body or material regions, or an equally substantial gradient, multi-zone, stratified, or other non-uniform colour construction. A monochrome or near-monochrome solution is permitted only when the user explicitly requests it or when the independently resolved concept construction genuinely requires it.

The Frozen Character Specification assigns colour directly to the character's body substrate, concept-derived materials, appendages, hair, eyes, and other identity-bearing regions. Hair and eyes are independently authored and do not automatically inherit the body's dominant hue family.

Population differentiation is judged through the **architecture of colour**, including dominant and subordinate hue families, value hierarchy, saturation hierarchy, warm/cool organization, material-specific colour, location of light/dark masses, location of saturated focal accents, hair/body relation, eye/body relation, appendage/body relation, cavity/interior/exterior relation where applicable, and distribution across the silhouette.

Within each initial sheet group, across its five characters, several of those relationships must change simultaneously. Reassigning different dominant hues to the same underlying colour distribution is insufficient. Hue count remains descriptive evidence rather than a diversity score. The global fifteen-character collision review additionally rejects near-template reuse of the same complete colour/body identity construction across sheets.
### 3.45 Main-character quality and character identity are mandatory per character — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Every character must be individually strong enough to function as the principal visual character of a high-quality anime/manhwa work or premium gacha release.

This includes developed cranial and face architecture, deliberate eye identity, deliberate hair architecture, coherent body construction, refined proportions, refined materials, deliberate surface design, intelligently authored colour/value structure, silhouette, asymmetry where useful, complete concept-fusion quality, and a deliberate appeal mode.

**Character identity is mandatory; conventional cosmetics are not.** Makeup, blush, lip colour, eyeliner, eyeshadow, nail treatment, decorative markings, pigmentation, unusual sclera treatment, or other beauty details may be selected when they improve the exact identity. A character may also use no conventional makeup at all and still pass if her underlying face, eyes, hair, materials, markings, proportions, and visual finishing are sufficiently distinctive and complete.

Identity finishing completes an already strong character. It never substitutes for structural identity, facial identity, concept fusion, or population differentiation.

### 3.46 Renderer-facing serialization uses positive language only — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The renderer-facing generation package uses positive, concrete visual construction language rather than long prohibition lists. For initial Phase 1 it serializes, in visual priority order:

- exact 16:9 landscape canvas and five left-to-right regions;
- exactly five large full-body true-front neutral-standing characters;
- each character's independent body regime and silhouette;
- exact face/head/eye/hair identity;
- every mandatory major recognizable feature with exact implementation;
- exact concept-fusion and higher-order shared structures;
- exact appendage/body-bound identity structure count, origin or anchor, geometry, material, and relation to the body;
- exact topology and cavities/openings where applicable;
- exact material hierarchy with materially distinct illustrated behavior;
- exact intrinsic body surface architecture across the chest and lower torso, with no separate studio/reference layer, neutral underlayer, or fallback garment-analogue;
- exact colour/value architecture;
- five mandatory exact 90-degree profiles and five mandatory direct-rear views, each locked to the same frozen identity as its dominant front and kept subordinate in scale to the front.

The package does not ask the renderer to infer missing design from raw concepts, generic uniqueness language, or quality adjectives.

### 3.47 BODY-CENTRIC PRESENTATION SCOPE — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Phase 1 is a character-construction sheet.

All concept realization must therefore be contained within the female character and the character's directly body-derived systems.

The visual field around the character remains neutral and analytically empty.

The compiler resolves concepts through:

- anatomy;
- body topology;
- body proportions;
- hair;
- eyes;
- facial construction;
- character-identity finishing, including optional cosmetics where selected;
- skin or other body substrate;
- markings;
- appendages;
- appendage roots;
- cavities and apertures;
- internal structural relationships;
- material transitions;
- silhouette;
- other directly body-derived systems.
