The renderer-facing package must describe those resolved character properties positively.

External presentation is not used as a carrier for concept information.

## 4 WORKFLOW ORGANISATION

### 4.1 Certified data flow — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The operative data flow is:

> **SUPPLIED PRIMARY-SLOT INPUT → FIXED / TLCR / SELECTOR-FAMILY / X / X{query} RESOLUTION → FREEZE EACH CANDIDATE'S COMPLETE RESOLVED PRIMARY CONCEPT SET → ZERO-INFERENCE EXTRACTION + CANONICAL CONCEPT-KNOWLEDGE LOADING → UNIVERSAL PHYSICAL DECOMPOSITION → COMPLETE RECOGNIZABLE-CONCEPT VOCABULARY → FIFTEEN INDEPENDENT COMPLETE-FUSION BODY SOLUTIONS → THREE CHECKPOINT-STRENGTH FIVE-CHARACTER DIFFERENTIATION PROOFS → GLOBAL FIFTEEN-CHARACTER COLLISION REVIEW → AESTHETIC SEARCH/TASTE REJECTION → REASONED ERA/CULTURE/CIVILIZATION SUB-CONCEPT 1 + GENUINELY RANDOM OPEN-RESERVOIR SUB-CONCEPT 2 → REASONED/EXPLICIT DESIGN-DIRECTION ROUTING INSIDE THE CONCEPT-VALID SPACE → CHARACTER-IDENTITY COMPLETION → MATERIAL + INTELLIGENT COLOUR AUTHORSHIP → CONCEPT-INTEGRATED INTRINSIC-COVERAGE RESOLUTION → VISUAL-HIERARCHY / DENSITY / CONTRAST PASS → FOUR-PASS FINAL REFINEMENT → WEAKEST-CANDIDATE COMPARISON → IMAGE-GENERATION ROBUSTNESS GATE → FREEZE ALL FIFTEEN INTERNAL CHARACTER SPECIFICATIONS → COMPILE THREE SHEET-LOCAL FINAL RENDER CONTEXTS → PREFLIGHT + RENDERER CAPABILITY GATE PER SHEET → INDEPENDENT READY TO RENDER STATES → USER `RENDER` → RECHECK TARGET SHEET → EXACTLY ONE TARGET-SHEET IMAGE CALL → ACTUAL-IMAGE AUDIT → OUTCOME RECORD → USER-CONTROLLED CONTINUATION**

No layer may jump over an unresolved predecessor.

ALTER and REFINE preserve the selected character identity.

They are not fresh concept generation.

For a user-requested alteration or fusion, the explicitly selected Frozen Specification is the authoritative identity anchor. Any available rendered image is observational evidence only: it may help inspect visible source features, but it never overrides the specification and never becomes the operative render source for the new revision. Initial concept creation uses its newly resolved blueprint. A requested design revision produces a new candidate only after complete new pre-generation certification.

Only the requested target and genuine dependencies may change.

Character Fusion is separate from Concept Fusion:

- **Concept Fusion:** concepts are fused before body construction.
- **Character Fusion:** existing resolved characters are fused only when explicitly requested.

Later phases may not repair a failed Phase-1 body.

Later phases MUST preserve:

- body architecture;
- proportions;
- unusual anatomy;
- concept-derived structures;
- internal topology;
- appendage origins;
- asymmetry;
- silhouette;
- face/eye identity;
- hair architecture;
- material identity;
- distinctive structures.

Later phases add systems. They do not normalize, erase, or retroactively redesign earlier systems.

### 4.2 Female visual identity

All initial Character Creation identities are **female**.

Do not infer or invent biography or real-world identity. Temporal primary concepts remain eligible visual design inputs. Sub-concept 1 is restricted to an actual real-world Era / Culture / Civilization source under §5.10.1 and remains a subordinate visual-design input rather than a biographical assignment.

The Broad Phase-1 population is resolved as one fifteen-character population partitioned into three five-character sheet groups.

For initial Broad Phase 1:

1. resolve fixed/TLCR/selector slots and freeze each candidate's complete Resolved Primary Concept Set before body construction;
2. independently resolve all fifteen complete characters;
3. prove Characters 01–05, 06–10, and 11–15 separately through the checkpoint-strength five-way matrix and all ten within-sheet pairwise comparisons;
4. perform the global fifteen-character collision review;
5. complete subordinate influences, Design Direction routing, alternative-design search, four-pass final refinement, weakest-character comparison, and image-generation robustness checks for all fifteen;
6. freeze all fifteen complete render-ready Frozen Character Specifications and the three sheet revision sets internally;
7. compile three self-contained sheet-local FINAL RENDER CONTEXTS, each containing only its exact five characters plus the mandatory presentation envelope, shared studio layer, active 2D grammar, cross-view lock, and terminal layout lock;
8. run renderer preflight and Renderer Capability Gate independently for all three sheets and mark each passing sheet READY TO RENDER;
9. make **zero** image calls until an explicit `RENDER`; then one `RENDER` authorizes exactly one call for one READY target sheet;
10. inspect the actual one-call output and record pass/failure without silently changing frozen designs or automatically continuing to another sheet.

A Phase-1 ALTER/REFINE/FUSE retains the established Phase-1 analytical presentation unless the targeted operation explicitly requires another authorized Phase-1 presentation. Phase 1 remains the complete body-construction and user-directed body-development stage until the user explicitly starts Phase 2.

### 4.3 Complete phase and operation routing — HARD

**Rule.** Route an operation by its active phase and explicit command. A phase can contain many selections, fusions, refinements, and alterations. Output completion and phase ending are different events.

| Active work | Design ownership | Protected inputs | Default initial output |
|---|---|---|---|
| Phase 1 initial CREATE | Exactly fifteen complete independently authored bodies in one population | Primary Input Specification; each candidate's frozen resolved primary set; standing rules | Three exact 16:9 analytical sheets of five; each identity has mandatory front/profile/rear; all fifteen resolved/frozen before any initial render |
| Phase 1 ALTER / REFINE / FUSE | Requested body development | Non-targeted source identity and required primary fusion | Requested result count; targeted single-character result defaults to exact 9:16 analytical front/profile/rear presentation |
| Phase 2 initial catalogue | Dedicated clothing systems for the selected frozen body | Body, character identity and finishing, material identity, primary concepts, two sub-concepts | Ten outfits, two rows of five |
| Phase 2 ALTER / REFINE / FUSE | Requested clothing development | Frozen body and non-targeted garment decisions | Requested result count, catalogue presentation |
| Phase 3 initial catalogue | Dedicated weapon systems | Frozen body and outfit | Ten weapons, two rows of five |
| Phase 3 ALTER / REFINE / FUSE | Requested weapon development | Body, outfit, selected category unless explicitly changed | Requested result count, catalogue presentation |
| Phase 4 assembly | Presentation of the chosen completed character | Frozen body, clothing, and weapon | Exact 9:16 analytical sheet: dominant true front + exact profile + direct rear on white |

**Explanation.** Initial-population counts apply to initial population creation. They do not force five clones of a requested single fusion or ten copies of one altered weapon. If FUSE asks for one composite, produce one new candidate. An ALTER of specified candidates creates the corresponding revised candidates. Keep the phase's white field, 2D grammar, preservation constraints, and analytical purpose. A targeted single-character Phase-1 ALTER/REFINE/FUSE result uses exact 9:16 vertical white analytical presentation with one dominant true front, one smaller exact profile, and one smaller direct rear of the same frozen identity; it is not expanded into a fifteen-character CREATE. Any explicitly requested different output contract must be resolved before certification.

**Application.** A ten-outfit catalogue may lead to selection of Outfits 02, 06, and 08 for a single fusion. This does not demand a second ten-outfit catalogue. Resolve the composite clothing completely, freeze the fused outfit internally, compile the clean renderer description, pass preflight and the Renderer Capability Gate, and enter READY TO RENDER. Image submission requires an explicit user `RENDER` command.

**Failure.** Treating a targeted operation as fresh random creation, automatically advancing phase, importing later systems into Phase 1, or duplicating a single target to satisfy an unrelated initial count is invalid.
### 4.4 Selection and freeze are separate decisions

**Rule.** A candidate may be selected for analysis or development without being frozen for downstream use. Only a passing resolved system that the user adopts may become a frozen downstream input.

**Explanation.** Technical inspection records whether a result is compliant. User selection records preference. Freeze records adoption and preservation. None implies the end of the phase. Multiple source candidates can remain selected while the user develops a combined result.

**Application.** The user selects three bodies, asks to combine two, retains the third for comparison, and later freezes the preferred composite. The unused originals stay intact. A failed image does not become a certified body merely because some of its features were attractive; an explicitly requested new development must resolve and validate its own blueprint.

**Validation.** Record status separately as RESOLVED, HANDOFF-CERTIFIED, RENDER-PASSED/FAILED, SELECTED, and FROZEN. Never replace these distinct facts with one ambiguous “approved” flag.

### 4.5 ALTER and REFINE transaction — HARD / ABSOLUTE

**Rule.** `ALTER` and `REFINE` create a new revision from an exact existing source while preserving every unaffected defining property. The command targets only the requested fields plus genuine dependencies.

**Procedure.** Identify source ID and revision; record requested changes; inventory protected fields; resolve dependencies; rebuild the affected design systems; re-resolve any dependent concept-integrated intrinsic coverage before final freeze; recheck complete character quality and active phase requirements; freeze the new revision; compile the positive renderer-facing description; run preflight and the Renderer Capability Gate; enter READY TO RENDER. Image submission requires an explicit user `RENDER` command.

The source remains unchanged. The new revision must not silently normalize proportions, lose concept structures, simplify identity, or substitute approximate source geometry. Missing source information is unresolved and must be recovered from active task state rather than invented.

**Validation.** Maintain a changed / preserved / dependency ledger internally. Every changed field traces to the command or an unavoidable consequence. Every identity-bearing unaffected field remains materially present in the new frozen revision.
### 4.6 FUSE transaction — HARD / ABSOLUTE

**Rule.** Character fusion combines explicitly selected existing constructions into one newly authored result. It is distinct from the primary concept fusion that originally creates every body. Outfit fusion and weapon fusion follow the same preservation-aware process within their owning phases.

**Explanation.** Fusion authors one coherent architecture from substantive source contributions. It is not mechanical averaging. Every selected source contributes identifiable structural value while the result receives a newly resolved skeleton, mass balance, topology, attachment logic, material hierarchy, silhouette, and identity integration.

**Procedure.** Inventory defining structures per source; identify user-prioritized contributions; resolve compatibility and conflicts; author one coherent architecture; record each source contribution and any explicitly authorized exclusions; preserve the active primary concept set and exactly two subordinate roles; certify female readability, complete concept fusion, macro hierarchy, source fidelity, material integrity, and character quality; resolve concept-integrated intrinsic coverage as part of the fused body; complete face/head/hair finishing and view consistency; freeze the complete fused specification; compile the Clean Renderer-Facing Description; run preflight and the Renderer Capability Gate; enter READY TO RENDER. Image submission requires an explicit user `RENDER` command.

**Application in Phase 1.** A fusion may combine one body's short deep torso and intrinsic shoulder-to-hip/lower-torso region channel organization with another body's split-rim joint system and long tapered limb architecture. The result requires a newly resolved skeleton, mass balance, joint transitions, and whole-body concept integration. Source components are transformed into one character rather than attached as separate modules.

**Priority.** The prohibition on deriving initial population members from each other applies to independent CREATE candidates. It does not prohibit explicitly requested `FUSE` or `ALTER`. Those operations record source continuity honestly and retain all other phase requirements.

**Sub-concepts.** A fused result retains exactly two subordinate roles: one actual real-world Era / Culture / Civilization vocabulary under §5.10.1 and one additional subordinate concept under §5.10.2–§5.10.3. Reconcile source selections deliberately. Do not accumulate every source subordinate concept into the fused result.

**Validation.** Every source contribution has physical evidence; all required primary concepts remain embodied; unaffected protected properties survive; the fused result has one coherent hierarchy; concept-integrated intrinsic coverage follows the final fused anatomy, remains body-owned and subordinate, and does not become a new silhouette authority.
### 4.7 Comparison space and irreversible attempt outcomes

**Rule.** Keep three distinct records: resolved design solutions, actual inspected output evidence, and accepted frozen systems.

**Explanation.** A failed image is not a canonical character. Its intended blueprint still records a construction already explored, so later initial candidates cannot reuse that blueprint under a different colour. Its actual defects remain evidence of what happened, not a design template. There is no need to invent a passing result to proceed with another authorized sheet.

**Application.** The five certified blueprints use five distinct architectures, but the rendered sheet repeats one architecture across several columns. Record a fidelity failure for that attempt. Preserve the five intended frozen designs and the failed image as separate records; do not treat duplicated rendered bodies as accepted characters or silently redesign the frozen specifications.

**Validation.** Every comparison record states whether evidence comes from a blueprint or an inspected image. Never describe planned visual differences as already observed image facts.

### 4.8 Primary selector / wildcard resolution transaction — HARD / ABSOLUTE

**Rule.** For initial Phase-1 CREATE, slot resolution is a mandatory transaction between input parsing and all concept interpretation whenever the Primary Input Specification contains a selector-family slot or wildcard slot. Fixed-only input passes through trivially.

**Procedure per candidate.**
1. Parse every primary slot as canonical fixed atomic, open literal fixed, selector-family, `X`, or `X{TYPE}`.
2. Match user-fixed concepts against `Concepts.md`. Canonical matches use their SC entries; unmatched user-fixed concepts create TLCRs instead of failing.
3. For every non-fixed slot, resolve its canonical pool under Section 12 before any concept decomposition or body authorship.
4. For a selector-family slot or `X{TYPE}`, use the branch/sub-branch/alias/intent precedence in §12.3. For `X`, use the complete eligible canonical SC registry.
5. Recursively collect descendant canonical leaves, deduplicate by SC ID, apply only explicit governing eligibility constraints, and draw uniformly from the resulting pool.
6. Resolve each candidate independently. Sampling is with replacement across candidates; repeated leaves or repeated complete resolved combinations are permitted.
7. Verify only that each candidate's own resolved set is internally coherent and that duplicate primary identities inside one candidate occur only when the user explicitly supplied that duplication with meaningful intent.
8. Record the Resolution Ledger and freeze every candidate's concrete Resolved Primary Concept Set.
9. Only then begin recognizable-vocabulary extraction, decomposition, pairwise/higher-order fusion, and independent body authorship.

**Resolution Ledger.** Record candidate, original slot expression, interpretation route, canonical pool or fixed/TLCR identity, any explicit eligibility constraint, draw method, resolved leaf, and freeze state. The ledger is internal and is excluded from renderer-facing text.

**No diversity weighting.** Population diversity does not alter selector probabilities. Do not prefer different families or leaves, exhaust branch members before repetition, ban duplicate resolved combinations, reroll a valid but difficult draw, or choose an aesthetically easier leaf. Body diversity is created through independent authorship and later collision review, not through biased concept selection.

**No post-resolution escape.** After freeze, a weak body solution is repaired under the same resolved inputs. A resolved selector result is not silently exchanged for another leaf to improve appearance or differentiation. Reopening requires explicit user authorization under the normal state rules.

**Fixed-slot dominance.** User-supplied fixed concepts—canonical or open-literal—are never replaced, diluted into a branch, or treated as preferences. Autonomous slots adapt around them.

**Failure.** Non-uniform authored selection presented as random; branch-depth or novelty weighting; population-diversity rerolls; silent pool widening; a non-selectable container/variant/mechanism materializing as the result; unrecorded resolution; late concept swapping; or bypassing an unresolved ambiguity fails the transaction.

## 5 PHASE 1 — BODY CREATION

### 5.1 Purpose and output contract

#### 5.1.1 Purpose

Phase 1 establishes the body and foundational character identity at premium gacha/anime/manhwa quality through a **character-specific character-reference presentation**. The body must already create a strong named-character impression through proportions, silhouette, face, hair, materials, colour, concept fusion, and identity finishing before later phases elevate it.

#### 5.1.1A Character creation is the primary objective — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Phase 1 is not a concept diagram, morphology exercise, material demonstration, creature sheet, or compliance mannequin. Its primary output is a set of compelling independent female characters whose concept fusion is inseparable from their identity.

Every candidate must succeed simultaneously as:

- a complete premium female anime/manhwa character;
- an independently authored body and proportion regime;
- a deliberately authored face, eye system, hair architecture, and head silhouette;
- a materially coherent body with resolved substrate and transitions;
- a complete embodiment of the supplied primary concept set;
- a distinct Concept-Fusion Architecture rather than a variant of another candidate;
- a memorable main-character-grade visual identity before outfit or weapon creation begins.

Character appeal, face/head/hair authorship, body architecture, concept embodiment, material hierarchy, silhouette, colour/value design, and aesthetic finishing are coequal requirements. A technically correct fusion with a generic character fails. A beautiful generic character with concepts added as decorations fails. A radical concept body that stops reading as a female character fails.

Before freeze, conduct the full alternative-design search, aesthetic/taste rejection, four-pass refinement, weakest-candidate comparison, and render-robustness process defined later in Phase 1. The final candidate must be the strongest resolved character, not merely the first structurally valid one.

#### 5.1.2 Mandatory population

Every initial Broad Phase-1 concept command creates exactly **fifteen independently authored female identities** as one population, partitioned into three five-character Phase-1 analytical sheets.

Every identity receives exactly three full-body depictions of the same frozen character: one dominant uncropped true-front neutral-standing view, one smaller exact 90-degree profile, and one smaller direct-rear neutral-standing view. All three are mandatory; compose them without materially reducing the analytical scale, body readability, or detail quality of the five principal fronts on each sheet.

#### 5.1.3 Format — HARD

Every initial Phase-1 population sheet is:

> **ONE CONTINUOUS 16:9 LANDSCAPE IMAGE**

with:

> **FIVE LEFT-TO-RIGHT CHARACTER REGIONS**

The five principal full-body fronts are the dominant information. No portrait-card collage, non-analytical hierarchy, decorative panel system, scene, or character-over-character overlap may reduce analytical readability.

#### 5.1.4 View architecture — HARD

The mandatory initial-view architecture is:

1. **ONE dominant true-front full-body view per character** — directly facing the viewer, neutral standing, complete extents visible.
2. **MANDATORY smaller exact 90-degree profile and direct rear** — subordinate analytical reconstruction views that must fit while the principal front remains large and analytically useful.

Each mandatory subordinate view depicts the same frozen body and remains neutral. It may not become a second front, a three-quarter view, a pose, an alternate design, or a substitute for a poorly readable principal front.

An initial sheet may not contain fronts only. It must contain the five dominant fronts plus five exact profiles and five direct rears of the same frozen identities.

#### 5.1.5 Neutral standing — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Every principal Phase-1 view is strictly neutral and standing:

- upright and naturally balanced;
- head and facial plane directed forward;
- torso axis directly frontal;
- shoulders not intentionally pose-shifted;
- hip/lower-torso orientation not intentionally pose-shifted;
- arms resting naturally with only enough separation for body inspection;
- hands relaxed;
- legs supporting the body without crossing or theatrical weight shift;
- no raised arm;
- no hand behind the head;
- no contrapposto;
- no hip-cocked glamour stance;
- no walking, crouching, kneeling, sitting, floating, combat action, or character-defining gesture.

Pose is never a differentiation mechanism. Static asymmetry belongs to anatomy and design.

#### 5.1.5A Character-reference presentation — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Phase 1 presents each completed frozen body as a **clean three-view female character-reference design**, not a fashion illustration. Presentation exists to make frozen identity, silhouette, proportions, material systems, topology, appendage roots, mandatory major morphology, and concept-fusion structures easy to inspect.

The presentation contract is:

> **FEMALE CHARACTER IDENTITY → STRICT NEUTRAL STANDING → ONE DOMINANT TRUE FRONT + ONE SMALLER EXACT 90-DEGREE PROFILE + ONE SMALLER DIRECT REAR → SAME FROZEN BODY → EVEN REFERENCE LIGHTING → PLAIN WHITE FIELD**

Hair, horns, wings, tails, scales, intrinsic plates, membranes, unusual substrates, apertures, markings, body-bound detached identity structures, and every other frozen identity system remain present whenever they belong to the resolved character. The front remains visually dominant; profile/rear provide analytical reconstruction evidence only and never become alternate designs.
#### 5.1.5B Concept-integrated intrinsic surface architecture — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Phase-1 presentation should be carried by the character's own concept-derived surface architecture whenever possible. The character is not dressed into identity. Scales, transformed skin/substrate, mineral or organic planes, membranes, feathers, fur, crystalline growth, intrinsic plates, layered body matter, topology-derived bridges, or other concept-authorized constituents follow the body and remain visibly anatomical.

Resolve, for every character:

- anatomical origin and continuity with existing body matter;
- contour and curvature tracking across the character's exact form;
- thickness and added volume;
- segmentation, spacing, density, opacity/translucency, and texture where applicable;
- transition into neighboring body-owned matter;
- relationship to appendage roots, cavities, channels, joints, and fusion structures;
- contribution to the concept combination rather than merely presentation;
- preservation of torso, waist, hip/lower-torso, limb, and joint readability;
- distinction from clothing architecture.

**Body-derived surface priority — HARD.** Intrinsic concept-derived matter remains the identity-bearing body construction and is also the sole permitted Phase-1 chest/lower-torso presentation coverage. It may never be replaced by garment logic, a studio/reference layer, a neutral underlayer, or any other separate presentation system.

**Reference-surface completeness — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** The frozen Phase-1 design includes complete presentation-ready surface construction across the chest and lower torso achieved solely through intrinsic concept-derived constituent matter. Use opaque, continuous concept-derived body material that follows the character's actual form and material logic without becoming a garment silhouette. Do not leave those regions dependent on unspecified presentation state or on later Phase-2 costume coverage. If the concept-derived anatomy cannot provide a coherent presentation surface by itself, return to body-materialization/design resolution and fix the design rather than adding a separate underlayer. Renderer serialization states the resulting positive intrinsic material/surface construction directly.

**No separate analytical underlayer — HARD / ABSOLUTE.** A separate Phase-1 studio/reference layer, neutral underlayer, or fallback garment-analogue is not used. Coverage across the chest and lower torso must be solved through intrinsic concept-derived body materialization only. If a candidate cannot satisfy presentation readiness through intrinsic body construction alone, the design fails and returns to body-materialization/design resolution. Renderer-facing text states the intrinsic construction directly rather than introducing a shared coverage garment or analytical underlayer.


**Garment-resemblance failure test.** A Phase-1 result fails presentation if its supposed intrinsic surface architecture reads as a designed costume through hems, sleeves, bodice/skirt organization, decorative straps, jewelry-like chains, detachable drape, cape-like masses, or other wearable construction rather than as constituent anatomy. Cloth-like or fibrous body matter is still allowed when it has anatomical continuity and no wearable seam/closure logic.

##### Renderer translation — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The Clean Renderer-Facing Description states the already-frozen visible body construction directly. Do not ask the renderer to invent `coverage`, fashion, or a costume. Describe material, region, contour, continuity, thickness, segmentation, texture, opacity/translucency, attachment, and transition.

Do not serialize any shared studio/reference layer, neutral underlayer, or fallback garment-analogue. Instead, state once per initial sheet in the sheet-local FINAL RENDER CONTEXT that Phase 1 uses only intrinsic concept-derived body surface construction for chest/lower-torso presentation coverage, then serialize the five character payloads through their actual body construction.

#### 5.1.6 Presentation hierarchy — HARD

The five dominant true-front bodies are the principal references. They receive the maximum useful pixel area and must remain large enough for direct analysis of body regime, face/head/hair, mandatory morphology, materials, topology, appendage roots, and cross-concept structures.

The mandatory smaller exact-profile/direct-rear views are secondary reconstruction evidence only. They never justify shrinking, cropping, simplifying, or posing the principal fronts.

#### 5.1.7 Exact allocation and pixel efficiency — HARD / ABSOLUTE

**Rule.** The Phase-1 canvas is exactly 16:9 with five left-to-right character regions on one continuous plain-white field. There are no drawn separators, card backgrounds, frames, decorative column boundaries, labels, or information modules competing with the bodies.

Equal-width regions are the default. Width may be redistributed modestly to fit actual wings, tails, horns, or other extents while keeping all five identities clearly owned and equally legible. The principal front of each character receives the largest practical scale.

**Full extent.** Include head, hair, hands, feet, wings, tails, horns, body-bound detached identity structures, and every other designed projection. Do not crop or delete a mandatory structure to fit. Do not let one character's structure visually merge with a neighbor.

**Failure.** A 3:2 image, non-analytical hierarchy, overlapping characters, cropped wings, tiny front views caused by unnecessary subviews, decorative captions, or a dynamic-pose lineup fails the presentation contract.

#### 5.1.8 Mandatory supplementary-view evidence — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Exact profile and direct-rear geometry are mandatory parts of every initial Phase-1 sheet and remain part of the internal Frozen Character Specification.

For every identity:

- profile is exactly 90 degrees;
- rear is directly rear-facing;
- both depict the same frozen body as the dominant front;
- all three remain neutral standing;
- mandatory morphology keeps the same count, origin, geometry, material, topology, asymmetry, and proportions;
- body-bound detached identity structures retain the same body-local relation;
- no alternate design, new anatomy, pose, or three-quarter substitution appears.

Omission of either required subordinate view is a presentation failure. It does not erase or redesign the frozen character state.
### 5.2 Character identity

#### 5.2.1 Female-character identity preservation — ABSOLUTE / HARD

Every Phase-1 solution must satisfy **female anime/manhwa character identity and complete concept fusion simultaneously as co-equal hard requirements**. Neither requirement is subordinate to the other. The active concepts may radically transform anatomy, proportions, skeletal/topological organization, body substrate, appendages, cavities, surface architecture, and silhouette, but they MUST NOT erase the underlying classification as a female character.

This is a structural identity lock, not a request for conventional anatomy. The body must remain simultaneously readable as a deliberate female character through the integrated combination of:

- female head/face construction;
- female torso and chest architecture as a structural body system;
- waist and hip/lower-torso architecture;
- coherent female-character limb, hand, and foot construction;
- character-scale head/body relationship;
- overall female character silhouette.

Extreme concept deformation is permitted inside this identity boundary. The following are specifically permitted when materially required by the concepts:

- extreme height differences;
- extreme but coherent body proportions;
- radically altered skeletal/topological architecture;
- unusual torso/chest construction;
- unusual waist/hip/lower-torso geometry;
- creature-derived morphology integrated into the body;
- wings, tails, horns, scales, fins, apertures, cavities, channels, branching structures, or other appendages/body systems;
- nonstandard substrates and materially integrated surface structures;
- strongly asymmetric anatomy;
- nonstandard silhouettes.

The concepts MUST transform the female character; they MUST NOT replace the female character.

The following solutions are invalid and MUST be rejected:

- animal-first or creature-first constructions in which the female character has effectively become the supplied animal/creature;
- mascot-like or species-avatar constructions;
- object-first constructions in which the character has become an abstract object or spatial artifact;
- amorphous or mass-like constructions that no longer read as a female character;
- vaguely human constructions whose gender classification is materially ambiguous;
- abstract forms in which the female character identity survives only through a face pasted onto a non-character structure;
- concept solutions that use the supplied creature/object/phenomenon as a replacement body instead of transforming a female character body.

**Character-first test:** remove all concept labels, semantic descriptors, external presentation, and cosmetic styling. The remaining body must still read unambiguously as a female anime/manhwa character.

**Concept-within-character test:** after the character-first test passes, the complete supplied concept set must still be materially legible through the body. Neither test may be satisfied at the expense of the other.

**Identity-loss rule:** if concept fusion makes the result animal-first, creature-first, object-first, abstract-first, amorphous, mascot-like, or gender-ambiguous, the blueprint FAILS regardless of how strong the concept materialization is. Rebuild the body while preserving the certified concepts.

#### 5.2.2 POPULATION QUALITY MUST NOT DECREASE FROM LEFT TO RIGHT — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Character 01, 02, 03, 04, and 05 each receive the same design and rendering budget in principle.

No character may become a simplification merely because it occupies a later column.

No character may become more generic because its concept fusion is difficult.

No character may become lower detail because the renderer is running out of space.

No character may become less developed because the first character already consumed visual attention.

If the page cannot preserve equal quality, the page architecture or internal design must be rebuilt before generation.

#### 5.2.3 UNIVERSAL FEMALE-CHARACTER EXAMPLE — REQUIRED

For every supplied concept set, the output remains a female anime/manhwa character.

Concept fusion may transform:

- proportions;
- topology;
- substrate;
- appendages;
- silhouette;
- internal structures;
- materials;
- face;
- hair;
- eyes.

The result must remain immediately recognizable as a female character rather than becoming a literal animal, abstract object, amorphous mass, or generic non-character shape.

The extreme nature of the concept fusion does not remove the character identity.

### 5.3 Concept decomposition and anatomical body embodiment domain

#### 5.3.1 Universal materialization axiom — HARD

Every supplied concept, regardless of category, MUST materially construct the character's body.

There is no “primary concept” exception. There is no lesser embodiment tier. A concept is either materially embodied in the body or the design fails.

The concept must alter what the character physically is.

The compiler must translate each concept into concrete consequences across applicable physical systems:

- geometry;
- proportion;
- mass;
- skeleton;
- joints;
- musculature;
- topology;
- continuity/discontinuity;
- cavities;
- internal architecture;
- material/substrate;
- appendages;
- attachment;
- segmentation;
- branching;
- recursion;
- compression/expansion;
- displacement;
- asymmetry;
- silhouette.

#### 5.3.2 No realism normalization — HARD

Realism, ordinary anatomy, conventional species anatomy, familiar fantasy anatomy, customary proportions, biological expectation, and common design practice have **no authority to weaken concept materialization**.

If the strongest faithful physical solution requires an extreme, impossible, hollow, expanded, compressed, duplicated, substituted, discontinuous, radically asymmetric, or otherwise non-standard body construction, that construction is permitted and MUST be considered.

“Unrealistic,” “unusual,” “not normally anatomical,” “not typical for the species,” or “difficult to render” are not valid reasons to normalize a design.

Phase, presentation, coherence, and readability constraints remain binding.

#### 5.3.3 Causal propagation — HARD

For every supplied concept, the compiler must **analyze** all relevant physical and identity channels without turning that analysis into a requirement to visibly occupy every channel.

The analysis considers, where applicable:

- body substrate and constituent matter;
- body architecture and proportions;
- mass distribution;
- skeleton and joints;
- musculature or soft-tissue organization;
- topology, continuity/discontinuity, cavities, channels, or openings;
- appendages and their roots;
- segmentation, branching, compression/expansion, displacement, or other structural behavior;
- face/head, eyes, and hair where the concept can materially affect them;
- material and surface behavior;
- asymmetry;
- silhouette;
- interaction with every other supplied concept;
- complete-set higher-order interaction when three or more concepts are supplied.

Only the channels that produce the strongest coherent character solution are instantiated. A substrate, cavity, appendage, proportion change, or other system may be legitimately absent when the concept does not require it and the complete fusion remains unmistakable.

What is mandatory is:

1. the concept's complete **major recognizable vocabulary** as classified for the active interpretation;
2. unmistakable material/physical embodiment rather than a label, colour token, or external effect;
3. a genuine physical interaction with every other supplied primary concept;
4. participation in the complete-set higher-order fusion when three or more concepts are supplied;
5. integration into the one authored character rather than an isolated concept island.

The compiler must not add extra carriers merely because an unused channel exists. Causal analysis is exhaustive; visible saturation is not.

#### 5.3.4 Multi-concept fusion — HARD

Multiple concepts are resolved as **one physical-design problem before body construction**.

Every pair of concepts must materially alter shared structures. For three or more concepts, complete higher-order fusion is mandatory.

The accepted construction is:

> **ONE BODY SUBSTRATE + SHARED STRUCTURES JOINTLY CAUSED BY THE COMPLETE CONCEPT SET + CONCEPT-SPECIFIC BUT INTEGRATED STRUCTURES**

The rejected construction is:

> **ORDINARY BODY + FEATURE A + FEATURE B + FEATURE C**

#### 5.3.5 Fusion structures

Every required concept interaction must produce a **real visible physical consequence** in the character.

For every primary-concept pair, resolve at least one genuine shared consequence whose geometry, topology, material, attachment, proportion, surface organization, identity system, or silhouette would be meaningfully different if either concept were removed.

For three or more concepts, resolve at least one unmistakable higher-order construction whose final state depends on the complete supplied set. More than one may be used when it strengthens the character, but there is no fixed numerical quota of major structures per pair or per complete set.

One integrated structure may legitimately carry several pairwise and higher-order interactions when those dependencies are actually visible and structurally meaningful. The workflow must not manufacture one appendage, cavity, material region, or decorative object per interaction merely to fill an inventory.

At least the important interactions must remain readable at macro or strong meso scale. Micro-detail may reinforce them but cannot be the only proof.

The required question is not only “Where is A, B, and C?” but also:

- How has A physically changed B?
- How has B physically changed C?
- How has C physically changed A?
- What exists specifically because the complete set is simultaneously present?

The exact shared structures are independently authored for every character.

#### 5.3.6 Embodiment verification — HARD / ABSOLUTE

This is the authoritative Phase-1 embodiment rule. Its detailed verification protocol is defined immediately below and its validation application is defined in Section 5.17. Verification is performed directly on the character body and its required reference viewpoints through body-origin and concept-fusion criteria.

For every character, verify directly that the Phase-1 display contains only the resolved female character body, its body-owned concept-integrated intrinsic structures, and its required reference views, and that the complete supplied concept combination is the causal origin of that body.

The body must embody the complete combination with unmistakable material presence and genuine required interactions across the applicable anatomy, proportions, topology, substrate, materials, appendages, apertures, face, eyes, hair, identity systems, asymmetry, and silhouette while remaining unambiguously readable as a female character. Complete embodiment does not require equal intensity in every system.

#### 5.3.7 Concept visibility — HARD

Every supplied concept must remain unmistakably present from the body alone.

Concept visibility is judged through the combination of:

- the complete major recognizable vocabulary classified for that concept;
- one or more strong material, morphological, topological, proportional, facial/head/hair, or other body-level manifestations as appropriate;
- visible pairwise interaction evidence;
- complete-set interaction evidence for three or more concepts;
- integration with the character's overall construction and silhouette.

There is **no fixed requirement** for one major signature plus one secondary signature plus one additional support system. A single exceptionally strong integrated system may carry several of those functions. Additional supporting manifestations are used only when they strengthen recognizability, fusion, material identity, composition, or character appeal.

Micro-detail may reinforce concept identity but cannot be the sole reason a primary concept can be detected.

Concrete concepts retain the major characteristics necessary to their immediate visual identity. Abstract concepts require an explicitly authored physical interpretation with visible consequences; the workflow must not fabricate a claim that arbitrary anatomy has universally recognizable semantic meaning.

The character design must carry strong, traceable evidence of the complete supplied combination without labels, independent props, environment, emitted/surrounding phenomena, or explanatory presentation. Certified body-bound detached identity morphology remains part of the character design.

#### 5.3.8 Operational interpretation of anatomical body concept materialization — HARD / ABSOLUTE

This subsection is an executable interpretation layer. It exists to prevent the renderer, compiler, validator, or operator from treating a concept as a decorative theme surrounding an already-resolved character.

The complete supplied concept set is the physical-design input to the character. The character body is the output of that fused input. The workflow MUST therefore resolve the body as a consequence of the concepts rather than resolve a generic female body first and subsequently attach concept-specific features.

The required causal direction is:

> **SUPPLIED CONCEPT SET → PHYSICAL PROPERTY ANALYSIS → CONCEPT INTERACTION ANALYSIS → FUSED BODY CONSEQUENCES → COMPLETE BODY ARCHITECTURE → RENDERER SERIALIZATION**

The following causal direction is invalid:

> **GENERIC FEMALE BODY → CONCEPT FEATURE ASSIGNMENT → COSMETIC MODIFICATION → INDEPENDENT EXTERNAL PRESENTATION**

The invalid direction remains invalid even when the resulting image is attractive, highly detailed, technically polished, or superficially recognizable as all supplied concepts.

##### 5.3.8.1 The character body and certified body-bound morphology are the sole conceptual carrier in Phase 1 — HARD

In Phase 1, the supplied concepts must be materially encoded by the female character itself. The principal carrier is her body: anatomy, topology, constituent matter, proportions, appendages, surface architecture, face/head/hair consequences, and silhouette. A spatially separated structure is eligible only when it passes the body-bound detached identity-structure rule in §0.0 and therefore remains fixed character morphology rather than an independent object or surrounding effect.

The permitted conceptual carrier domain includes:

- skeletal architecture;
- cranial architecture;
- facial geometry;
- ocular structures;
- hair architecture;
- neck construction;
- shoulder and clavicle construction;
- ribcage architecture;
- chest architecture;
- torso geometry;
- waist geometry;
- hip/lower-torso region and hip architecture;
- limb geometry;
- joint construction;
- hands and feet;
- musculature;
- body mass distribution;
- internal topology;
- cavities;
- apertures;
- openings;
- discontinuities;
- integrated surface structures;
- body substrate;
- material transitions;
- appendages;
- appendage roots;
- attachment structures;
- certified body-bound detached identity morphology;
- integrated markings;
- asymmetry;
- silhouette;
- other directly observable physical structures of the same character design.

The concept implementation must remain attributable to the character when labels, environment, independent props, emitted phenomena, and narrative explanation are removed. A body-bound detached structure remains attributable because its owner, body-local anchor, scale, placement, orientation, and morphological role are fixed by the character specification.

##### 5.3.8.2 Embodiment verification — detailed body-origin protocol — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The authoritative question is whether the Phase-1 body itself is the complete physical materialization of the supplied concept combination and whether the sheet contains only that resolved character, her certified body-owned and body-bound identity structures, the mandatory principal front and the mandatory exact-profile/direct-rear analytical support views required by the active initial Phase-1 presentation.

For every character, verify before renderer-description materialization:

1. the complete body architecture is resolved;
2. the complete supplied concept combination is the causal origin of that body architecture;
3. every supplied concept materially contributes to the construction;
4. every concept's classified major recognizable vocabulary is preserved;
5. every primary-concept pair has a genuine shared physical consequence;
6. the complete set has an unmistakable higher-order shared consequence when three or more concepts are supplied;
7. visible concept manifestations are sufficient for immediate recognition without relying on maximum saturation or uniform distribution;
8. the complete combination remains identifiable from the body itself;
9. the body is a unique solution to the complete combination rather than a pre-existing body receiving additions;
10. the body remains unmistakably a female anime/manhwa character;
11. the mandatory principal front, exact profile, and direct rear all depict that same resolved body and body-bound identity morphology;
12. no independent prop, environment, symbol, emitted phenomenon, or other separate presentation carrier is required to explain, complete, or communicate the concept combination;
13. quiet regions and restrained anatomy are permitted when the required vocabulary and interactions are already complete and the resulting character is aesthetically stronger.

The correct causal model is:

> **COMPLETE SUPPLIED CONCEPT COMBINATION → COMBINED PHYSICAL CONSEQUENCES → UNIQUE COMPLETE FEMALE CHARACTER BODY**

The verification does not require every possible body system to be altered. It requires the body as a whole to have been authored under the fusion and the visible manifestations to be complete, unmistakable, physically integrated, and aesthetically controlled.

If embodiment verification fails, return to concept decomposition and complete-fusion resolution before handoff. Do not repair missing embodiment through presentation effects or renderer improvisation.

##### 5.3.8.3 Combination analysis must demonstrate shared construction — HARD

For a multi-concept command, do not merely verify that every individual concept is visible. Verify that the body contains structures whose final form is caused by the interaction of concepts.

The validator must be able to identify:

- concept-specific physical consequences;
- pairwise physical consequences;
- higher-order physical consequences;
- whole-body consequences of the complete combination.

A valid fused structure cannot be completely explained by one concept alone.

For three or more concepts, the body must contain higher-order structures whose final geometry, topology, material, proportion, morphology, placement, or silhouette depends on the interaction of the complete concept set.

##### 5.3.8.4 Generalized operational example: separate feature assignment — INVALID

Consider an arbitrary command:

`CONCEPT A × CONCEPT B × CONCEPT C`

The following construction is invalid:

> ordinary female body + A-derived appendage + B-derived appendage + C-derived marking.

It remains invalid if the three features are large, beautifully rendered, asymmetrical, highly detailed, or visually distinctive.

It is also invalid if the renderer changes the body proportions slightly while retaining the same concept-to-feature assignment.

The reason is structural: the concepts remain partitioned into independent feature carriers instead of jointly constructing the body.

##### 5.3.8.5 Generalized operational example: fused body construction — REQUIRED

For the same arbitrary command:

`CONCEPT A × CONCEPT B × CONCEPT C`

first determine the physical properties contributed by A, B, and C.

Then determine how A changes the physical consequences of B, how B changes the physical consequences of C, how C changes the physical consequences of A, and how all three jointly alter the resulting body.

The final body may therefore contain altered:

- proportions;
- torso architecture;
- chest geometry;
- hip/lower-torso geometry;
- limb construction;
- skeletal topology;
- internal cavities;
- substrate;
- appendage roots;
- appendage morphology;
- hair;
- eyes;
- surface structures;
- material transitions;
- asymmetry;
- silhouette.

The exact implementation is resolved independently for each character.

The body must read as one unified physical solution to A × B × C.

##### 5.3.8.6 Generalized operational example: external phenomenon substitution — INVALID

If a concept has a physical association with a phenomenon that could conventionally be drawn around a character, rendering that phenomenon around the character does not constitute embodiment.

The concept must instead be translated into physical consequences inside the character's construction.

The required reasoning is:

> **CONCEPT → PHYSICAL PROPERTY → BODY CONSEQUENCE**

**INVALID:**

> **CONCEPT → EXTERNAL PHENOMENON → CHARACTER PLACED INSIDE PHENOMENON**

##### 5.3.8.7 Generalized operational example: appendage assignment — INVALID

For a creature-derived or morphology-derived concept, repeatedly assigning one familiar appendage to that concept across every character is insufficient differentiation.

The population must instead resolve different structural manifestations through the complete fusion problem. An appendage may differ in:

- number;
- origin;
- attachment depth;
- orientation;
- length;
- thickness;
- branching;
- geometry;
- articulation;
- material;
- integration with the axial body;
- interaction with another concept;
- contribution to silhouette;
- symmetry/asymmetry.

The appendage architecture must be a consequence of the complete concept combination rather than a default feature associated with the raw concept label.

##### 5.3.8.8 Generalized operational example: cavity/opening materialization — REQUIRED

When the physical properties of a supplied concept support internal discontinuity, penetration, absence, separation, void, rupture, aperture, cavity, or another related structural condition, the compiler must consider direct body-level topology that materially expresses that property.

A detached visual representation of the condition does not substitute for body topology.

The final implementation may use internal architecture, cavities, apertures, openings, discontinuities, altered structural boundaries, negative spaces, or other directly body-integrated mechanisms when those mechanisms are physically supported by the resolved concept combination.

##### 5.3.8.9 Generalized operational example: materialization through the body substrate — REQUIRED

A material concept must not be reduced to a surface colour or superficial coating.

The compiler must determine whether the material changes:

- body substrate;
- surface structure;
- internal structure;
- structural density;
- translucency;
- opacity;
- reflectivity;
- continuity;
- segmentation;
- attachment;
- topology;
- material boundaries;
- silhouette;
- relationships between anatomical systems.

A conventional body carrying a material-like surface treatment is insufficient whenever the supplied concept supports deeper physical embodiment.

##### 5.3.8.10 The concepts remain individually visible without becoming separate systems — HARD

Fusion does not mean that a concept may disappear into an indistinguishable mixture.

Every supplied concept must remain highly visible in the final body.

However, visibility must arise through integrated physical structures and interactions rather than through separate concept islands.

The required state is:

> **EACH CONCEPT HIGHLY VISIBLE + EACH CONCEPT PHYSICALLY CONSEQUENT + PAIRWISE INTERACTION VISIBLE + HIGHER-ORDER INTERACTION VISIBLE + ONE INDIVISIBLE BODY**

The following state is invalid:

> **CONCEPT A VISIBLE IN ONE REGION + CONCEPT B VISIBLE IN ANOTHER REGION + CONCEPT C VISIBLE IN ANOTHER REGION**

##### 5.3.8.11 The body must identify the complete combination — HARD

A viewer examining only the resolved body must be able to determine the supplied concept combination from its physical construction.

This requirement remains active after removal of:

- labels;
- concept names;
- environmental context;
- effects;
- props;
- external presentation;
- pose-based cues;
- expression-based cues;
- colour-only cues;
- cosmetic cues;
- micro-detail.

The remaining macro and structural body information must still carry the combination.

#### 5.3.9 Deliberate body-mass diversity and no lazy differentiation — HARD

Character Creation MUST NOT use accidental, crude, caricatural, or mechanically inflated bulk as a shortcut for differentiation. It likewise MUST NOT use caricaturally compressed proportions, mascot proportions, spherical placeholder torsos, undeveloped swollen limbs, or amorphous mass merely to occupy a population slot.

This is **not** a prohibition on broad, muscular, powerful, heavy-framed, soft, full-figured, thick-limbed, broad-shouldered, or otherwise high-mass female body regimes. Those are valid and important parts of the design space when deliberately authored, proportionally coherent, aesthetically strong, and integrated with the complete concept fusion.

Short characters remain fully proportioned, intentionally compact, and main-character-grade. Tall characters remain proportionally coherent rather than simple vertical stretches. Broad characters require coherent changes across skeleton, ribcage, shoulders, waist, hip/lower-torso region, limbs, mass distribution, face/head relationship, and appendage scale rather than merely widening a standard torso.

The valid variation space remains extremely broad through:

- very short through extremely tall stature;
- compact through elongated skeletal architecture;
- narrow through very broad shoulder and ribcage regimes;
- shallow through deep ribcage architecture;
- petite, athletic, muscular, soft, curvy, full-figured, powerful, broad, and other deliberately resolved mass distributions;
- different chest architecture and projection;
- different waist construction;
- different hip/lower-torso region and hip geometry;
- different limb length, thickness, taper, and joint placement;
- different musculature and skeletal prominence;
- radically different internal topology;
- radically different appendage integration;
- controlled extreme silhouettes.

The body must remain **structurally intentional, anatomically legible in the chosen stylized anatomy, aesthetically successful, and character-grade** at every extreme.

#### 5.3.10 Macro character integrity — HARD

Every body must read as a deliberate finished character at thumbnail and full-body scale before micro-detail is inspected.

Micro-detail MUST NOT be used to conceal weak anatomy, weak silhouette, generic torso construction, indistinct limbs, unresolved appendage roots, repeated body architecture, accidental bulk, or accidental caricaturally compressed proportions.

#### 5.3.11 High-deformation / non-tame concept materialization — HARD

The workflow must not retreat to a generic body plus concept ornaments merely because the supplied concepts are difficult. It must actively consider substantial transformation when that is the strongest faithful answer.

At the same time, **unusualness is not a quota**. The workflow must not mutate anatomy merely to prove that it can. A largely conventional or elegant female anatomy may remain when the concepts are already unmistakably and materially fused through substrate, topology, hair, face/head, appendages, internal structures, silhouette, or other genuinely consequential systems and when further mutation would make the character worse.

The valid design space therefore includes both restrained and extreme solutions. The compiler should consider axial structure, torso/ribcage/chest geometry, skeleton, limbs, substrate, topology, cavities/openings, appendage roots, appendage morphology, face/head, hair, silhouette, and material boundaries, then select the combination that produces the strongest complete character. Even at high intensity, the target remains a female character. Unless the user explicitly requests a full creature/species transformation, do not let a morphology concept replace the entire body with a near-total animal/monster template plus a female face. Preserve an independently authored female/human-readable anatomical foundation and decide deliberately which systems are transformed, how far, and in different proportions across the five characters.

A character fails this gate when its underlying construction is generic **because the concepts were left as superficial ornaments**. A character does not fail merely because some anatomical regions remain conventional or quiet after the complete concept problem has already been solved convincingly.

**CONCEPT CO-DOMINANCE IS MANDATORY:** no supplied primary concept may collapse into a minor accent while another carries nearly all of the physical identity. Co-dominance means equal necessity to the fused construction, not equal surface area or equal numbers of visible manifestations.

For three or more concepts, at least one unmistakable higher-order structure must exist whose final form depends on the complete set. Additional structural propagation is used only when it strengthens completeness, readability, material identity, composition, or premium character appeal.

The strongest character is preferred over the most mutated character.
#### 5.3.11A MAXIMAL-INTENSITY-FIRST SEARCH AND MINIMUM EMBODIMENT FLOOR — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The initial design search starts **above** the minimum recognizability threshold. For every primary concept, first explore the strongest coherent whole-character embodiment that preserves the complete major recognizable vocabulary and makes the concept unmistakable through macro or strong-meso construction. Only after that high-intensity solution exists may refinement simplify redundant carriers.

The process is:

> **FULL MAJOR VOCABULARY → HIGH-INTENSITY BODY CONSEQUENCES → PAIRWISE SYMBIOSIS → COMPLETE-SET SYMBIOSIS → CHARACTER QUALITY PASS → REMOVE ONLY REDUNDANCY**

Never use the opposite process of starting from a generic character and adding the fewest possible cues.

The final character has a hard minimum embodiment floor:

- every primary concept remains immediately readable from the character itself at ordinary sheet scale;
- every classified major characteristic remains present;
- every primary concept changes at least one major or strong-meso body system;
- every required pairwise interaction changes real geometry, material, topology, proportion, attachment, appendage architecture, or another consequential physical system;
- for three or more concepts, at least one of the character's principal visual/structural hooks is a complete-set higher-order construction;
- no primary concept is carried only by palette, markings, micro-detail, pose, expression, a separate coverage surrogate, a symbolic object, or an emitted phenomenon.

If aesthetic simplification would push any concept below this floor, simplify another region instead.

#### 5.3.11B SYMBIOTIC FUSION PROMINENCE AND COUNTERFACTUAL DEPENDENCY — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Concept interaction must be visually consequential, not merely provable in prose. The final body contains shared structures whose geometry would be materially different if any contributing concept were removed.

For each required pair and for the complete set:

1. identify the shared physical construction;
2. state what each contributing concept changes in that construction;
3. verify that the result is not merely one concept's structure with another concept's colour/texture applied;
4. verify that the structure contributes materially to the character's normal-scale body read;
5. perform the counterfactual test: remove one contributing concept mentally and confirm that the construction must change in geometry, topology, material organization, proportion, attachment, or morphology.

If the same structure could remain essentially unchanged after removing one concept, the interaction is weak and must be rebuilt. Clean role partition is also a failure state. If one concept is doing almost all species/morphology work, another is doing almost all material work, and another is doing almost all auxiliary mass or surface work without deeply changing the same shared structures, the fusion remains compartmentalized and must be rebuilt until the concepts materially deform one another inside the body.

The complete-set higher-order construction should be one of the character's major identity hooks. It does not need to dominate the entire silhouette, but it cannot be a hidden technicality or micro-detail.
#### 5.3.12 Character-body carrier integrity lock — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Phase-1 concept materialization is judged directly on the character body and its certified body-bound identity morphology. The complete supplied concept combination must physically construct the resolved female character; no independent symbol, prop, scene, or emitted phenomenon may carry the combination.

A concept-bearing structure passes when its geometry, topology, material, body-local anchoring, proportion, or silhouette is constitutive of the character's identity. Spatial separation alone does not invalidate a structure that passes the strict body-bound detached identity-structure rule in §0.0. A free object or surrounding effect that can exist independently of the character does not pass.

The embodiment verification therefore checks: anatomical-reconstruction display, complete causal origin in the supplied concept combination, preserved major recognizable vocabulary, unmistakable material embodiment, complete required interactions, aesthetic economy, and sufficient concept recognizability without another visual system.

#### 5.3.13 Joint-fusion carrier lock and aesthetic-economy reconciliation — HARD

For a three-concept command, every required pairwise interaction and the complete A × B × C interaction must have a genuine physical consequence. The preferred solution is never three adjacent independent concept carriers.

The compiler MUST produce **one or more major or strong meso-scale shared structures** whose final geometry, material, topology, morphology, proportion, or attachment would not exist from any one concept alone. At least one unmistakable higher-order structure must depend on all active concepts simultaneously.

A single well-designed shared structure may satisfy several interaction obligations when its construction genuinely depends on those interactions. The workflow must not fabricate a separate appendage, cavity, material zone, or large structure for every pair merely to increase the count.

The three concepts must therefore be visible **through interaction inside the body**, not as:

> CONCEPT A FEATURE + CONCEPT B FEATURE + CONCEPT C FEATURE

but as:

> **A × B × C → SHARED PHYSICAL CONSTRUCTION**

Higher-order fusion must be strong enough to alter the character's actual design and remain readable at macro or strong meso scale. It should propagate into additional systems or regions when that propagation strengthens completeness, coherence, recognizability, or aesthetic quality. It is **not** mandatory to create a separate silhouette structure, separate topology structure, and separate material structure solely to satisfy a numeric quota when the same integrated construction already carries those consequences convincingly.

Concept completeness and interaction completeness are mandatory. Redundant manifestation is not. A valid higher-order construction must not collapse into a reusable recipe such as 'species body + material plating + attached atmospheric mass' or another stable carrier split that could be restated almost unchanged across multiple candidates.
#### 5.3.14 Concept-carrier sufficiency without forced plurality — HARD

Each supplied concept must have **sufficient physical evidence**, but the workflow must not impose a carrier-count quota.

For every concept, confirm:

- its classified major recognizable vocabulary is present;
- at least one unmistakable physical or identity-bearing manifestation exists at macro or strong meso scale;
- it materially participates in every required pairwise interaction;
- it participates in the complete-set higher-order construction when three or more primary concepts are supplied;
- it remains integrated into the one character rather than isolated as a token feature.

A concept may spread into several body systems when that produces a stronger character. It may also be concentrated into one exceptional architectural system plus its required interaction consequences when that is already complete and unmistakable.

A single structure may perform several roles simultaneously. Do not multiply structures to satisfy categories, occupy unused anatomy, or manufacture diversity.

A concept fails when it survives only as a label, one minor colour cue, a tiny marking, an insignificant appendage, an external effect, or another weak token that would not materially change the character if removed.

For concepts whose familiar depiction is an external phenomenon, energy, force, weather, fluid, material state, or abstract property, translate the chosen physical interpretation into character-owned construction. Emitted arcs, bolts, clouds, puddles, surrounding fluid, environmental weather, floating symbolic objects, or similar presentation effects do not carry the Phase-1 concept. A spatially separated form is eligible only when it independently passes the body-bound detached identity-structure rule in §0.0; generic surrounding effects do not.

The criterion is **complete recognizable vocabulary + unmistakable material presence + required interaction completeness + aesthetic economy**.

#### 5.3.15 ABSTRACT / BEHAVIOURAL / PSYCHOLOGICAL CONCEPTS — UNIVERSAL RULE

A primary concept whose source meaning is abstract, behavioural, emotional, psychological, relational, or otherwise nonmaterial is held to the same embodiment standard as every material or morphological primary concept.

Derive direct body-relevant consequences from the concept's usable properties. Depending on the resolved interpretation, these may alter topology, continuity/discontinuity, distribution, proportional tension, symmetry/asymmetry logic, structural rhythm, material organization, repeated or suppressed body systems, face/head architecture, eye architecture, hair architecture, appendage relationships, or other consequential physical systems.

At least one macro or strong-meso body construction must materially depend on the primary nonmaterial concept. Face, hair, pigmentation, markings, and identity finishing may reinforce it, but cannot be its sole carrier unless they participate in a larger structural consequence.

The concept must also modify the required shared structures with the other primary concepts rather than remaining a parallel mood or identity layer. Perform the counterfactual test: mentally remove the concept. If the same major body architecture, topology, material organization, and shared fusion structures could remain substantially unchanged, materialization is too weak.

The physical rule must be deliberate and reconstructable. Random noise, arbitrary deformation, or generic irregularity does not satisfy an abstract primary concept.

Within each initial five-character sheet group, independently re-derive the physical rule for the nonmaterial concept. Reusing the same structuralization strategy with only different intensity, colour, expression, or local placement is population convergence.

Neutral analytical standing remains unchanged; the concept is carried by the character construction itself.
#### 5.3.16 AESTHETIC / CULTURAL / SOCIAL-VISUAL CONCEPTS — UNIVERSAL RULE

When a supplied concept describes an aesthetic, cultural, social-visual, beauty, or style identity, separate intrinsic character-design properties from optional later outfit conventions.

Phase-1 materialization may use face geometry, eye/lash/brow architecture, hair mass and strand organization, skin/value treatment, nail and hand finishing, pigmentation, markings, body proportions, intrinsic surface finishing, colour relationships, material relationships, asymmetry, and other character-owned systems that genuinely belong to the resolved concept.

The concept must remain legible in the analytical body reference without depending on Phase-2 clothing, a special pose, a prop, a narrative setting, or imported identity shorthand.

When the concept is primary, it also participates in the pairwise and complete-set fusion obligations. Intrinsic styling cues alone are insufficient if the rest of the body architecture would remain unchanged after removing the concept.
#### 5.3.17 ELEMENTAL / ENERGY / FORCE / PROCESS CONCEPTS — UNIVERSAL RULE

An elemental, energetic, force-based, environmental-process, or dynamic physical concept must be compiled into bounded character-owned construction before rendering.

Resolve the applicable physical behavior first, then propagate it into one or more consequential systems such as substrate, internal organization, surface geometry, topology, appendage architecture, structural transitions, hair construction, eye/head treatment, local morphology, asymmetry, colour/value architecture, or silhouette.

The physical behavior must alter the body itself. External emission or surrounding presentation cannot substitute for the concept's body construction.

For a primary concept, at least one macro or strong-meso structure must depend on the resolved physical behavior and must physically interact with the other primary concepts. The result remains a coherent character rather than a body plus a surrounding phenomenon.
#### 5.3.18 CONCEPT-COMBINATION PRIORITY — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The final character is not:

> a conventional body + one isolated feature for each supplied concept.

That is additive partitioning, not complete fusion.

The final character is:

> **COMPLETE SUPPLIED CONCEPT SET → ONE INTERDEPENDENT BODY ARCHITECTURE**.

For every supplied concept, resolve its own useful physical consequences. For every distinct pair, resolve the pairwise physical interaction. For three or more concepts, resolve the complete-set higher-order interaction and any intermediate interaction that materially affects the construction.

For the common three-concept example A + B + C, this means analyzing A, B, C, A×B, A×C, B×C, and A×B×C. The example is schematic; it does not establish a three-concept limit.

If the causal analysis can assign all major features to separate independent concept modules without any genuine shared physical consequence, the fusion is insufficient.

The body must contain one or more structures/systems whose final form, placement, material, geometry, topology, proportion, attachment, identity behavior, or silhouette is explained by interaction among the supplied concepts. The number of such structures is governed by interaction completeness and aesthetic economy, not by a fixed quota.

#### 5.3.19 Worked examples remain methodological — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The textual examples in this document explain visual quality, petite-body interpretation, macro hierarchy, body-integrated structural complexity, and concrete failure cases.

They are not templates for character identity, species, concept meaning, costume, pose, or composition. Every new input requires its own resolved construction.

The embedded drawing grammar governs rendering separately from character identity.

#### 5.3.20 Universal-first, example-second rule — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Whenever a rule is expressed through a concept-specific example, the document's operative interpretation is universal first and example second.

The generalized rule governs all concepts. The concept-specific example demonstrates one concrete application and does not create a mandatory recipe for unrelated concepts.

Existing examples remain operationally useful and are retained. They must not be treated as the only valid implementation.

This protocol converts the repeated materialization and differentiation requirements into one universal execution system.

It applies to every supplied concept combination.

It does not replace the earlier rules.

It makes their intended relationship explicit and operational.

The governing objective is:

> **SUPPLIED CONCEPT COMBINATION → ONE FEMALE CHARACTER BODY DESIGNED FROM THE COMBINATION**

The governing construction principle is:

> **CONCEPTS ARE MATERIALIZED BY THE BODY, NOT ATTACHED TO A PRE-EXISTING BODY.**

The body is therefore not a neutral mannequin awaiting decoration.

The body is a design result.

Every major physical decision must be selected with the complete concept combination in mind.

#### 5.3.21 Body-first construction is a causal requirement

The compiler must resolve the concept combination before committing to a final body architecture.

The correct sequence is:

1. identify the supplied concepts;
2. decompose each into directly useful visual and physical properties;
3. identify intersections between those properties;
4. identify pairwise interactions;
5. identify higher-order interactions;
6. identify the body systems capable of expressing those interactions;
7. construct a complete female body solution around those interactions;
8. refine the body while preserving the complete concept causality;
9. validate that the resulting body itself communicates the combination.

The incorrect sequence is:

1. construct a generic female body;
2. assign familiar concept features;
3. attach those features;
4. add surface treatment;
5. add external presentation;
6. call the result a fusion.

The second sequence is structurally incapable of producing the required result.

#### 5.3.22 Body systems are all available to concept materialization

Any supplied concept may alter any physically relevant body system.

The compiler must consider, where useful:

- head proportions;
- skull geometry;
- facial planes;
- eyes;
- brows;
- nose;
- mouth;
- jaw;
- ears;
- neck;
- shoulders;
- clavicular structure;
- chest/ribcage;
- chest architecture;
- sternum;
- spine;
- abdomen;
- waist;
- hip/lower-torso region;
- hips;
- gluteal architecture;
- arms;
- forearms;
- wrists;
- hands;
- fingers;
- thighs;
- knees;
- calves;
- ankles;
- feet;
- skeletal geometry;
- joint construction;
- musculature;
- connective structures;
- body-mass distribution;
- body surface;
- hair;
- eyes and their material response;
- markings;
- body-integrated decorative structures;
- repeated structural units;
- appendages;
- appendage roots;
- cavities;
- apertures;
- internal channels;
- body openings;
- material transitions;
- silhouette;
- asymmetry.

The compiler must not reserve concept materialization for only the most obvious associated body feature.

#### 5.3.23 Materialization depth

Materialization depth describes **where evidence can exist**, not how many depth levels every concept must occupy.

Available levels are:

- **Depth 1 — macro:** silhouette, major anatomy, major appendages, major topology, dominant material masses;
- **Depth 2 — meso:** secondary structural organization, joints, grouped surface systems, major transitions;
- **Depth 3 — local/internal:** internal construction, material boundaries, smaller topology, structured surface organization;
- **Depth 4 — micro:** fine texture, small markings, tiny segmentation, fine edge or material detail.

A primary concept must have unmistakable evidence at macro or strong meso scale. It may additionally appear at deeper levels when that strengthens the design.

A concept that appears only at Depth 4 is insufficient. A concept that appears only as decorative surface styling over an otherwise unrelated body is insufficient. An independent detached object or effect is not made sufficient by detail density. Certified body-bound detached morphology is judged by its structural identity role, not by detail density.

The workflow must seek the **strongest useful depth combination**, not the maximum number of occupied depth levels. Once complete recognition and required interactions are secure, deeper repetition is optional and subject to aesthetic economy.

#### 5.3.24 Macro prominence of concept fusion

The complete concept combination must materially contribute to the character's immediate macro/strong-meso read, but not every concept must independently dominate the outer silhouette.

The goal is not maximal clutter. The goal is maximal **recognizability and character value per important shape**.

Each character must therefore have:

- a clear macro silhouette;
- a small number of dominant visual hooks or masses;
- concept-derived structures/material systems readable at macro or strong meso scale;
- clear cross-concept interaction evidence;
- controlled supporting structures;
- quiet regions where useful;
- intricate micro-detail subordinate to the major construction.

A concept may be strongest through substrate, face/head/hair, topology, internal structure, an appendage, or another identity-bearing system rather than through silhouette width alone.

Complexity belongs inside the resolved character. It must not escape into surrounding visual noise or be multiplied merely to force every concept into the first silhouette read.

#### 5.3.25 Concept visibility and fusion occur simultaneously

Every supplied concept must remain individually detectable in the finished body.

At the same time, the concepts must be fused so that the body is not interpretable as separate concept modules.

> **INDIVIDUAL VISIBILITY + SHARED PHYSICAL CAUSALITY = VALID FUSION**
