When the supplied concept vocabulary or complete fusion produces apertures, cavities, breaches, channels, missing volume, recessed chambers, discontinuities, or other internal-topology systems, those systems are body architecture rather than decoration.

The workflow MUST search for structurally meaningful solutions rather than defaulting to one convenient hole in the same limb or torso region.

Possible topology architectures include recessed cavities, through-openings, nested openings, perforated structural regions, interrupted continuity, split planes, branching internal channels, recessed compartments, missing-volume systems, openings integrated into appendage roots, openings integrated into load-bearing structures, discontinuous plates, asymmetric void systems, or other concept-specific constructions.

##### 5.11.21.1 Topology locus must diverge — HARD

The same topology locus MUST NOT be repeated across the five characters as a default. Vary, where the active concept/fusion supports it, cranial/head, neck, shoulder/clavicular, ribcage/torso, waist, hip/lower-torso, upper limb, lower limb, dorsal, appendage-root, internal axial, or integrated multi-region systems.

The exact locus must be determined by the independently resolved Concept-Fusion Architecture, not by a fixed population rotation.

##### 5.11.21.2 Topology architecture must diverge — HARD

Do not differentiate cavities merely by moving one identical opening. Vary the full system where applicable: count, scale, depth, geometry, orientation, branching, connectivity, nesting, distribution, rim/edge material, relationship to skeleton/support, relationship to appendages, material transitions, asymmetry, and silhouette consequence.

If an aperture/cavity system is part of a concept's major recognizable vocabulary, it remains present across all characters while these architectural parameters diverge. If it is genuinely optional, absence is allowed when justified by the complete design.

##### 5.11.21.3 Topology must interact with the complete concept fusion — HARD

A topology-derived concept cannot remain an isolated third feature group. Its openings, cavities, channels, or discontinuities must physically alter and be altered by the other active concepts through shared geometry, material, attachment, support, proportion, or silhouette. When the resolved interpretation depends on absence or missing volume, the Frozen Character Specification must define that absence through boundary geometry, depth, continuity/connectivity, and its effect on neighboring anatomy or support; interior colour may support readability but cannot be the carrier of the topology. Never resolve absent volume as a dark fill, bodysuit-like underlayer, generic shadow field, or another pseudo-solid substitute for the missing space.

Possible generalized interactions include an appendage root reorganized around a cavity; a material field that grows around or through an opening; support structures rerouted by missing volume; internal channels connecting concept-derived regions; or a topology whose rim, depth, fill, and shape are changed by another concept. These are examples, not presets.
##### 5.11.21.4 Principal fusion-mechanism divergence — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Changing only the location, count, shape, or size of the same topology recipe is insufficient population differentiation. Across an initial five-character sheet, the **principal causal mechanism** by which a topology-derived concept participates in the complete fusion must itself be independently authored. Do not allow all five bodies to collapse into variants of “opening + rim + internal bridge,” “hole + crystal support,” or any other repeated higher-order recipe merely because the openings occupy different regions.

Valid divergence may involve materially different mechanism classes such as interrupted continuity with reconnected load paths, offset or displaced anatomical planes, split-and-rejoined appendage construction, internal branching channels, partial absent-volume architecture, nested structural chambers, discontinuous plates, longitudinal clefts, transverse separations, topology integrated into skeletal or appendage roots, or other concept-specific solutions. These are search directions, not a required menu. Each character's principal higher-order carrier must have a different structural logic, and repeated secondary mechanisms must not become the population's hidden template.

Before certification, compare the five principal higher-order carriers in plain structural language. If two or more can be summarized by the same causal sentence with only body-part names, aperture positions, colours, or materials changed, rebuild the weaker design before Frozen Character Specification certification.

#### 5.11.22 Universal iconic-morphology and appendage divergence engine — HARD / ABSOLUTE

When a concept has an iconic morphological vocabulary, the workflow must first classify its **major recognizable characteristics**. Every character preserves that complete major set. The workflow then radically varies the physical answer for each retained characteristic.

Do not interpret morphological differentiation as “Character 1 gets wings, Character 2 gets horns, Character 3 gets a tail.” If wings, horns, and a tail are all classified as major recognizable characteristics, all characters retain them.

For every retained major morphology system, vary applicable parameters including count, pairing, origin, attachment depth, scale, length, width, thickness, curvature, taper, branching, segmentation, articulation, jointure, direction, orientation, spread, symmetry/asymmetry, material, visible texture, internal construction, skeletal/support relationship, interaction with cavities/topology, interaction with the other concepts, and silhouette role. For every pair of characters sharing the same mandatory morphology class, at least three implementation dimensions must materially differ, and at least one of those differences must be structural—origin/attachment, internal support, topology, or cross-concept interaction—rather than only size, curvature, colour, or surface texture. Reusing the same root and support architecture with cosmetic variation is not an independent implementation.
##### 5.11.22.1 Wing or membrane systems — HARD WHEN PRESENT/REQUIRED

When wings or equivalent membrane/flight appendages are present or required, do not default to one scapular pair. The population may explore multiple symmetrical pairs, unilateral groups, unequal left/right systems, different root regions, radically different sizes, upward or lateral spread, compact folded architectures, torn/discontinuous forms when conceptually justified, feathered/membranous/scaled/crystalline/liquid-like or other constituent materials, different skeletal support, and different integration with other concept structures.

If wings are part of the active major recognizable vocabulary, **zero wings is not a valid differentiation option**.

##### 5.11.22.2 Horn / crest / cranial appendage systems — HARD WHEN PRESENT/REQUIRED

Resolve count, pairing, origin, scale, length, width, taper, curvature, branching, orientation, cross-section, attachment, symmetry, material, visible texture, internal construction, relation to skull/hair/ears, concept interactions, and silhouette role. A keratin-like horn, saline-crystal horn, bone horn, liquid-like horn, layered-scale horn, fibrous horn, or another concept-derived material must visibly read differently through both construction and 2D rendering.

##### 5.11.22.3 Tail and axial extension systems — HARD WHEN PRESENT/REQUIRED

Resolve origin, count, branching, length, taper, thickness, articulation, direction, material, texture, internal support, hip/lower-torso region/spine integration, interaction with cavities/topology, interaction with other concepts, and silhouette role.

##### 5.11.22.4 Repeated surface morphology such as scales/plates/feathers — HARD WHEN PRESENT/REQUIRED

Resolve density, size, geometry, orientation, regional distribution, layering, material, texture, transition into neighboring matter, relation to musculature/support, relation to appendages/topology, asymmetry, and visual hierarchy. Do not cover every available body region with the same unit at the same density merely to prove concept presence.

##### 5.11.22.5 Mandatory morphology is complete but independently built — HARD

When a morphology class is classified as major, every character retains it. Population diversity changes its origin, count/pairing where semantically permitted, attachment depth, support architecture, topology, articulation, material state, relation to neighboring anatomy, cross-concept interaction, and silhouette role. Omission is not diversity, and cosmetic parameter changes are not independent construction.
#### 5.11.23 Universal nonuniformity / instability / irregularity divergence engine — HARD / ABSOLUTE

When an active concept is interpreted through nonuniformity, instability, irregularity, disruption, or comparable organizational behavior, the result must be controlled physical design rather than random noise.

Available manifestations include unequal bilateral development, irregular branching, variable segment lengths, nonuniform appendage relationships, competing structural directions resolved coherently, uneven cavity distribution, asymmetric joints/supports, heterogeneous interlocking substrate, abrupt but coherent transitions, non-repeating topology, varied local growth directions, and irregular but readable silhouette events.

##### 5.11.23.1 Irregularity must remain coherent — HARD

Every irregularity remains structurally integrated, visually intentional, and compatible with the complete character. The viewer must perceive designed nonuniformity, not generation damage or arbitrary disorder.

##### 5.11.23.2 Irregularity mechanism must differ between characters — HARD

Do not repeat one irregularity grammar five times. Vary which systems are affected, where irregularity begins, its scale, whether it is axial/appendicular/surface/topological/multi-system, its degree of asymmetry, how structures merge, and how the irregularity interacts with every other active concept.

##### 5.11.23.3 Irregularity must not become detail soup — HARD

The macro silhouette and primary body architecture remain simple and readable. Express irregularity first through deliberate large/meso-scale structural choices and only then through subordinate detail. Tiny spikes, fragments, marks, or micro-branches alone do not establish the concept.

##### 5.11.23.4 Input-specific organizational mapping is recomputed — HARD

No abstract organizational concept receives one permanent visual mapping. Re-derive its physical rule for the active combination and candidate. The result must remain coherent, causal, and integrated with the complete body rather than becoming generic disorder.
#### 5.11.24 Higher-order fusion construction stress test — HARD / OPERATIONAL

For any three-or-more-concept combination, the compiler must reject a stable carrier split in which each concept keeps a separate role. At least one identity-bearing major or strong-meso construction must depend on the complete set simultaneously.

Search may emphasize topology, substrate, appendage support, axial/cranial organization, limb/torso architecture, or another body system, but those are internal search dimensions rather than population slots. Every candidate recomputes the governing interaction from scratch.

The five solutions must remain visibly female characters, preserve the complete major recognizable vocabulary, and differ in causal architecture rather than by parameter substitution. Quiet body regions remain valid once concept completeness and interaction completeness are achieved.
#### 5.11.25 Population-wide structural parameter coverage — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Before certification, the compiler must verify that the five characters collectively explore meaningful breadth across the following systems:

- height;
- head/body ratio;
- torso/leg ratio;
- torso length;
- shoulder width;
- ribcage geometry;
- waist geometry;
- hip/lower-torso region geometry;
- hip architecture;
- chest volume;
- chest projection;
- chest placement;
- limb length;
- limb thickness;
- joint placement;
- musculature;
- body mass distribution;
- face geometry;
- eye geometry;
- hair mass;
- body substrate;
- aperture/cavity locus where applicable;
- aperture/cavity count where applicable;
- aperture/cavity scale where applicable;
- aperture/cavity depth where applicable;
- aperture/cavity branching/connectivity where applicable;
- wing-pair count where applicable;
- wing origin where applicable;
- wing geometry where applicable;
- horn count where applicable;
- horn origin where applicable;
- horn geometry where applicable;
- tail count where applicable;
- tail origin where applicable;
- tail geometry where applicable;
- scale/plate or other repeated-morphology architecture where applicable;
- appendage attachment;
- appendage material;
- asymmetry mechanism;
- fusion locus;
- pairwise interaction structures;
- higher-order interaction structures;
- silhouette mechanism;
- colour architecture;
- material architecture;
- character-identity finishing.

This is a coverage requirement, not a requirement that every parameter differ in isolation. The parameters must combine into coherent independent designs.

##### 5.11.25.1 No single-axis solution — HARD

A character cannot be considered differentiated because it differs strongly on one axis while remaining nearly identical on all others.

Examples of insufficient differentiation include:

- only height changes;
- only body mass changes;
- only chest changes;
- only wing count changes;
- only aperture position changes;
- only colour changes;
- only face changes;
- only asymmetry changes.

The final difference must be multi-system and architecturally coherent.

##### 5.11.25.2 Multi-axis differentiation must be intentional — HARD

Each character must contain a unique combination of differences across multiple major systems.

Those differences must reinforce one another aesthetically.

A tall body should not be tall merely because the population needs a tall body. Its torso, limb proportions, silhouette, face, material system, concept-fusion architecture, and the scale or placement of appendages, apertures, or other unusual systems where they actually exist should be designed to make that stature belong to that specific character.

The same principle applies to short, compact, elongated, broad-shouldered, narrow-shouldered, long-torso, short-torso, high-projection-chest, low-projection-chest, heavily asymmetric, lightly asymmetric, multi-appendage, compact-appendage, topology-dominant, substrate-dominant, and other body regimes, provided no major recognizable characteristic is removed merely to create variation.

#### 5.11.26 Macro integrity after structural enrichment — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The workflow must distinguish between a complex body and a visually overloaded macro presentation.

A body may contain extensive structural information while presenting a clean, deliberate macro silhouette.

The compiler must therefore organize the body into:

1. primary silhouette masses;
2. primary anatomical masses;
3. primary concept-fusion structures;
4. secondary structures;
5. tertiary micro-structures.

Only the first three levels are allowed to dominate the macro reading.

Secondary and tertiary structures must remain visually subordinate.

##### 5.11.26.1 Structural saturation correction — HARD

If a character accumulates too many large spikes, overlapping appendages, cavities, plates, branches, or other major structures in the same visual region, the compiler must redistribute or simplify their macro arrangement without removing their underlying concept consequences.

The correction must preserve required concept consequences and genuinely strong unrelated decisions while improving hierarchy. If the resolved body architecture itself is the cause of the aesthetic/saturation failure, that architecture must be reopened pre-render rather than artificially protected merely because it was structurally valid.

Do not solve saturation by deleting the concept.

Do not solve saturation by adding background space, effects, lighting, or presentation devices.

Do not solve saturation by changing the character's pose.

##### 5.11.26.2 Concept structures require breathing room — HARD

Major concept-derived structures, topology/cavity systems, appendages, materials, and other shared fusion structures must remain individually legible enough to be understood as parts of one coherent body.

They must not merge into an unreadable mass.

#### 5.11.27 Structural collision test — HARD

Strip conceptually:

- colour;
- hair styling;
- markings;
- clothing;
- accessories;
- weapons;
- effects;
- pose;
- expression;
- lighting;
- background;
- rendering treatment;
- micro-detail.

The remaining bodies must still be clearly different constructions.

A numerical criterion may be used as a diagnostic, but it never overrides concrete construction evidence. If two planned characters still read as one body, revise the affected constructions before rendering. If the actual image collapses them despite distinct certified plans, record the image failure without rebuilding it.

Collision repair changes the structural systems that actually caused the collision; cosmetic or finishing changes alone cannot repair structural convergence.

The five characters are five independently authored female anime/manhwa characters.

The same concepts are the shared input.

The characters are not a base design with variants.

The compiler must therefore author five distinct solutions from the beginning.

#### 5.11.28 Independence is structural

Independence must exist simultaneously across:

- face;
- eyes;
- hair;
- head/neck;
- body proportions;
- torso;
- chest architecture;
- waist;
- hip/lower-torso region;
- hips;
- limbs;
- skeleton;
- musculature;
- body-mass distribution;
- topology;
- cavities;
- appendages;
- appendage roots;
- asymmetry;
- materials;
- colour architecture;
- character-identity finishing, including optional cosmetics where selected;
- silhouette;
- concept-fusion architecture;
- concept interaction mechanisms.

#### 5.11.29 Height is one axis among many

Height must vary visibly within every initial five-character sheet group.

The Frozen Character Specifications must include a clearly shortest character, a clearly tallest character, and intermediate statures that do not collapse into one common height band. The stature difference is part of the body design itself and is not supplied by pose, cropping, footwear, or appendage height.

However, height variation alone does not establish independence.

A pair of characters with different height but the same body architecture remains related.

Height must therefore be accompanied by deliberate changes in other major systems.
#### 5.11.30 Body regime is deliberately authored

Each body is authored as a specific aesthetic and structural regime.

Possible axes include:

- elongated;
- compact;
- broad;
- narrow;
- long-torso;
- short-torso;
- long-limbed;
- short-limbed;
- high-shouldered;
- sloped-shouldered;
- narrow-waisted;
- expanded-waisted;
- different hip/lower-torso geometries;
- different mass distributions;
- different muscular architecture.

These are options within the universal body-design space. When muscularity is selected, the default strength-oriented solution is an athletic, visibly trained body with defined musculature and controlled mass rather than extreme bulk. Broader, softer, fuller, or heavier regimes remain valid when they specifically strengthen that character; they are not used merely as an easy population-difference slot.

They are not random sliders.
#### 5.11.31 Every parameter exists to create a character

The compiler must actively use the available parameter space to author a specific, coherent, highly refined identity.

Parameters must be selected because they improve the character design.

Random differences are not useful differentiation.

Pure parameter noise is not useful differentiation.

A valid difference has a visible aesthetic consequence.

#### 5.11.32 Independent face authorship

Each character must have a developed face.

Differences may occur in:

- face length;
- face width;
- jaw shape;
- chin shape;
- cheek structure;
- brow structure;
- eye size;
- eye width;
- eye angle;
- iris design;
- eyelid construction;
- lash structure;
- nose design;
- mouth shape;
- lip structure;
- facial asymmetry;
- cosmetic organization.

The face must not become one shared facial template repeated across five bodies.

#### 5.11.33 Character-identity finishing is part of main-character quality

Identity finishing is not optional filler, but **conventional cosmetics are optional**.

Every character must receive deliberate facial/head/appearance completion appropriate to her exact identity. This may use eye styling, lash architecture, brow styling, hair finishing, pigmentation, facial material transitions, intrinsic markings, nail treatment, accent placement, or conventional makeup where useful.

The actual selection is character-specific. A no-makeup solution may be the strongest choice. What cannot be omitted is the resolved identity itself.

Identity finishing must be coordinated with the character's body and concept-fusion architecture and contribute to individual recognizability.

#### 5.11.34 Main-character quality applies individually

Each character must independently be strong enough to function as the main character of an anime/manhwa.

The population is not averaged.

A strong Character 01 cannot compensate for a weak Character 04.

A structurally unusual character cannot be rendered as a rough placeholder.

A petite character cannot be simplified.

A highly asymmetric character cannot be simplified.

Every character receives:

- full facial development;
- complete hair design;
- complete body construction;
- complete concept fusion;
- complete material system;
- complete colour architecture;
- complete character-identity finishing, with optional cosmetics only where selected;
- professional 2D rendering;
- controlled micro-detail.

#### 5.11.35 Petite-quality law

A petite character is permitted and encouraged as an independent body solution where useful.

Petite means:

- compact scale;
- coherent proportions;
- attractive design;
- complete body development;
- complete face development;
- complete concept materialization;
- professional rendering.

Petite does not mean:

- caricatural compression;
- caricaturally compressed simplification;
- undeveloped anatomy;
- mascot-like construction.

A petite character must receive the same design effort and quality as every other character.

#### 5.11.36 Appendage independence

Appendage systems must be actively designed for each character.

Variation may occur in:

- count;
- number of pairs;
- partial versus complete pairing;
- unilateral versus bilateral distribution;
- origin;
- attachment;
- support structure;
- direction;
- length;
- width;
- thickness;
- curvature;
- branching;
- segmentation;
- material;
- surface architecture;
- articulation;
- asymmetry;
- silhouette role.

The compiler must not default every character to the same appendage count and origin.

#### 5.11.37 Appendage roots are part of identity

The body region from which an appendage originates is itself an identity-bearing design decision.

The compiler must consider origins in multiple anatomically coherent or intentionally transformed body regions.

The root may modify:

- shoulder architecture;
- spine;
- ribcage;
- hip/lower-torso region;
- limb attachment;
- axial topology;
- local material;
- silhouette.

Appendage roots should therefore participate in concept fusion rather than being decorative plugs added to a standard body.

#### 5.11.38 Aperture independence

Aperture or cavity systems must vary when the concepts support them.

Variation includes:

- body locus;
- count;
- size;
- depth;
- shape;
- orientation;
- distribution;
- branching;
- local topology;
- surrounding material;
- edge architecture;
- symmetry;
- interaction with appendages;
- interaction with internal structures.

The same body region must not become the default carrier across all five characters.

#### 5.11.39 Material independence

Each character receives an independently authored material architecture.

Material identity may alter:

- substrate;
- surface reflectivity;
- transparency;
- translucency;
- hardness;
- softness;
- layering;
- internal structure;
- edge response;
- highlight shape;
- colour transition;
- regional distribution.

Material differences must be structural enough to contribute to character identity.

#### 5.11.40 Colour-architecture independence

The population must explore genuinely different palette architectures without imposing a numeric hue-count schedule.

A single character may use one dominant colour family, two or several major families, restrained near-monochrome, broad multicolour systems, spectral/rainbow-like distributions, or another coherent solution. Multiple characters may use the same approximate number of hue families when the actual visual architecture differs substantially.

Compare and deliberately author:

- dominant and subordinate families;
- value range and location of light/dark masses;
- saturation structure and location of saturated accents;
- warm/cool relationships;
- local contrast;
- material-specific colour behavior;
- distribution;
- hair/body relationship;
- eye/body relationship;
- appendage/body relationship;
- cavity/interior treatment where applicable;
- focal hierarchy.

Monochrome is one possible authored solution. It is not a population default. Hue count alone neither proves nor disproves differentiation.

#### 5.11.41 Silhouette independence

Every character must have an individually authored silhouette.

The compiler must compare:

- major mass placement;
- width distribution;
- height;
- appendage spread;
- appendage density;
- negative spaces;
- asymmetry;
- hair outline;
- body contour;
- concept-derived structural contour.

A character that differs internally but shares the same outer silhouette remains insufficiently differentiated.

#### 5.11.42 Aesthetic coherence

Independence does not mean visual incoherence.

Each character must have one deliberate aesthetic system connecting:

- face;
- hair;
- body;
- concept structures;
- material;
- colour;
- character-identity finishing, including optional cosmetics where selected;
- silhouette.

The result must appear authored rather than randomly assembled.

#### 5.11.43 No first-character template

Character 01 has no privileged design status.

It is not a baseline.

It is not the population seed.

It is not the default body.

Characters 02–05 are not modifications of Character 01.

Each character must be independently resolved before population comparison.

#### 5.11.44 No sequential derivation

The compiler must not design:

> Character 01 → modify Character 01 → modify Character 02 → modify Character 03.

Instead:

> Character 01 = independent solution
> Character 02 = independent solution
> Character 03 = independent solution
> Character 04 = independent solution
> Character 05 = independent solution

Population comparison happens only after independent solutions exist.

#### 5.11.45 Useful-difference removal protocol

To test useful differentiation:

##### 5.11.45.1 Test A — finishing/colour stripping

Temporarily ignore:

- colour;
- hairstyle finishing;
- markings;
- character-identity finishing, including optional cosmetic detail where selected;
- presentation;
- micro-detail.

The body identities must remain materially distinct.

##### 5.11.45.2 Test B — labels hidden, anatomy intact

Hide names, semantic labels, and causal explanations. Keep all physical body structures. Compare proportions, masses, joints, topology, appendage integration, and material distribution as visible form. Distinct labels cannot create a pass for similar bodies.

##### 5.11.45.3 Test C — intact-body fusion strategy comparison

Compare the resolved causal construction of the intact bodies: how the same concepts jointly determine geometry, topology, substrate, joints, mass distribution, and connections. Identify evidence of different complete fusion strategies. Do not delete the body or inspect floating concept ornaments.

All three tests must pass. Body identity and fusion strategy are complementary views of one construction, never separable layers pasted together.

#### 5.11.46 Independent-character aesthetic completion

After structural differentiation is established, each character receives individual design finishing.

This includes:

- face refinement;
- eye refinement;
- hair refinement;
- cosmetic refinement;
- material refinement;
- colour refinement;
- silhouette refinement;
- micro-detail refinement.

The finishing stage must reinforce the already-independent character.

It must not be used to create the illusion of independence after structural convergence.

#### 5.11.47 Pairwise population comparison

All ten character pairs must be compared.

For each pair, compare:

- body regime;
- proportions;
- face;
- eyes;
- hair;
- chest architecture;
- torso;
- hip/lower-torso region;
- limbs;
- topology;
- aperture system;
- appendage system;
- appendage roots;
- asymmetry;
- material;
- palette;
- character-identity finishing, including optional cosmetics where selected;
- silhouette;
- concept-fusion architecture.

Any pair that reads as the same design requires reconstruction.

#### 5.11.48 Group convergence test

In addition to pairwise comparison, inspect the population for clusters.

No three-character subset should share the same dominant construction strategy.

A population may contain local similarities.

It must not contain a dominant repeated design recipe.

#### 5.11.49 Independent quality benchmark

Each character is compared independently against the embedded quality benchmark.

The benchmark establishes:

- physical sophistication;
- body construction quality;
- structural readability;
- material integration;
- appendage integration;
- facial quality;
- aesthetic finish;
- rendering quality.

The benchmark does not establish a copied identity.

#### 5.11.50 True-front geometry

Every principal character view is truly frontal: face plane centered, torso unrotated, shoulders and hip/lower-torso orientation frontal except for actual anatomical asymmetry, arms neutral, legs naturally supporting the body. A 3/4 camera or pose-created asymmetry is not an analytical front.

#### 5.11.51 Mandatory supplementary profile geometry

Every mandatory profile is an exact 90-degree neutral side view of the same frozen body.

#### 5.11.52 Mandatory supplementary rear geometry

Every mandatory rear view is directly rear-facing and preserves the same frozen appendage origins, rear topology, material distribution, hair architecture, and asymmetry.

#### 5.11.53 Analytical view set

Each initial Phase-1 sheet requires exactly five dominant full-body true fronts, five exact 90-degree profiles, and five direct-rear views. Profile and rear views are mandatory subordinate analytical evidence and may not be omitted.

#### 5.11.54 Neutral standing

Every principal front is upright, stable, directly frontal, naturally balanced, static, and anatomically inspectable. Arms remain relaxed; no raised arms, hand-behind-head poses, contrapposto, crossed-leg stance, hip cock, kneeling, walking, floating, or combat posture is permitted.

#### 5.11.55 No pose substitution

Concepts influence construction, never pose. Character individuality and concept recognition must survive standardized stance.

#### 5.11.56 Phase-1 visual field

Phase 1 is a clean analytical presentation. The surrounding field remains plain white and carries no concept information. The only separated forms allowed are certified body-bound detached identity structures with fixed character ownership under §0.0.

#### 5.11.57 No auxiliary-module substitution

No lower-row module, face crop, concept symbol, palette strip, material board, or decorative study may replace full-body character space. The mandatory profile and rear views remain subordinate in scale to the dominant fronts.

#### 5.11.58 Exact 16:9 landscape format

Each initial sheet output is one continuous 16:9 landscape raster with five left-to-right character regions. The sheet is not converted into portrait cards, a vertical non-analytical arrangement, a scene, or a staggered glamour lineup.

#### 5.11.59 Format is part of the design specification

Preserve one continuous landscape canvas, exactly five principal fronts, neutral standing, complete body extents, plain white field, and clear ownership of all appendages/body-bound identity structures.

#### 5.11.60 No format inference

The renderer receives the exact certified geometry. A visually attractive lineup that uses glamour posing, crops, overlaps, or undersized bodies is incorrect.

#### 5.11.61 Simple macro / intricate micro

The macro layer must be immediately readable.

The first read should reveal:

- five distinct characters;
- five distinct silhouettes;
- five distinct body architectures;
- the major concept structures;
- the major material structures.

The micro layer may then become extremely detailed.

Micro-detail must remain subordinate to:

- silhouette;
- body proportions;
- major concept structures;
- appendages;
- topology;
- face;
- material masses.

#### 5.11.62 Macro readability priority

If complexity begins to obscure the primary body architecture, the compiler must reorganize the major masses.

The correct correction is:

> **RESTRUCTURE THE MACRO**

**INVALID:**

> **REDUCE THE CHARACTER TO A GENERIC BODY**

Concept richness must remain.

The visual hierarchy must improve.

#### 5.11.63 Face readability

Faces must be individually developed and readable at sheet scale.

The face must include enough construction information to establish character identity without a subordinate close-up. The eyes must remain deliberate. Hair must remain identifiable as a major identity and silhouette system. Character-identity finishing must be visible through the resolved face/head/hair/material/marking design; conventional makeup need not be present. Concept-derived facial transformations must preserve premium female face readability. When several materially valid facial carriers exist, prefer the solution that keeps clear eye/brow/nose/mouth readability and heroine-grade appeal while still making the concept unmistakable. A full beak, muzzle replacement, or comparable takeover of the central facial read is invalid when an equally strong or stronger concept-valid solution can be achieved through surrounding planes, framing structures, cranial/throat architecture, or other less face-destructive carriers.

#### 5.11.64 Character identity and neutral presentation coexist

Character identity is a construction system. Neutral standing is presentation geometry. They are separate systems.

A character may have highly developed identity finishing with no conventional cosmetics while standing completely neutrally. Another may use elaborate makeup if that is the stronger identity choice. The compiler must not use pose, gesture, or expression as a substitute for identity authorship.

#### 5.11.65 Positive serialization carries the design

The renderer-facing package must prioritize:

- female anime/manhwa character identity;
- body architecture;
- body proportions;
- resolved concept structures;
- concept interactions;
- appendages;
- body topology;
- apertures;
- materials;
- colours;
- face;
- hair;
- character-identity finishing, including optional cosmetics where selected;
- dominant full-body true front;
- mandatory exact profile/direct-rear support only when included;
- strict neutral standing;
- 16:9 analytical layout;
- embedded 2D drawing language.

#### 5.11.66 No semantic weakening

Renderer-facing rewording must not change:

- body proportions;
- body type;
- body build;
- appendage count;
- appendage origin;
- topology;
- apertures;
- materials;
- colours;
- face;
- character-identity finishing, including optional cosmetics where selected;
- concept-fusion architecture;
- silhouette;
- quality;
- format.

#### 5.11.67 No generic diversity requests

The final generation package must not rely on generic instructions such as “make them different.”

The exact differences are already encoded.

The renderer receives five independently resolved design blocks.

#### 5.11.68 Renderer block completeness

Each Clean Renderer-Facing character block contains the **render-critical complete design**, not the complete internal audit record. It must communicate, in coherent visual priority:

- identity fingerprint and dominant visual hook;
- stature/body regime and the proportion relationships necessary to preserve it;
- face/head/eyes/hair identity;
- dominant complete-fusion architecture and major concept carriers;
- major topology/apertures and appendage architecture where they affect anatomy or silhouette;
- body substrate, material boundaries, and material-specific 2D appearance;
- colour/value architecture;
- defining asymmetry and subordinate influences where visible;
- macro priorities and selected identity-defining micro-detail;
- the dominant-front presentation contract, internally resolved three-dimensional consistency anchors, every profile/rear view required by the active presentation contract, and the active 2D rendering treatment.

The full analytical blueprint remains more detailed. A render block fails when a missing render-critical decision would force the generator to invent the character, or when excessive bookkeeping obscures the actual design.

#### 5.11.69 Traceability

Every certified design decision remains traceable through the internal blueprint and Frozen Character Specification. The Clean Renderer-Facing Description must contain every **render-critical visible consequence** at the intended scale.

A hidden or scale-invisible construction detail can remain in the authoritative blueprint without being repeated in the clean renderer block. A major body feature, dominant fusion structure, identity-defining material boundary, face/hair decision, appendage/topology anchor, colour/value mass, or view-critical decision cannot be omitted.

Traceability therefore means:

> **internal decision → complete materialized visual specification → render-critical clean clause or justified scale-invisible preservation**.

#### 5.11.70 No semantic loss through renderer compilation

The complete blueprint is authoritative. Renderer compilation follows §2.14: reorder, combine, and compress repeated analytical material while preserving every visible decision needed to realize the design at the intended scale.

Do not equate fidelity with verbosity. A shorter clause that precisely specifies the same visible geometry is preferable to several repeated validator sentences. Conversely, brevity cannot delete an identity-bearing or concept-critical visible feature.

#### 5.11.71 UNIVERSAL CHARACTER-IDENTITY INTEGRATION RULE — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Character identity is not an afterthought and is broader than cosmetics.

The final identity must integrate the underlying face/head geometry, eyes, hair architecture, proportions, body/material transitions, colour/value relationships, pigmentation/markings where selected, and any optional cosmetic treatment into one coherent character-specific design.

Identity finishing may interact with concept-derived body structures when physically and visually appropriate. For example, a marking may follow an existing structural channel, eye construction may reflect a concept-derived material, or hair may integrate with cranial morphology. Such finishing cannot become the sole carrier of a primary concept.

Character identity is not visually subordinate as a blanket rule. The face/head/eyes/hair should normally remain among the strongest focal regions, while the body/concept architecture supplies the complete physical foundation. Identity finishing remains **evidentially subordinate** to structural concept proof: makeup or small finishing details cannot rescue weak fusion or body differentiation. Conventional makeup is never required merely to prove completion.

#### 5.11.72 UNIVERSAL FINAL RENDERING PACKAGE ORDER — HARD / ABSOLUTE

For each initial sheet, the sheet-local FINAL RENDER CONTEXT is ordered exactly as follows:

1. immutable exact 16:9 presentation envelope: five left-to-right regions, five dominant full-body true fronts, five smaller exact profiles, five smaller direct rears, strict neutral standing, complete extents, plain uninterrupted white field;
2. one concise shared intrinsic-coverage paragraph;
3. one active illustrated 2D drawing/material grammar paragraph;
4. Character 01/06/11 shortest-lossless visible payload, depending on sheet;
5. next character payload in left-to-right order;
6. next character payload;
7. next character payload;
8. fifth character payload;
9. one cross-view consistency lock preserving the same frozen identity across front/profile/rear;
10. one terminal layout/extent lock preserving five regions, full extents, ownership, white field, and front dominance.

Each character payload follows §3.5 visual-priority order and preserves every render-critical macro/strong-meso decision. No other sheet's characters, internal proof language, selector/random bookkeeping, SC/DD IDs, subordinate labels, raw concept formula, rejected alternatives, or reasoning enter the context.
#### 5.11.73 UNIVERSAL CHARACTER-BLOCK COMPLETENESS — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Internally, every system that exists in the character is completely resolved. Renderer-facing completeness means that the clean block communicates every system that materially determines the visible character at current scale.

The block therefore preserves the character's body regime, identity geometry, major proportions, face/head/eyes/hair, silhouette, dominant concept-fusion structures, major recognizable concept carriers, defining topology/appendages where applicable, material/value architecture, colour architecture, subordinate visual consequences, asymmetry where used, and the selected micro-detail that contributes to recognition.

Pairwise and higher-order proof, full parameter matrices, random-pool records, and N/A bookkeeping stay internal. Their **visible results** must be present. The schema remains dynamic with concept count and never invents absent systems merely to fill a renderer prompt.

#### 5.11.74 UNIVERSAL DIFFERENTIATION MATRIX — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Before generation, create a five-row internal matrix.

Required columns include:

- stature;
- body regime;
- head/body ratio;
- torso logic;
- ribcage;
- chest;
- waist;
- hip/lower-torso region;
- hips;
- limb proportions;
- skeletal logic;
- mass distribution;
- topology;
- cavity architecture;
- appendage count;
- appendage pairing;
- appendage origin;
- appendage form;
- appendage material;
- fusion locus;
- shared structures;
- higher-order structures;
- substrate;
- material architecture;
- colour architecture, with hue-count recorded descriptively;
- colour architecture;
- face identity;
- eye identity;
- hair identity;
- character-identity finishing;
- asymmetry;
- silhouette;
- macro structure.

No two rows may remain substantially equivalent across the majority of major structural columns.

If two rows converge, rebuild before rendering.

If three rows converge, discard the clustered solutions and re-resolve those characters from the complete concept set.

#### 5.11.75 UNIVERSAL "NO SHARED DEFAULT" RULE — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The compiler may not use an implicit default body for all five characters.

The compiler may not use an implicit default face.

The compiler may not use an implicit default appendage set.

The compiler may not use an implicit default aperture location.

The compiler may not use an implicit default substrate.

The compiler may not use an implicit default palette.

The compiler may not use an implicit default colour distribution.

The compiler may not use an implicit default asymmetry.

The compiler may not use an implicit default fusion locus.

The compiler may not use an implicit default concept carrier.

Defaults are a primary source of population convergence.

Every character must explicitly resolve its relevant architecture.

#### 5.11.76 UNIVERSAL "COMMON STYLE, INDEPENDENT CHARACTERS" RULE — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The five characters should clearly belong to the same rendering exercise.

They should not clearly belong to the same body template.

Common:

- 2D rendering language;
- overall line language;
- general rendering quality;
- concept set;
- Phase-1 presentation;
- female-character identity;
- analytical format.

Independent:

- body;
- proportions;
- face;
- hair;
- appendages;
- topology;
- fusion;
- materials;
- colour;
- asymmetry;
- character-identity finishing, including optional cosmetics where selected;
- silhouette;
- exact concept implementation.

The purpose is a coherent art-direction set containing five independent characters.

#### 5.11.77 UNIVERSAL ACCEPTANCE STANDARD — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The final question is not:

> "Are there differences?"

The final questions are:

> **Are these five different characters?**

> **Are they different because they were constructed differently?**

> **Is the complete concept combination visibly responsible for each body?**

> **Can the combination be recognized from the body itself?**

> **Does each character remain unmistakably female?**

> **Does each character have a developed face and deliberate character-identity finishing?**

> **Does every character reach principal-character quality?**

> **Is the macro structure immediately readable?**

> **Is the micro-detail intricate but subordinate?**

> **Is the rendering genuinely drawn 2D and consistent with the embedded 2D grammar?**

> **Is the presentation exactly one 16:9 landscape sheet with five regions, five large complete true-front neutral-standing characters, with mandatory exact profile/direct-rear views while preserving front readability?**

Before generation, supported YES answers concern the resolved blueprint, Frozen Character Specification, Clean Renderer-Facing Description, and final preflight and permit the image action. After rendering, supported YES answers concern actual image evidence and permit technical acceptance. The two evaluations cannot substitute for each other.

#### 5.11.78 SAME CONCEPTS, FIVE INDEPENDENTLY AUTHORED CHARACTERS — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Every one of the five characters is an independently authored female anime/manhwa character.

The five characters MUST NOT merely be:

- one character with five colourways;
- one character with five appendage arrangements;
- one character with five body heights;
- one character with five different surface materials;
- one character with five different hairstyles;
- one character with five different facial expressions;
- one character with five different asymmetry patterns;
- one character with five different aperture placements;
- one character with five cosmetic treatments;
- one character with five combinations of those parameter changes.

Those are variations of an existing solution.

Each initial sheet group consists of five independently solved character identities; the complete population consists of fifteen generated from their frozen candidate-specific complete Resolved Primary Concept Sets.

Each character must therefore receive an independent:

- body construction;
- structural grammar;
- concept-fusion architecture;
- concept-interaction architecture;
- body-regime;
- proportion system;
- material/substrate architecture;
- appendage architecture;
- internal-topology architecture;
- face architecture;
- hair architecture;
- character-identity finishing;
- colour architecture;
- asymmetry architecture;
- silhouette architecture.

The compiler MUST resolve these systems independently before population comparison.

No character may be authored by copying another character and modifying a finite list of parameters.

No character may be treated as a derivative branch of another character.

No character may be treated as a variant skin, costume variation, alternate colour, alternate species form, alternate transformation stage, alternate pose, or alternate finish of another character.

The five identities must be capable of standing beside one another as five separate principal characters belonging to the same concept-generation exercise without appearing to be five stages of one design.

#### 5.11.79 POPULATION PREVIEW MUST BE READABLE AS FIVE CHARACTERS — HARD / ABSOLUTE

At normal display scale, the five principal fronts must be immediately separable.

A viewer should not need to zoom in to understand that there are five distinct character identities.

Silhouettes must separate.

Body regimes must separate.

Major concept structures must separate.

Face identities must separate.

Colour/material architectures must separate.

Mandatory exact-profile/direct-rear views support structural verification without becoming competing illustrations or reducing the five principal fronts below useful scale.

#### 5.11.80 EMBEDDED QUALITY CALIBRATION MUST BE USED ACTIVELY — HARD

The embedded quality standard defines the minimum expected level of:

- character readability;
- face development;
- eye development;
- body construction;
- appendage integration;
- material separation;
- silhouette quality;
- 2D rendering quality;
- structural clarity;
- aesthetic refinement.

This is a quality benchmark.

It is not a template.

The compiler must ask:

> **Could each of the five generated characters stand alone as the principal designed character in a high-quality illustrated production?**

If the answer is no for even one character, the population fails.

#### 5.11.81 UNIVERSAL DIFFERENTIATION EXAMPLE — INVALID

Five characters are invalid if they are:

> same body architecture
> + different height
> + different colours
> + different appendage counts
> + different hairstyles.

Five characters are also invalid if they are:

> same body architecture
> + same fusion architecture
> + different aperture locations.

> same fusion architecture
> + five different colour palettes.

> same concept mapping
> + one character shorter
> + one character taller
> + one character broader
> + one character narrower.

These are parameter variations of one solution.

#### 5.11.82 PRE-GENERATION AUTHORSHIP CHECKLIST — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Before generation, every requirement must receive a supported PASS. Required-property questions must answer YES; prohibited-inheritance questions beginning “Does any character inherit/reuse” must answer NO. UNKNOWN or an unsupported assertion blocks generation. Conditional checks may be N/A only with a recorded applicability reason.

##### 5.11.82.1 Character independence

- Is Character 01 independently authored?
- Is Character 02 independently authored?
- Is Character 03 independently authored?
- Is Character 04 independently authored?
- Is Character 05 independently authored?
- Does any character inherit another's body architecture?
- Does any character reuse another's fusion architecture?
- Does any character reuse another's silhouette architecture?
- Does any character reuse another's appendage architecture?
- Does any character reuse another's aperture architecture?
- Does any character reuse another's face architecture?
- Does any character reuse another's colour recipe?

Any YES to a prohibited-inheritance question causes pre-generation structural revision. A shared mandatory category alone is not evidence of inherited construction.

##### 5.11.82.2 Concept materialization

- Does every concept physically alter the character?
- Is every concept materially embodied rather than represented by a token or external carrier?
- Is every concept's complete major recognizable vocabulary preserved?
- Are concepts fused before body construction?
- Does every primary-concept pair have a genuine physical interaction?
- Is an unmistakable complete-set higher-order construction present for three or more concepts?
- Are the important shared consequences readable at macro or strong meso scale?
- Is concept recognition preserved without maximum saturation?
- Does concept recognition survive removal of colour-only and optional-cosmetic cues?
- Does concept recognition survive external-system removal?
- Does the body itself identify the combination?

Any NO or UNCERTAIN answer blocks generation.

##### 5.11.82.3 Body differentiation

- Do the five characters have perceptually distinct full-body proportion regimes?
- Does stature vary where useful without being treated as a mandatory five-slot ladder?
- Do head/neck/shoulder/ribcage/waist/hip/lower-torso region/limb/mass relationships propagate coherently within each regime?
- Do they differ in torso architecture?
- Do they differ in chest architecture?
- Do they differ in hip/lower-torso architecture?
- Do they differ in limb architecture?
- Do they differ in skeletal architecture?
- Do they differ in internal topology?
- Do they differ in aperture architecture where applicable?
- Do they differ in appendage architecture where applicable?
- Do they differ in fusion locus?
- Do they differ in concept-interaction architecture?
- Do they differ in asymmetry?
- Do they differ in silhouette?
- Do they differ in face identity?
- Do they differ in character-identity finishing?
- Do they differ in material architecture?
- Do they differ in colour architecture?

The answer must be a concrete difference, not a general statement that the characters are different.

##### 5.11.82.4 Aesthetic completion

- Could every character independently function as a principal anime/manhwa character?
- Is every face fully developed?
- Is every hair design deliberately authored?
- Is every colour system deliberate?
- Is every material system deliberate?
- Is every silhouette refined?
- Is every unusual structure intentional?
- Is the most unusual character still polished?
- Is the smallest character still polished?
- Is the tallest character still polished?

Any weak character blocks generation.

#### 5.11.83 PRE-GENERATION FORMAT CHECKLIST — HARD / ABSOLUTE

For each initial sheet:

- one continuous exact 16:9 landscape canvas;
- exactly five left-to-right character regions;
- exactly five dominant uncropped full-body true-front views;
- exactly five smaller full-body exact 90-degree profiles;
- exactly five smaller full-body direct-rear views;
- exactly fifteen full-body figure depictions total;
- every view strictly neutral standing and same-identity consistent;
- no raised arms, glamour poses, contrapposto, 3/4 camera, or pose-based differentiation;
- complete head/hair/hands/feet and all mandatory horns, wings, tails, appendages, body-bound identity structures, and other extents visible;
- no overlap that confuses ownership;
- chest/lower-torso presentation surface is resolved solely through intrinsic concept-derived body materialization, with no separate studio/reference layer, neutral underlayer, or outfit-like coverage;
- intrinsic concept-derived body systems remain visible/readable and are never replaced by presentation clothing;
- no Phase-2 outfit architecture;
- no decorative page modules, environment, symbolic props, or emitted concept phenomena;
- plain uninterrupted white field;
- active 2D drawing/material grammar preserved.

Failure of a required profile/rear view is a format/presentation failure, not permission to redesign the frozen character.
#### 5.11.84 PRE-GENERATION 2D STYLE CHECKLIST — HARD / ABSOLUTE

For every generation:

- Have the embedded 2D parameters actually been resolved?
- Is its line language encoded?
- Is its face grammar encoded?
- Is its eye grammar encoded?
- Is its hair grammar encoded?
- Is its body grammar encoded?
- Is its colour language encoded?
- Is its shading language encoded?
- Is its highlight language encoded?
- Is its material illustration language encoded?
- Is its texture frequency encoded?
- Is its edge behaviour encoded?
- Is its detail hierarchy encoded?
- Is its background simplification encoded?
- Is the renderer being asked to draw rather than simulate a 3D object?
- Are contour authority, shadow grouping, and illustrated material behavior stated strongly enough to prevent soft volumetric drift?
- Are the five character payloads pairwise distinct in body regime, height/read, fusion locus, colour strategy, transformation ratio, and carrier grammar rather than being one base solution with variations?
- Is the 2D style active rather than optional?

Any failure blocks generation.
#### 5.11.85 POST-RENDER INDEPENDENCE AUDIT — HARD / ABSOLUTE

After rendering, inspect every pair of characters.

For each pair, ask:

> If all colour, hair styling, cosmetics, markings, lighting, pose, expression, page presentation, and micro-detail are removed mentally, do the two bodies still look like independently authored physical solutions?

If not, record the rendered population as FAILED; do not change or retry either character.

Then ask:

> If their concept names were hidden, would their body constructions still look like different solutions to the same design problem?

If not, record FAILED without post-render correction.

> With all labels hidden and the complete anatomy intact, does the causal construction record demonstrate a different fusion strategy for this member?

If not, record the relevant failure without modifying the rendered character.

#### 5.11.86 POST-RENDER CONCEPT-FUSION AUDIT — HARD / ABSOLUTE

For every character:

1. inspect the complete dominant true-front body;
2. inspect any mandatory exact profile/direct-rear views actually present without treating their absence as failure;
3. confirm that no prohibited independent external presentation carries a concept;
4. verify that every certified body-bound detached identity structure remains fixed to its character and reads as morphology rather than an effect;
5. suppress colour coding as differentiation evidence while preserving material structure;
6. ignore cosmetic finishing as structural evidence while retaining anatomy;
7. mentally remove micro-detail;
8. inspect major body geometry;
9. inspect topology;
10. inspect substrate;
11. inspect appendage roots;
12. inspect appendages and mandatory morphology;
13. inspect silhouette;
14. inspect pairwise cross-concept structures;
15. inspect the complete-set higher-order construction;
16. determine whether the complete concept set remains physically readable at normal sheet scale.

If one concept has become merely cosmetic, record FAILED without correction or retry.

If one concept has been externalized into independent presentation rather than character construction, record FAILED without correction or retry.

If the combination reads as separate concept islands, record FAILED without correction or retry.

If the body reads as generic with attached concept symbols, record FAILED without correction or retry.

#### 5.11.87 POST-RENDER APERTURE AUDIT — HARD / ABSOLUTE

For every relevant character, inspect:

- aperture/cavity count where applicable;
- aperture/cavity locus where applicable;
- aperture/cavity scale where applicable;
- aperture/cavity depth where applicable;
- aperture geometry;
- aperture distribution;
- aperture asymmetry;
- aperture interaction with major anatomy;
- aperture interaction with appendages;
- aperture interaction with substrate;
- aperture interaction with the other concepts.

If all characters use the same recurring aperture solution without an independent physical reason, record FAILED without correction or retry.

The audit must not accept:

> five different leg openings

as meaningful divergence when the underlying fusion logic is unchanged.

The audit must compare the construction logic, not only the locations.

#### 5.11.88 POST-RENDER APPENDAGE AUDIT — HARD / ABSOLUTE

- count;
- pair count;
- origin;
- attachment;
- direction;
- form;
- length;
- thickness;
- branching;
- articulation;
- material;
- asymmetry;
- integration depth;
- interaction with the body;
- interaction with the other concepts.

If multiple characters use the same appendage architecture with cosmetic changes, record FAILED without correction or retry.

If multiple characters use the same number of appendage pairs, same root location, same general form, and same silhouette contribution without an independent reason, record FAILED without correction or retry.

#### 5.11.89 POST-RENDER FACE AUDIT — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Every face is independently inspected for:

- head shape;
- forehead;
- brow;
- eye geometry;
- eye spacing;
- iris;
- pupil;
- eyelids;
- eyebrows;
- nose;
- mouth;
- jaw;
- chin;
- hairline;
- face-framing hair;
- cosmetic finish.

If the faces feel like one face copied five times, the population fails even when the bodies differ.

#### 5.11.90 POST-RENDER COLOUR/MATERIAL AUDIT — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Inspect the population for:

- repeated dominant hue;
- repeated value hierarchy;
- repeated saturation distribution;
- repeated material hierarchy;
- repeated body/appendage relationship;
- repeated hair/body relationship;
- repeated eye/body relationship;
- repeated accent placement;
- repeated cavity treatment.

Monochrome is permitted for a character.

Population-wide palette convergence is not.

#### 5.11.91 FINAL POPULATION ACCEPTANCE PRINCIPLE — HARD / ABSOLUTE

A population passes only when all of the following are simultaneously true:

> **FIVE INDEPENDENT FEMALE CHARACTER IDENTITIES**
>
> +
>
> **FIVE INDEPENDENT BODY ARCHITECTURES**
>
> +
>
> **FIVE INDEPENDENT CONCEPT-FUSION ARCHITECTURES**
>
> +
>
> **FULL-BODY CONCEPT MATERIALIZATION**
>
> +
>
> **MAJOR CROSS-CONCEPT STRUCTURES**
>
> +
>
> **RADICAL BUT DELIBERATE BODY DIFFERENTIATION**
>
> +
>
> **DELIBERATE FACE/HEAD/HAIR AND CHARACTER-IDENTITY SYSTEMS**
>
> +
>
> **DIVERSE MATERIAL AND COLOUR ARCHITECTURES**
>
> +
>
> **DELIBERATE VISUAL HIERARCHY + DENSITY RHYTHM + CONTROLLED INTERNAL CONTRAST**
>
> +
>
> **CONCEPT COMPLETENESS WITHOUT UNIFORM OR MAXIMUM SATURATION**
>
> +
>
> **PREMIUM CHARACTER APPEAL + EXPLICIT AESTHETIC/TASTE PASS FOR ALL FIVE**
>
> +
>
> **MAIN-CHARACTER QUALITY FOR ALL FIVE**
>
> +
>
> **SIMPLE MACRO / INTRICATE MICRO**
>
> +
>
> **NATIVE 2D EMBEDDED-GRAMMAR RENDERING**
>
> +
>
> **ONE DOMINANT TRUE FRONT + ONE EXACT PROFILE + ONE DIRECT REAR PER IDENTITY**
>
> +
>
> **STRICT NEUTRAL STANDING**
>
> +
>
> **EACH SHEET IS ONE CONTINUOUS 16:9 LANDSCAPE SHEET**
>
> +
>
> **THE BROAD PHASE-1 OUTPUT CONTAINS THREE INDEPENDENTLY AUDITED FIVE-CHARACTER SHEETS REPRESENTING ONE FIFTEEN-CHARACTER POPULATION**
>
> =
>
> **ACCEPTABLE PHASE-1 CHARACTER POPULATION OUTPUT**

If any component fails before rendering, revise the affected design or renderer-facing description and revalidate. If a rendered image fails, record FAILED without rebuilding, correction, or retry.

#### 5.11.GLOBAL Global fifteen-character collision review — HARD / ABSOLUTE

After all three within-sheet checkpoint-strength differentiation proofs pass, compare the complete fifteen-character population for high-severity reuse. This global review is not a 105-pair formal divergence matrix and does not impose uniqueness on resolved concept leaves, Design Directions, Era/Culture/Civilization sources, or subordinate source families.

Reject and rebuild the causally responsible character construction when the global population contains:

- a literal duplicate or mirror-equivalent duplicate;
- near-template reuse severe enough that two characters are one underlying body with restyling;
- a repeated body chassis with only palette, hair, marking, surface, or appendage-parameter changes;
- a repeated complete Concept-Fusion Architecture severe enough to make two constructions interchangeable;
- repeated identity construction severe enough that face/head/hair + body regime + fusion organization no longer read as independently authored characters.

Repeated inputs are legal. Repeated autonomous primary leaves are legal. Repeated Design Directions are legal. The forbidden condition is repeated **character construction**. Record this review internally; do not serialize its proof language to the renderer.

### 5.12 Simple macro, intricate micro, and main-character quality

#### 5.12.1 Macro first

The image must be readable immediately at normal sheet viewing scale.

Macro structure must clearly communicate:

- five distinct silhouettes;
- perceptually distinct body/proportion regimes;
- stature/scale differences where they are part of those regimes;
- major body architecture and mass distribution;
- chest, torso, waist, hip/lower-torso region, and hip relationships;
- major limbs and joint rhythm;
- dominant visual hook or hooks;
- major or strong meso-scale concept/fusion structures;
- major appendages **where present**;
- major cavities/negative spaces **where present**;
- major material/substrate regions and boundaries;
- primary asymmetry where used;
- face/head/hair identity as one of the strongest character reads.

Macro readability is about clear organization, not forcing every possible system into the silhouette.

#### 5.12.2 Micro second

Only after macro readability is secure may the design add:

- fine anatomical transitions;
- secondary segmentation;
- fine topology;
- small cavities;
- attachment detail;
- controlled surface transitions;
- fine material transitions;
- small structural motifs.

Micro-detail MUST reinforce macro structure.

Micro-detail must never obscure silhouette, body architecture, concept signatures, or view readability.

#### 5.12.3 Simple macro / intricate micro execution hierarchy — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Simple macro / intricate micro is a strict rendering hierarchy, not a suggestion to reduce detail.

The macro layer must be structurally simple enough that the body can be understood immediately while still being sufficiently distinctive to identify the five different physical solutions.

The micro layer may be extremely intricate, but it must remain subordinate to the macro architecture.

##### 5.12.3.1 Required macro reading order

At normal viewing scale, the intended hierarchy is:

1. overall silhouette and immediately dominant visual hook;
2. face/head/eyes/hair as one of the strongest identity focal regions;
3. major body regime, height, proportions, torso/chest/hip/lower-torso region relationships;
4. dominant concept-fusion structure or small set of dominant shared structures;
5. major appendage, cavity, and topology architecture where present;
6. major material, value, and colour masses and controlled contrasts;
7. supporting concept-derived structures;
8. secondary structure;
9. fine topology/material texture;
10. micro-surface detail.

This is a design-priority hierarchy, not a rigid claim that every viewer must look at the exact same pixel first. A spectacular character-specific hook may lead the first read, but it must direct attention back into a developed face/head identity rather than burying the character beneath anatomy or effects.

##### 5.12.3.2 Macro complexity control — HARD

A complex macro arrangement that destroys immediate body readability is a failure even when the individual structures are conceptually correct.

The compiler must simplify the arrangement of large masses while preserving the complexity of the underlying body construction.

This means:

- major structures remain separated enough to read;
- the silhouette remains legible;
- major appendages remain individually readable;
- cavities remain visually intelligible;
- body proportions remain inspectable;
- concept signatures remain visible;
- no dense cluster of micro-structures is allowed to become the primary macro silhouette;
- the five bodies remain distinguishable from one another at thumbnail scale.

##### 5.12.3.3 Micro complexity is subordinate — HARD

Once macro structure is established, micro-detail may substantially increase construction richness through:

- fine anatomical transitions;
- small-scale topology;
- structural segmentation;
- material transitions;
- attachment detail;
- secondary cavities;
- fine surface architecture;
- controlled repetition;
- small-scale asymmetry;
- fine linework.

Micro-detail must reinforce the already-resolved body rather than introduce an unrelated second design layer.

##### 5.12.3.4 Micro-detail cannot rescue a weak macro — HARD

If a character only becomes recognizable after zooming into fine details, the macro construction is insufficient.

Before rendering, revise the major body architecture rather than add more micro-detail. If weak macro construction appears only in the rendered image, record FAILED without rebuilding it.

##### 5.12.3.5 Concept readability must exist at macro or strong meso scale — HARD

Each supplied primary concept must have at least one strong **macro or strong meso-scale** physical/identity-bearing manifestation, together with its required interaction evidence.

The complete concept combination must therefore be recognizable before the viewer depends on the smallest details.

Not every concept must independently dominate the outer silhouette. A concept may be carried most strongly by substrate, face/head/hair, a major material system, topology, internal structure, appendage architecture, or another large/meso identity-bearing construction.

Micro-detail may reinforce concept identity but may never be the sole mechanism through which a supplied primary concept is identifiable.

#### 5.12.4 Detail-density prohibition

Reject:

- detail soup;
- excessive spikes/fragments;
- excessive overlapping effects;
- dense surface noise;
- uncontrolled reflections;
- excessive bloom/glare;
- decorative complexity that destroys body readability.

Complexity belongs inside the resolved body. It must not become visual noise around it.

#### 5.12.5 2D-native rendering execution lock — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The required visual language is an intentionally drawn 2D anime/manhwa illustration language. This is an execution requirement, not a preference to be balanced against another rendering approach.

The embedded 2D rendering specification is always active, and the renderer MUST explicitly target its resolved drawing language.

The generator MUST NOT select a 3D, CGI, PBR, photoreal, game-render, or physically simulated appearance merely because such an appearance can represent complex materials or anatomy.

The desired result must read as drawn 2D artwork immediately, before inspection of micro-detail. The drawing itself—not only the surface finish—must read as 2D-designed and line-led; a 3D/CGI mannequin with anime polish does not pass.

##### 5.12.5.1 2D construction signals — HARD

The renderer-facing package must positively serialize:

- drawn lineart;
- deliberate contour hierarchy;
- graphic shape construction;
- illustrated anatomical planes;
- controlled cel-shaded grouping;
- restrained drawn gradients;
- hand-designed highlight shapes;
- hand-designed reflective accents;
- stylized material representation;
- crisp illustrated edges;
- controlled 2D value masses;
- coherent anime/manhwa facial construction;
- deliberate illustrated surface detail;
- high-resolution clean finish.

The rendering must communicate design through drawn shapes and linework rather than simulated physical optics.

##### 5.12.5.2 2D is not a negative constraint — HARD

The renderer-facing package must not depend on a long list of forbidden rendering labels.

The primary serialization must positively describe the desired 2D rendering language.

Validator-level drift checks may still identify an incorrect rendering family after generation, but the generation call itself must be dominated by the positive 2D construction specification.

This distinction exists to reduce irrelevant lexical interference while preserving the mandatory rendering target.

##### 5.12.5.3 No 3D fallback for complex materials — HARD

Complex concept-derived materials, aperture interiors, heterogeneous substrates, translucent structures, reflective surfaces, or intricate appendages MUST be represented through deliberate 2D illustration techniques. Do not solve these materials by making them look more sculpted, more rendered, or more physically simulated than the surrounding illustration.

The renderer must not solve complexity by switching to volumetric CGI, physically based materials, photographic lighting, or three-dimensional asset-rendering conventions.
##### 5.12.5.4 2D quality applies equally to all fifteen initial characters — HARD

The rendering quality of Characters 01–15 must remain at the same professional 2D class across all three sheets.

Later-numbered characters or later sheets may not become rougher, flatter, blurrier, less detailed, or more three-dimensional because the population is larger.

#### 5.12.6 Renderer-language clarity and positive serialization — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Renderer-facing language must preserve the certified design while minimizing unnecessary semantic ambiguity.

The workflow must distinguish:

> **INTERNAL DESIGN SPECIFICATION**

> **RENDERER-FACING SERIALIZATION**.

The internal specification may contain detailed validation terminology. The renderer-facing serialization must use concrete, professional, neutral visual language that preserves the same physical construction.

##### 5.12.6.1 No wording-driven simplification — HARD

A suspected lexical risk MUST NOT cause the compiler to remove a certified structural requirement.

> identify risky wording → replace it with neutral concrete visual wording → recheck structural equivalence → preserve the design.

**INVALID:**

> identify risky wording → delete the concept consequence → generate a weaker body.

##### 5.12.6.2 No negative-prompt or prohibition dumping — HARD

The renderer-facing package must not be overloaded with long chains of prohibition language.

Positive serialization must carry the majority of the design information.

Negative validation remains active internally and during post-render audit.

##### 5.12.6.3 Concrete wording over semantic labels — HARD

Where a supplied concept name could be interpreted broadly, the renderer-facing package must serialize the already-resolved physical consequence rather than relying on the label.

A topology-oriented concept is serialized through its certified body topology; an organizational concept through its certified structural organization; a morphology concept through its certified integrated morphology. Renderer cues describe the resolved construction rather than repeating source labels.

The concept name may remain as metadata, but it must never be the sole instruction.

##### 5.12.6.4 Neutral body wording must remain anatomically specific — HARD

Renderer-facing rewording must not collapse the design into “humanoid,” “generic figure,” “creature-like person,” or other ambiguous identity language.

The renderer-facing identity remains an explicitly female anime/manhwa character with the certified body architecture.

##### 5.12.6.5 Submission and interpretation failures preserve frozen design — HARD

If generation does not execute or the returned image materially misinterprets the renderer package, preserve the complete Frozen Character Specification, Clean Renderer-Facing Description, submission record, and actual attempt outcome. A non-executed or serialization-layer attempt may use same-design serializer correction under §10.2. The correction may clarify visual serialization but cannot redesign the character, and any new image call requires a new explicit user `RENDER` command.
#### 5.12.7 Macro must be architecturally simple

Simple macro does not mean simple character.

It means that the major design masses are organized clearly enough to be understood immediately.

An extremely intricate character can still have a simple macro.

The compiler MUST therefore separate:

- primary silhouette masses;
- secondary structural masses;
- tertiary structural detail;
- micro-detail.

A design may contain enormous micro-detail.

The primary silhouette must remain clear.

The major concept structures must remain clear.

The face must remain clear.

The major appendages must remain clear where present.

The major cavities must remain clear where present.

#### 5.12.8 Micro must stay inside the body design

Micro-detail must enrich:

- feather/fibre structures;
- scales/plates;
- material transitions;
- eye detail;
- hair detail;
- fine topology;
- fine appendage articulation;
- surface patterning.

It must not generate an external cloud of detail around the body.

Complexity belongs inside the resolved design.

#### 5.12.9 Macro saturation audit

Reject any character when:

- micro-detail occupies the same visual weight as the silhouette;
- repeated small elements create an accidental new macro shape;
- highlights break the silhouette into noise;
- appendage fragments overlap so heavily that their parent structures cannot be identified;
- material transitions become a patchwork unrelated to major forms;
- concept details multiply without architectural hierarchy.

Before rendering, reorganize the macro while preserving required construction and micro detail. If the saturation is observed only in the actual output, record FAILED without modifying it.

Before accepting a generated character, ask all of the following independently for each of the five characters:

##### 5.12.9.1 Identity

- Does this read immediately as a female anime/manhwa character?
- Does the face have independent identity?
- Does the hair have independent architecture?
- Does the body have independent architecture?
- Does the silhouette have independent authorship?

##### 5.12.9.2 Concept fusion

- Can the complete supplied concept set be recognized from the body alone?
- Are concepts fused rather than partitioned?
- Are there higher-order structures caused by concept interaction?
- Does each concept materially alter more than one relevant system where appropriate?
- Are the major concept structures prominent enough at macro or strong meso scale to remain unmistakable without relying on micro-detail?

##### 5.12.9.3 Differentiation

- Would this still look unrelated to the other four after stripping cosmetics and colour?
- Does the body construction differ?
- Does the fusion architecture differ?
- Does the appendage architecture differ?
- Does the aperture/topology architecture differ where applicable?
- Does the face differ?
- Does the material architecture differ?
- Does the colour architecture differ?

##### 5.12.9.4 Quality

- Is this independently strong enough to be the main character of an anime/manhwa or a premium gacha character?
- Is the body aesthetically deliberate rather than random?
- Is this a genuinely strong design rather than merely the first technically valid construction?
- Is the face/head memorable, attractive, and identity-bearing?
- Is the hair a deliberate identity and silhouette system?
- Is there a clear first/second/third visual hierarchy?
- Are there one or more memorable visual hooks?
- Does concept coverage use deliberate density rhythm rather than uniform saturation?
- Has concept completeness been achieved without adding structures merely because anatomical space remains?
- Does each important structure earn its place and strengthen the complete character?
- Is there useful controlled contrast inside the design?
- Is a petite solution compact rather than caricaturally compressed?
- Is the silhouette readable before micro-detail?
- Is micro-detail subordinate to the macro?
- Are identity finishing elements completing rather than rescuing the identity?
