- Would the character still attract attention in a roster of hundreds of professionally designed characters?

##### 5.12.9.5 Presentation

- Are there exactly five identities?
- Does each identity have one large complete full-body true-front depiction?
- Is every principal front directly frontal and strictly neutral standing?
- Are all mandatory appendages and body-bound identity structures visible within the character's owned region without cropping?
- Does each target sheet remain exactly one 16:9 landscape analytical field?
- Is the background plain white and visually quiet?
- Are concept-derived intrinsic body surface systems preserved as character identity rather than replaced by presentation clothing?
- Is chest/lower-torso presentation surface resolved solely through intrinsic concept-derived body materialization, with no separate studio/reference layer, neutral underlayer, or outfit-like coverage?
- Is the sheet free of Phase-2 outfit architecture, glamour posing, scenery, symbolic concept props, emitted concept phenomena, and decorative page modules?
- Are all five exact profiles and all five direct rears present, same-body, neutral, compact, and clearly subordinate to the five principal fronts?

##### 5.12.9.6 Rendering

- Does the image visibly read as drawn 2D immediately?
- Does it use the embedded 2D drawing language?
- Does material complexity remain illustrated rather than physically simulated?
- Is every character rendered at the same professional quality floor?

Any failure blocks a pre-render pass and requires pre-render revision, or records a rendered result as FAILED without correction or retry, according to the evaluation stage.

#### 5.12.10 STRUCTURAL SATURATION REQUIRES RE-DESIGN, NOT BLURRING — HARD / ABSOLUTE

When structural density threatens readability, do not solve the problem by:

- blurring;
- lowering resolution;
- hiding structures;
- reducing concept prominence;
- reducing body differentiation;
- reducing appendage count automatically;
- removing major concept materialization automatically;
- pushing structures into micro-detail.

- simplify the macro silhouette;
- consolidate secondary structures;
- reduce unnecessary overlap;
- establish clear negative spaces;
- separate major structural layers;
- clarify attachment;
- preserve major concept structures;
- retain concept prominence;
- retain body uniqueness.

The concepts must remain strongly embodied.

The macro must remain simple.

The correction occurs through architectural organization.

##### 5.12.10.1 COMPLETE CONCEPT DISTRIBUTION IS NOT UNIFORM VISUAL SATURATION — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The workflow must not confuse complete concept distribution with good visual composition.

A concept being active throughout the character does not mean that every body region should receive the same amount, scale, density, texture, or type of manifestation. Technically correct concepts can become evenly distributed over the body until the character looks like a female character covered in one repeated material or motif. This is especially dangerous for feathers, scales, crystal, bone, mechanical structures, vegetation, fur, shells, membranes, cavities, repeated markings, or any other concept capable of multiplying across many regions.

That produces concept coverage, but not necessarily character design.

The workflow must therefore deliberately create visual hierarchy. Every character should have a small number of immediately dominant visual hooks, several supporting structures, and quieter regions that allow those important structures to remain readable. The complete concept must still be present, but presence does not mean equal visual intensity everywhere.

A major manifestation may dominate the silhouette. Another may define the face or hair. Another may become an internal structure. Smaller manifestations may reinforce the same concept elsewhere without turning the entire body into one homogeneous texture. The workflow must control distribution, density, scale, and visual emphasis, not merely verify presence.

The designer must actively determine the intended first, second, and third visual reads. The face/head identity should normally remain among those strongest reads. Hair must likewise be treated as a major silhouette and identity system rather than leftover space surrounding the face.

##### 5.12.10.2 DENSITY RHYTHM AND REGIONAL VARIATION — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The body must have rhythm. Useful contrast includes, where appropriate:

- large → small;
- dense → sparse;
- complex → simple;
- hard edge → soft transition;
- symmetrical region → asymmetric interruption;
- smooth material → textured material;
- dark mass → bright focal accent;
- visible structure → quiet uninterrupted anatomy;
- rigid structure → soft or flowing structure.

Repeated-unit systems must therefore vary their regional behavior. If feathers are involved, this does not mean covering every available anatomical region with similarly sized feathers. Feather architecture may shift between enormous primary feathers, dense soft plumage, isolated contour feathers, down-like transitions, bare or visually quiet skin regions, structural feather masses, wing feathers, hair-like feathers, rigid feathers, translucent feathers, damaged feathers, or other materially justified forms.

The same generalized principle applies to scales, crystal, bone, liquid matter, mechanical components, vegetation, cavities, fur, shells, membranes, plates, petals, or any other repeated or distributable concept-derived structure.

##### 5.12.10.3 CONCEPT COMPLETENESS DOES NOT MEAN MAXIMUM CONCEPT SATURATION — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Concept completeness does not mean maximum concept saturation.

Every concept must be unmistakably and materially present, and all required interactions must genuinely exist, but once the concept is strongly readable and structurally integrated, the designer must preserve aesthetic economy.

Additional manifestations should only be added when they strengthen the character.

The workflow must actively avoid over-designing a successful character merely because unused anatomical space remains available.

The optimal character is not the one with the maximum number of concept-derived structures. It is the one where every important structure earns its place in symbiosic interaction with the others.

A quiet region is not an unmaterialized region when the complete body has already been authored under the concept fusion and the concept remains unmistakable through stronger structures elsewhere. Conversely, aesthetic economy cannot be used to justify a generic body carrying one isolated concept token. Complete materialization and selective visual emphasis must pass simultaneously.

Aesthetic economy also cannot be used to remove a characteristic already classified as part of the concept's major recognizable vocabulary. Complete vocabulary preservation, interaction completeness, and selective visual emphasis must pass at the same time.

##### 5.12.10.4 CONTROLLED INTERNAL CONTRAST — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Memorable characters often gain identity from oppositions inside the same design. The workflow must deliberately investigate useful internal contrast rather than allowing every region to share one texture, value, density, geometry, or visual intensity.

Useful contrasts may include extremely soft hair against rigid horns, smooth skin against granular crystal, delicate facial features against enormous appendages, pale material against saturated accents, an elegant silhouette interrupted by one monstrous structure, visible internal anatomy surrounded by otherwise beautiful clean forms, or one highly complex focal structure against broad simple body regions.

These are examples of a generalized compositional principle, not required pairings. The designer must choose contrasts that strengthen the actual character and concept fusion. Contrast introduced only for novelty, without improving the character, does not pass.

##### 5.12.10.5 AESTHETIC SEARCH, TASTE, AND REJECTION STAGE — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Technical validity is only the first filter.

The first physically coherent answer must not automatically become the character. After structural validity is established, the designer must actively evaluate whether the construction is actually beautiful, memorable, charismatic, desirable, coherent, and strong enough to function as a premium main-character or high-value gacha/anime/manhwa design.

Before certification, the designer must compare the valid construction against plausible alternative implementations and reject weaker answers. This does not authorize endless random mutation. It requires purposeful design search inside the valid solution space.

For every character, explicitly ask:

- Is this the most attractive version of this idea that has been found?
- Is there a stronger silhouette or more memorable dominant hook?
- Is the face sufficiently memorable and attractive for this exact identity?
- Is the hair sufficiently distinctive in silhouette, mass, and relationship to the face?
- Are the concept structures helping the female character's design or merely covering her?
- Is the detail concentrated intelligently rather than distributed uniformly?
- Does the character have one or more immediate visual hooks?
- Is there enough useful contrast between materials, shapes, value masses, and visual density?
- Does anything look generic, procedural, over-designed, under-designed, like the obvious first solution, or like a generic AI-default aggregate rather than a deliberately authored premium character design?
- Would this character still attract attention in a roster filled with hundreds of professionally designed characters?

A character that passes structural requirements but fails this aesthetic comparison must be rejected before rendering. The workflow is expected to exercise taste, not merely conformance. Its task is not to preserve every technically valid decision; its task is to search the valid design space for the strongest character.

Attractiveness must itself remain diverse. Premium appeal does not authorize one hidden beauty template. One character may be cute and soft, another severe, another full-figured, another slender and elegant, another muscular, another eerie but beautiful, another glamorous, another dangerous, another regal, another strange and captivating. What they share is not one body, face, hair, or beauty archetype. What they share is the feeling that every visual decision was made deliberately by an exceptional character designer.

##### 5.12.10.6 FINAL CHARACTER-REFINEMENT PASS — HARD / ABSOLUTE

A structurally valid character is still unfinished until the complete design has been refined as one visual object. Before the Frozen Character Specification is certified for handoff, perform a final refinement pass that judges the character at three distances: thumbnail silhouette, normal full-body reading, and face/identity reading.

At thumbnail scale, the silhouette, dominant hook, and large concept masses must read immediately. At normal full-body scale, body regime, concept fusion, material hierarchy, colour/value structure, appendage/topology organization, and density rhythm must feel intentional rather than accumulated. At face/identity scale, face geometry, eyes, hair framing, hair mass, and the chosen appeal mode must feel authored and memorable rather than default.

Refine awkward joins, tangent collisions, arbitrary protrusions, weak appendage roots, accidental symmetry, repetitive motif coverage, unresolved material transitions, low-value micro-detail, generic hair, generic facial construction, and any body region that looks like an uncompleted placeholder. Simplification is permitted when it strengthens the design and preserves complete recognizable vocabulary and required fusion.

The five candidates are then compared side by side. The weakest candidate is not allowed to pass merely because it satisfies checklists. If one looks one refinement pass behind the others, return that candidate to design refinement before Frozen Character Specification certification.

The renderer receives only the post-refinement design state. No image-generation call may be used as a discovery sketch, quality probe, or substitute for this refinement work.

#### 5.12.11 NO COUNTERPRODUCTIVE NEGATIVE-PROMPT / PROHIBITION DUMPING — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Internal failure criteria remain extensive.

The final renderer-facing description/context must not simply copy every internal failure criterion into a negative prompt or prohibition list.

The actual positive design must carry the intended image.

Internal gates determine whether generation is allowed.

Positive serialization determines what generation should create.

This separation must remain absolute.

#### 5.12.12 GENERATOR ESCAPE IS PROHIBITED — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The following phrases, by themselves, carry no structural information:

- make five different characters;
- vary the anatomy;
- create unique bodies;
- make the concepts fused;
- make them more original;
- make them extreme;
- create diverse appendages;
- make the colours different;
- improve the faces;
- use a 2D style.

These may appear as secondary reminders.

They may never substitute for the actual character-specific specifications.

Every required difference must be written as concrete physical design information.

Every concept manifestation must be written as concrete physical design information.

Every format requirement must be written as concrete page geometry.

Every quality requirement must be written as concrete rendering behaviour.

### 5.13 Neutral standing and exact analytical view architecture

#### 5.13.1 True-front geometric camera lock — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The dominant view is an analytical front elevation.

It is not a perspective hero view.

It is not a 3/4 presentation.

It is not a fashion pose.

It is not a low-angle composition.

It is not a high-angle composition.

It is not a tilted camera.

The front view is the principal reference view from which:

- face;
- shoulders;
- chest;
- torso;
- waist;
- pelvis;
- hips;
- limbs;
- appendages;
- cavities;
- asymmetry;
- silhouette

can be inspected.

#### 5.13.2 Lower-row isolation — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The smaller profile and rear views form the complete subordinate view row.

Nothing else is permitted to compete with that row.

No additional front belongs in the row.

No micro-study belongs in the row.

No colour strip belongs in the row.

No eye crop belongs in the row.

No face crop belongs in the row.

No material crop belongs in the row.

No concept icon belongs in the row.

No alternate pose belongs in the row.

No secondary character belongs in the row.

#### 5.13.3 View hierarchy — HARD

For every character region:

> **FRONT = PRIMARY ANALYTICAL BODY VIEW**
>
> **PROFILE = SECONDARY STRUCTURAL VIEW**
>
> **REAR = SECONDARY STRUCTURAL VIEW**

The front is larger.

The profile and rear are smaller.

The three views remain part of one body identity.

#### 5.13.4 Front-view consistency check — HARD

Before accepting a rendered sheet, compare the dominant front against:

- the body blueprint;
- the certified silhouette;
- the certified proportion system;
- the certified fusion architecture.

Reject if perspective distortion causes the front to become a different perceived body.

Reject if the camera angle makes the front materially resemble a 3/4 view.

Reject if body parts are hidden in a manner that prevents structural inspection.

The dominant view is a true frontal view.

It must show the character directly facing the viewer with the frontal body plane readable.

A 3/4 view is NOT a front view.

Any visible deliberate yaw that exposes unequal front/side planes fails the front-view requirement.

The front view must permit thorough analysis of:

- face;
- eyes;
- shoulders;
- torso;
- chest architecture;
- waist;
- pelvis;
- hips;
- limbs;
- appendage origins;
- body topology;
- silhouette.

The subordinate views are:

> **ONE EXACT 90-DEGREE SIDE VIEW + ONE DIRECT REAR VIEW**.

There is NO subordinate front view.

There is NO alternate front.

There is NO second front.

There is NO 3/4 view anywhere in the analytical view set.

There are NO additional lower-row character views beyond the certified side and rear views.

#### 5.13.5 Neutral standing is mandatory

Gyaru, slyness, confidence, attitude, fire, or any other supplied concept does not authorize posing.

Hands remain naturally neutral.

Legs remain naturally positioned for analysis.

The spine remains neutral.

The head remains aligned for the specified view.

The character does not lean, twist, gesture, crouch, strut, point, wink through pose, or perform an action.

Concept identity comes from construction, not posing.

The entire sheet is a character-analysis sheet.

No concept presentation layer may be added outside the characters.

No environment.

No scenery.

No atmospheric effect.

No detached elemental phenomenon.

No floating concept icon.

No symbol panel.

No title block consuming character space.

No palette board.

No accessory board.

No unrelated close-up board.

No lower-row module that was not explicitly certified.

No subordinate front.

The white analytical field remains clean.
### 5.14 Integrated construction-quality calibration

Consider a worked feather-constituted female body with intrinsic layered structures, clear anatomical roots, and a readable female silhouette. The example explains how concept-derived matter can materially form anatomy.

It is NOT a character template.

It is NOT permission to copy its exact body.

It is NOT permission to import its exact costume.

It is NOT permission to import its exact colour palette.

It is NOT permission to import its exact head structure.

It is NOT permission to turn every future concept into feathers or wings.

Its authority is limited to the visible construction methodology described below.

#### 5.14.1 Worked construction principle: body remains the character

The worked construction keeps a clearly readable central female character while the major concept-derived structures become part of her visible physical architecture.

The body is not a small conventional figure surrounded by concept decoration.

The concept-derived structures occupy substantial physical volume around and across the body.

They alter the silhouette.

They alter the apparent mass distribution.

They alter the transitions between body regions.

They remain attached to the character.

They remain readable in the character's immediate silhouette.

This is the required methodological principle:

> **CONCEPT MATERIALIZATION MUST CHANGE THE CHARACTER, NOT MERELY DECORATE THE CHARACTER.**

The worked construction demonstrates that a major concept can occupy more than one anatomical region.

The visible feather structures are not confined to a single tiny decorative point.

Large wing structures originate from the upper body and back region.

Feather masses also occur around other body regions and overlap the central anatomy.

The resulting construction makes the concept a distributed bodily system rather than one localized ornament.

Universal rule:

A concept capable of affecting multiple body regions should be allowed to do so.

Do not repeatedly assign the same concept to one fixed anatomical location across all five characters.

The worked construction contains large primary structures and smaller subordinate feather structures.

The large structures establish the macro silhouette.

Smaller structures refine the surfaces and transitions.

This is a direct example of:

> **SIMPLE MACRO → INTRICATE MICRO**.

The workflow MUST reproduce the structural principle, not the literal feather content.

For every concept set:

- a small number of dominant character/fusion structures or material systems must establish the macro or strong meso read;
- supporting structures are added where they strengthen those dominant forms;
- tertiary details remain subordinate;
- micro-detail must never replace coherent macro architecture;
- a concept that is already complete and unmistakable is not repeated merely to occupy more anatomy.

#### 5.14.2 Worked construction principle: repeated units become a physical system

The worked construction’s feathers are individually visible, but they are not random noise.

They form grouped masses.

They overlap.

They taper.

They follow directional organization.

They create larger structural shapes.

When a concept naturally produces repeated structural units, those units must be organized into coherent anatomical systems rather than scattered as decorative texture.

This applies universally to any repeated concept-derived element, including but not limited to:

- scales;
- plates;
- petals;
- fibers;
- crystalline facets;
- apertures;
- ridges;
- membranes;
- strands;
- segments;
- layered surfaces;
- other repeated structural units.

#### 5.14.3 Worked construction principle: material and colour can remain integrated

The worked construction uses contrasting light and dark feather masses while retaining a coherent body.

The distinction is carried through the physical structures themselves.

Material and colour variation may reinforce concept architecture, but they must never become substitutes for it.

A palette change over the same body is not concept fusion.

A material change without topology change is not sufficient when the supplied concepts demand structural consequences.

The worked construction contains complex directional and mass relationships that prevent the concept-derived structures from reading as a perfectly mirrored decorative pair.

When the concept permits asymmetry, asymmetry must be actively authored rather than left to chance.

Vary independently:

- left/right structural mass;
- attachment height;
- attachment angle;
- length;
- thickness;
- curvature;
- number of secondary structures;
- density;
- material;
- direction;
- overlap;
- termination shape.

Asymmetry must contribute to a refined character design and not merely create visual disorder.

The worked construction’s major structures visually intersect, overlap, frame, and emerge around the body.

They are not simply placed behind a generic character.

Concept-derived structures must have meaningful anatomical relationships with the body.

They may alter:

- attachment points;
- local surface topology;
- silhouette;
- body segmentation;
- transitions between regions;
- perceived mass;
- structural continuity.

The exact implementation is concept-dependent.

The methodological requirement is universal.

#### 5.14.4 Construction-to-workflow application rule

The construction is therefore expressed as the following reusable construction grammar:

> **MAJOR CONCEPT → STRONG BODY-INTEGRATED SIGNATURE OR SHARED STRUCTURAL SYSTEM**
>
> **MAJOR SYSTEM → COHERENT ANATOMICAL ATTACHMENT / TRANSITION LOGIC, SINGLE OR MULTIPLE AS THE DESIGN REQUIRES**
>
> **PRIMARY STRUCTURE / MATERIAL SYSTEM → SUPPORTING STRUCTURES WHERE USEFUL → TERTIARY DETAIL**
>
> **STRUCTURAL UNITS → GROUPED DIRECTIONAL MASSES**
>
> **MASS → SILHOUETTE**
>
> **SILHOUETTE → CHARACTER IDENTITY**
>
> **MATERIAL + COLOUR → REINFORCEMENT OF STRUCTURE**
>
> **ASYMMETRY → CHARACTER-SPECIFIC DESIGN**
>
> **A × B × … × N → COMPLETE-SET HIGHER-ORDER BODY CONSEQUENCE WHEN N ≥ 3**.

No future concept should be forced into the literal feather solution.

Every future concept must receive its own equivalent construction grammar.

### 5.14A Sheet-local FINAL RENDER CONTEXT compilation — HARD / ABSOLUTE

For the initial Broad Phase-1 population, compile exactly three internal sheet-local FINAL RENDER CONTEXTS after all fifteen designs have passed final refinement, robustness, differentiation, freeze, and validation. Each context is bound to exactly five frozen current Character/Revision IDs and is not a file-handoff layer.

Serialize each initial-sheet context in this exact order:

1. immutable 16:9 presentation envelope and five-region / three-view geometry;
2. one concise shared intrinsic-coverage paragraph;
3. one active illustrated 2D drawing/material grammar paragraph;
4. exactly five complete shortest-lossless visible character payloads in left-to-right order;
5. one cross-view identity-consistency lock;
6. one terminal layout/extent lock.

The shared intrinsic-coverage paragraph is equivalent in visual meaning to:

> **Each figure's chest and lower-torso presentation surface is formed by its own opaque intrinsic concept-derived constituent body matter, anatomically continuous with the surrounding torso, waist, hip/lower-torso region, and concept-bearing structures. Surface geometry follows the exact certified body form and material logic while preserving the full readability of appendage roots, transformed anatomy, cavities/topology, intrinsic material transitions, markings, and all other concept-bearing systems at their actual roots, extents, boundaries, and silhouette.**

The context never contains other sheets' characters; selector/random bookkeeping; SC/DD IDs; Era/Culture/Civilization or Sub-concept labels; raw concept formulas; validation/proof prose; rejected alternatives; reasoning; changelog data; or unrelated conversation history. It preserves body regime/proportions, silhouette, face, eyes, hair, complete-fusion construction, appendage count/root/form, topology/cavities, material zones, asymmetry, major markings, palette/value architecture, and all other render-critical macro/strong-meso decisions.

No mandatory `.txt`/`.md` renderer artifact is created. An optional export requested by the user is inspection/copy material only and can never become more authoritative than frozen runtime state and the current FINAL RENDER CONTEXT.

### 5.15 Phase-1 positive renderer serialization

#### 5.15.1 Frozen Character Specification — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Every render-ready character exists internally as a complete **Frozen Character Specification** before image generation. This is the authoritative character design state whether or not the user asks to see it.

It contains every identity-bearing fact needed to reconstruct the exact character without invention: female identity; proportions and silhouette; face/head/eyes/hair; torso, limb, hand, foot, skeletal and mass architecture; concept-fusion structures; appendages; topology/cavities/channels; materials and boundaries; colour/value architecture; concept-integrated intrinsic coverage; asymmetry; subordinate influences; and cross-view consistency anchors.

The Frozen Character Specification remains exhaustive. It is not constrained to image-prompt length and may retain analytical evidence needed by the workflow. Written specifications are displayed only when the user explicitly asks to inspect, compare, edit, or archive them.

#### 5.15.2 Clean Renderer-Facing Description — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Compile one concise, positive, self-contained render block for each frozen character. Each block carries the visible result, not the proof transcript.

The final context begins with the exact five-character 16:9 true-front neutral-standing format and then serializes each character independently through body regime → face/head/hair → major proportions → mandatory major morphology → complete fused structures → topology/appendages → material hierarchy → intrinsic surface architecture → subordinate influences → colour/value → readable micro-detail.

**Mandatory-feature retention.** Every major-vocabulary item classified for a primary concept appears explicitly in that character's block with its resolved count/origin/scale/form/material/topology. Never rely on the renderer to remember that another character had wings, horns, a tail, or another iconic structure.

**Positive serialization efficiency — HARD.** State what exists, where it originates or body-locally anchors, how large it is, what material it uses, how it transitions, and what dominates the silhouette. Omit audit language, rejected alternatives, repeated concept labels, and scale-invisible bookkeeping before removing any visible identity feature.

For a five-character Phase-1 sheet, compactness is useful only when it is lossless. There is no word target that authorizes deletion of mandatory anatomy or higher-order fusion.

#### 5.15.3 Renderer inference minimization — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The renderer receives no unresolved macro or strong-meso design choices. Before handoff, resolve internally and serialize at useful image scale:

- stature and overall feminine proportion regime;
- shoulder/torso/leg relationship and major silhouette masses;
- face geometry, eye identity, and hair mass;
- horn, wing, tail, claw, fin, ear, crest, or other appendage count/origin/geometry;
- topology, cavities, channels, split structures, and continuity;
- principal materials and transitions;
- dominant colour/value placement;
- render-critical intrinsic-coverage geometry and its continuity with unusual body-owned structures;
- all view-critical asymmetry;
- dominant-front geometry plus mandatory exact-profile/direct-rear geometry and cross-view consistency anchors.

Fine line texture, tiny pores, individual grains, and comparable microfinish may be realized illustratively within those frozen systems. The renderer is never asked to choose a new body architecture, appendage system, face identity, topology, or presentation silhouette.

#### 5.15.4 Phase-1 renderer preflight — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Run after serialization to determine whether the frozen Phase-1 revision is eligible to proceed to the Renderer Capability Gate. Re-run the integrity-sensitive portions immediately before any explicit `RENDER` call.

**Geometry and presentation envelope.** Confirm the certified Phase-1 presentation geometry and active figure count, complete full-body framing, true-front camera for every principal front, head and torso directly forward, shoulders and hip/lower-torso unposed, both arms naturally hanging with inspection clearance, relaxed hands, stable naturally supporting legs, and the certified plain analytical field. Confirm these facts occupy Block 1 of the FINAL RENDER CONTEXT before any character-specific payload and that no later clause conflicts with them. For each initial sheet-local context, additionally confirm the certified delivered-sheet ratio is exact 16:9, exactly five left-to-right character regions, five dominant full-body true fronts, five exact 90-degree profiles, five direct rears, complete body extents, and non-overlapping ownership between characters. Native renderer output need not expose exact 16:9 control when the closest supported landscape orientation/ratio plus deterministic non-generative white-canvas fitting or padding can produce the exact delivered ratio without cropping, regenerating, redesigning, non-uniformly rescaling, or materially altering character content. Confirm every subordinate profile/rear is exact, identity-consistent, compact, and does not reduce the principal fronts below useful analytical scale.

**Design completeness.** Confirm all characters are final-refined; every primary concept remains unmistakable at macro or strong-meso scale; every required interaction exists; female identity and individual face/hair identity remain strong.

**Intrinsic coverage and body membership.** Confirm every character's concept-integrated intrinsic coverage was resolved before freeze; it follows the character's exact form; it remains body-owned and materially continuous with the active fusion; it preserves silhouette, proportions, appendage roots, topology, and high-intensity character presence. For every complex intrinsic region, confirm the Clean Renderer-Facing Description states body origin, continuity/path, attachment or root logic, material transition, and termination strongly enough that the structure remains unambiguously part of the body rather than a separate worn system. Any independently attached decorative construction not already frozen as certified body-bound identity morphology fails preflight.

**Renderer vocabulary.** Confirm the Clean Renderer-Facing Description contains ordinary positive visual language rather than internal analysis terminology; intrinsic structures are described as ordinary anatomical/material character features with concrete geometry and continuity; overall proportions are stated as design facts rather than inspection goals; abstract aesthetic/temperament/direction descriptors have been replaced by their already-frozen visible consequences; and long counterproductive negative lists have been removed.

**Serialization fidelity.** Confirm every defining identity-bearing macro/strong-meso structure is present, no body-critical fact contradicts another, and the renderer is not being asked to infer a missing design decision.

**Description integrity.** Confirm the final render context begins with the complete immutable presentation envelope, then retains every character block, dominant geometry, appendage/topology anchor, face/hair identity, material hierarchy, body-membership continuity, and presentation-critical fact. Confirm each of the five payloads opens with its own concrete macro structural anchor and that the five anchors preserve materially different body chassis rather than relying on colour, hair, surface pattern, or appendage decoration to signal diversity. Confirm no character clause can be read as changing pose, camera, page composition, background, or permitted visible object classes. Compress duplicate wording and scale-invisible microdetail first.

A failed preflight returns to the exact responsible internal layer. A passing preflight does not authorize submission; it permits the Renderer Capability Gate to run. Only a SUPPORTED capability result permits READY TO RENDER state.
#### 5.15.5 Phase-1 renderer wording and 2D execution — HARD / ABSOLUTE

For an initial Phase-1 CREATE, the immutable presentation envelope states a **clean five-character three-view female character-reference sheet** on a plain white field: five large uncropped true-front neutral-standing bodies plus one smaller exact 90-degree profile and one smaller direct rear for each same frozen identity. For a targeted single-character Phase-1 revision render, use the exact 9:16 front/profile/rear analytical geometry. These presentation facts are serialized before character payloads and remain immutable.

Each character's concept-derived body structures are then described through actual geometry, region, body origin or certified body-local anchor, material, root/attachment logic, thickness, continuity across neighboring anatomy, material transition, termination, topology, and silhouette role. Body-owned surface architecture is described as constituent anatomy. For chest/lower-torso presentation, serialize the exact opaque intrinsic concept-derived constituent body matter, its anatomical continuity, boundaries, thickness, material behavior, and transitions into neighboring body regions. Renderer-facing text remains entirely positive and physical; internal exclusions about presentation layers are not copied into the operative image instruction.

Character-direction qualities affect only the already-frozen physical payload. Renderer-facing text expresses them through concrete hair, face, anatomy, appendage, material, topology, colour/value, and silhouette facts rather than through abstract direction words that could alter the certified presentation.

Every primary concept remains immediately readable in each dominant front through major or strong-meso character-owned consequences, and the complete-set higher-order construction remains a prominent identity hook. Mandatory profile/rear views supplement reconstruction but never rescue weak primary embodiment.

Rendering remains intentionally drawn 2D anime/manhwa with material-specific line, shadow, highlight, edge, opacity, granularity, fracture/fiber/layering, and texture behavior. Similar colours do not justify similar material rendering.
#### 5.15.6 Handoff record — HARD

Record the Frozen Character Specification revision, Clean Renderer-Facing Description revision, output geometry, generation settings, image-attempt record, and actual result.

### 5.16 Positive Phase-1 certification stress test

This subsection verifies the complete Phase-1 result through required positive evidence rather than through a catalogue of unwanted imagery.

A population passes only when all five characters in the applicable initial sheet group simultaneously demonstrate:

- complete independent female character authorship;
- the complete supplied concept set as causal body construction;
- unmistakable macro or strong-meso evidence for every primary concept;
- every required pairwise interaction and at least one major or strong-meso complete-set interaction for three or more concepts;
- five materially different Concept-Fusion Architectures;
- a clear shortest and tallest character with intermediate stature variation;
- different proportion, skeletal, torso, limb, musculature, face, hair, colour, material, topology, morphology, and silhouette solutions as applicable;
- body-owned intrinsic structures with explicit origin, continuity, transition, attachment, and termination;
- material-specific illustrated behavior that preserves physical state and material discriminability;
- a stable analytical presentation envelope;
- native premium 2D line-led illustration;
- complete independent renderer-facing blocks for all active candidates.

Passing one dimension never compensates for an unresolved dimension.
#### 5.16.1 Complete pre-generation certification matrix — HARD / OPERATIONAL

Before READY TO RENDER, certify the following matrix from actual frozen geometry and renderer-facing evidence:

| System | Required passing evidence |
|---|---|
| Character foundation | Each candidate reads immediately as a complete premium female anime/manhwa character with independently authored anatomy and identity |
| Population independence | All ten character pairs differ meaningfully in causal architecture; no literal, mirror-equivalent, or near-template reuse |
| Concept materialization | Every primary concept changes macro or strong-meso character construction and remains necessary under counterfactual removal |
| Symbiosis | Every required pair changes a shared physical construction; the complete-set construction is major or strong-meso when three or more concepts are active |
| Human/transformed balance | Morphology transformation is deliberate per body system and varies across the population when relevant |
| Body diversity | Stature, torso, shoulder, waist, hip/lower-torso, limb, skeletal, musculature, cranial, and appendage relationships are coherently authored rather than uniformly scaled |
| Identity finishing | Face, eyes, hair, pigmentation/markings, hands/nails/claws where applicable, and asymmetry reinforce distinct character identity |
| Colour architecture | Each initial character has substantial internal colour/value differentiation and the five characters use different distribution logic |
| Material behavior | Each material retains its physical state and its own illustrated edge, shadow, highlight, opacity, grain, fracture/fiber/layering, flow, porosity, or transition behavior as applicable |
| Topology/morphology | Required cavities, channels, appendages, or iconic morphology have independently resolved origin, support, topology, material state, interaction role, and silhouette function |
| Body membership | Intrinsic structures have explicit body origin, continuity/path, root or attachment logic, material transition, and termination |
| Presentation envelope | Certified canvas, figure count, order, direct-front camera, neutral stance, full-body framing, plain field, and allowed visible object classes are complete and immutable |
| 2D drawing family | Line authority, shape-first construction, grouped illustrated shadows, and material-specific drawn behavior remain dominant |
| Serialization | Five complete independent payload blocks preserve every render-critical macro/strong-meso decision without workflow or narrative metadata |
| Handoff capability | Renderer Capability Gate is SUPPORTED for the complete FINAL RENDER CONTEXT with presentation-envelope priority preserved |

Any unresolved row returns to its owning stage before READY TO RENDER.
#### 5.16.2 Noncompensation and exact repair targeting — HARD

Every certification row is independently mandatory. Correct output geometry cannot compensate for weak concept fusion; strong concept fusion cannot compensate for population duplication; good material rendering cannot compensate for weak character identity; and high character appeal cannot compensate for an unresolved presentation or handoff requirement.

When a check fails, identify the owning layer—construction, materialization, symbiosis, population uniqueness, identity, presentation envelope, serialization, renderer capability, or 2D drawing family—and repair only that layer plus genuine dependencies. Preserve all passing unrelated systems.

Validation cites concrete geometry, topology, material, identity, view, and renderer-description evidence. It does not infer hidden renderer behavior.
### 5.17 Embodiment verification — validation application

This subsection applies the authoritative embodiment-verification rule defined in Section 5.3 to the Phase-1 validation process. It is a validation implementation, not a second or competing embodiment definition.

The verification asks one question:

> **Does the Phase-1 sheet display only the resolved female characters, their body-owned concept-integrated intrinsic structures, and their required reference views, and are those bodies fully and uniquely materialized from the complete supplied concept combination with unmistakable concept presence, complete required interactions, preserved major recognizable vocabulary, and controlled aesthetic economy while remaining unambiguously readable as female characters?**

Verification is performed on the character body as the sole Phase-1 design object; concept-integrated intrinsic coverage counts as body-owned design matter, not as a separate garment or prop.

For each character, verify all of the following before rendering:

1. The body exists as a complete resolved physical architecture before rendering.
2. The complete supplied concept combination is the causal origin of that body architecture.
3. Every supplied concept materially contributes to the body.
4. Pairwise concept interactions materially contribute to shared body structures.
5. Higher-order interaction materially contributes to the body when the request contains three or more concepts.
6. The concepts jointly create sufficient major or strong meso-scale shared construction rather than remaining isolated decorative carriers; no fixed number of altered systems is required.
7. The concepts remain unmistakably visible at macro or strong meso scale without requiring equal silhouette dominance.
8. The final construction remains a single coherent female character.
9. The body is uniquely resolved for that character and does not reuse another population member's fusion architecture.
10. The complete body remains readable without requiring any additional visual system to explain the concepts.

This is a direct verification that **the body itself—including its frozen body-owned concept-integrated intrinsic structures—is the complete materialization of the supplied concept combination and the only character-design subject of Phase 1**.

#### 5.17.1 Embodiment verification — generalized example

**Required:** `CONCEPT A + CONCEPT B + CONCEPT C` is resolved into a female body whose proportions, topology, substrate, anatomy, appendages, internal structures, hair, eyes, markings where physically integrated, asymmetry, and silhouette jointly arise from A × B × C.

**Failure:** the body is substantially conventional and the concepts are made recognizable mainly by three isolated concept-associated additions.

#### 5.17.2 Embodiment verification — population application

Perform the verification independently for all fifteen characters. Then, for each sheet group (01–05, 06–10, 11–15), compare the five resolved bodies. Every body must pass its own embodiment verification, and the five bodies must additionally pass the population differentiation and fusion-divergence gates.

#### 5.17.3 Embodiment verification failure recovery

When verification fails, return to concept decomposition and complete fusion resolution. Do not attempt to repair insufficient embodiment during image rendering by adding visual effects, presentation, or decorative content.

When a pre-render failure is identified, the correction record names the failing layer and specific requirement. When the same failure is found in an actual rendered image, record diagnostic responsibility only; the image is not corrected or retried.

During pre-render correction or an explicit user-authorized new revision, only the targeted failing layer and its genuine dependencies are altered. Passing unrelated layers remain preserved. However, a layer that previously passed a technical structural gate is not protected from revision when the aesthetic/taste gate identifies that very layer as the cause of a weak character. In that case the weak layer becomes the explicit correction target, its dependent decisions are invalidated as necessary, and all affected checks are repeated. Aesthetic rejection must not be blocked merely because the weak construction was technically coherent.

- 3/4 dominant front → correct presentation/camera serialization while preserving body architecture;
- repeated aperture locus → correct aperture architecture while preserving unrelated body systems;
- shared wing template → correct appendage/fusion architecture while preserving genuinely unrelated accepted decisions;
- 3D rendering drift → correct active 2D style serialization while preserving certified character construction;
- weak concept prominence → correct body-level concept carriers while preserving already-passing format and view geometry;
- palette convergence → correct colour architecture and any dependent material/focal relationships that caused the convergence;
- face convergence → correct face/identity authorship and dependent hair/framing relationships while preserving unrelated body architecture;
- technically valid but aesthetically weak construction → reopen the responsible body, fusion, proportion, appendage, topology, face, hair, material, colour, or hierarchy layer instead of preserving it solely because it previously passed structural validity.

A correction that changes unrelated passing layers without dependency evidence is not a targeted correction. A correction that refuses to change the actual weak layer merely because it previously passed is also invalid.

The specification is executed as one dependency-controlled system. Every design decision is resolved before rendering; every renderer handoff contains the already-certified result; every generated sheet is audited against the certified state; pre-render failures return to their responsible design or serialization layer; post-render failures are recorded without modification; accepted state is created only after the actual required validations pass.

Generation is forbidden until every item passes.

#### 5.17.4 Concept gate

- every supplied concept is active;
- every concept materially constructs the body;
- every concept preserves its complete classified major recognizable vocabulary;
- every concept has unmistakable physical evidence at macro or strong meso scale;
- all supplied primary concepts remain equally necessary to the fused construction without requiring equal surface area or carrier count;
- no concept is externalized;
- no energetic/fluid/material/force concept is represented only by an external phenomenon;
- every applicable primary-concept pair is physically fused;
- higher-order complete-set fusion exists for three or more concepts;
- embodiment verification passes;
- label-blind body identification passes;
- concept fusion remains readable without colour-only or optional-cosmetic cues;
- concept completeness is achieved without maximum saturation or redundant manifestation;
- body remains intentionally proportioned and premium-character-grade in its chosen body regime;
- macro anatomy and identity remain legible;
- female-character identity remains unambiguous after concept fusion;
- character-first test passes;
- concept-within-character test passes;
- no animal-first, creature-first, object-first, abstract-first, mascot-like, amorphous, or gender-ambiguous replacement of the character exists.

#### 5.17.5 Concept embodiment evidence record — HARD

Before the concept gate can pass, every supplied concept must have an explicit physical embodiment record containing:

1. concept identity in the user's exact wording;
2. concrete visual/physical properties selected for the active interpretation;
3. classification of major recognizable characteristics, supporting characteristics, and optional possibilities;
4. the major recognizable vocabulary actually retained in the character;
5. primary visible carrier or integrated system;
6. additional supporting carriers only where they genuinely strengthen the design;
7. material/substrate consequence where applicable;
8. topology consequence where applicable;
9. proportion/mass consequence where applicable;
10. appendage consequence where applicable;
11. cavity/opening consequence where applicable;
12. face/head/eye/hair consequence where applicable;
13. silhouette consequence where applicable;
14. interaction with every other supplied primary concept;
15. participation in complete-set higher-order interaction when three or more concepts are supplied;
16. macro/strong-meso recognition evidence;
17. aesthetic-economy check explaining why additional manifestation is or is not useful.

Items describing a physical channel may be recorded as justified N/A when that channel is not required by the concept or fusion. N/A must never be used to omit a characteristic already classified as major.

No concept may pass on the basis of a label alone.

#### 5.17.6 Fusion evidence record — HARD

For every multi-concept character, maintain an explicit fusion record containing:

- complete literal primary concept set;
- primary fusion locus or principal shared construction;
- secondary fusion systems where useful;
- every distinct pairwise interaction for the actual number of concepts;
- the visible physical consequence of each required pair;
- the complete-set higher-order construction for three or more concepts;
- any meaningful intermediate higher-order interactions that materially determine the body;
- body/substrate consequence where applicable;
- topology consequence where applicable;
- appendage-root or appendage consequence where applicable;
- face/head/hair consequence where applicable;
- silhouette consequence;
- asymmetry mechanism where useful;
- material-boundary consequence;
- embodiment-verification evidence;
- aesthetic-economy check confirming that no extra shared structure was added merely to satisfy a count.

Several interactions may share the same physical structure when the record explains how each concept changes that structure. The record must demonstrate interdependent synthesis rather than a set of adjacent concept features.

#### 5.17.7 Analytical body-reference evidence record — HARD

Phase-1 evidence is evaluated as a **neutral analytical body-reference**. The compiler must explicitly identify every concept-bearing structure as intrinsic constituent body matter, anatomically integrated morphology, or certified body-bound detached identity morphology under §0.0. A cavity is an absence within body topology. A spatial gap does not make a structure invalid when its ownership, body-local anchor, geometry, orientation, scale, and identity role are fixed; an independent floating object or surrounding effect remains invalid.

The dominant true front remains the mandatory neutral analytical view of the complete character-specific body. Hair, permanent body-owned structures, and certified body-bound identity morphology remain because they belong to physical identity. For the initial Phase-1 sheet, the mandatory exact profile and direct-rear views depict that same frozen construction.

Any concept-bearing item that requires environment, an independent prop, a symbolic presentation device, or an emitted/surrounding phenomenon to explain its conceptual role causes the concept gate to fail. Certified body-bound detached identity morphology is evaluated as character construction, not as an effect.

#### 5.17.8 Macro/micro evidence record — HARD

The compiler must identify the macro structures that establish:

- silhouette;
- height;
- body regime;
- torso/chest/hip/lower-torso region architecture;
- major concept structures;
- major appendages where present;
- major cavities/negative spaces where present;
- substrate/material architecture;
- asymmetry.

It must separately identify micro structures that reinforce those macro structures.

If a concept exists only in the micro record, the concept gate fails.

#### 5.17.9 Female-character evidence record — HARD

Every character must have an explicit female-character body architecture record independent of concept labels.

The record must remain recognizable after the concept names, labels, cosmetic presentation, and external context are removed.

Concept deformation may be extreme, but it must remain a deformation of a female character rather than replacement by an animal, creature, object, abstract mass, mascot-like form, or gender-ambiguous form.

#### 5.17.10 Population gate

Population certification requires both each body’s individual pass and every applicable comparison pass. The detailed quality and independence checks follow.

#### 5.17.11 Independent-character quality gate — HARD / ABSOLUTE

All five characters in each initial sheet group must independently pass a premium main-character-grade evaluation; the same quality floor applies across all fifteen.

For each character, verify:

- distinctive cranial/face construction;
- distinctive eye identity;
- distinctive hair architecture and silhouette;
- distinctive body architecture and proportion regime;
- distinctive concept-fusion architecture;
- distinctive aperture/topology architecture **where applicable**;
- distinctive appendage architecture **where applicable**;
- distinctive asymmetry system where used;
- distinctive material architecture;
- intelligently authored colour/value architecture;
- complete character-identity finishing, with conventional cosmetics optional;
- coherent macro silhouette and dominant visual hook;
- deliberate visual hierarchy and density rhythm;
- controlled internal contrast;
- concept completeness without maximum saturation;
- intricate subordinate micro-detail;
- professional 2D finish with material-specific rendering behavior;
- explicit aesthetic/taste pass and a compelling character-specific appeal mode.

A population fails if even one character is visibly a weaker variant, procedural concept demonstration, anatomy experiment, or aesthetically inferior placeholder.

#### 5.17.12 No related-character illusion gate — HARD / ABSOLUTE

After removing colour, cosmetics, markings, hair styling, and micro-detail, the five characters must not appear to be one character repeatedly redesigned.

With concept labels hidden and concept-derived anatomy intact, the complete bodies must still differ materially.

Comparing the causal construction of those intact bodies, the five fusion mechanisms must still differ materially.

All three tests must pass simultaneously.

- five independent bodies exist;
- each of the five characters uses its own frozen complete Resolved Primary Concept Set; fixed primary slots remain identical while autonomous slots may differ;
- no canonical first solution exists;
- no body derives from another;
- full-body proportion regimes are perceptually distinct;
- stature and scale vary where useful but are not forced into arbitrary extremes;
- chest architecture differs materially;
- body type/build differs materially;
- skeletal/mass architecture differs materially;
- appendage systems differ materially where applicable;
- internal topology differs materially where applicable;
- concept-fusion mechanisms differ materially;
- concept-interaction loci differ materially;
- pairwise and higher-order concept interactions are not repeated recipes;
- each character has a distinct Concept-Fusion Architecture;
- differentiation remains useful after cosmetic removal;
- silhouettes differ materially;
- all ten character pairs pass structural comparison;
- no pair is a repeated body with surface changes;
- no three-character cluster shares the same dominant construction strategy;
- every character has a proportionally refined body; compact bodies remain petite and attractive rather than caricaturally compressed or visually undeveloped;
- no character is a tame/conventional humanoid with concept ornaments substituted for structural fusion;
- every character is wholly authored under the complete concept fusion, with sufficient unmistakable manifestations and required interactions but no forced multi-system saturation;
- no supplied concept is reduced to an insignificant accent or token, even though local visual emphasis may differ;
- all five meet the same main-character-grade quality floor;
- material and colour architecture are not population-uniform;
- each character has independent macro-silhouette readability;
- all five identities remain unambiguously female anime/manhwa characters;
- female-character identity is independently resolved in every body rather than carried only by shared assumptions;
- no character is animal-first, creature-first, object-first, abstract-first, mascot-like, amorphous, or gender-ambiguous.

#### 5.17.13 Phase gate

For every initial five-character sheet group:

- exactly five female identities belonging to the already-certified fifteen-character population;
- every identity remains a readable female anime/manhwa character under extreme concept deformation;
- no animal/creature/object/abstract/mass form replaces the character body;
- exactly one dominant complete full-body true-front depiction per identity;
- exactly one smaller exact 90-degree full-body profile per identity;
- exactly one smaller direct-rear full-body view per identity;
- every view is strictly neutral standing and analytically readable;
- all three views depict the same frozen body without redesign;
- presentation contains only the certified Phase-1 character-reference content;
- no shared studio/reference layer, neutral underlayer, or fallback garment-analogue is present; chest/lower-torso presentation coverage remains intrinsic body materialization only;
- plain white field and complete extents remain intact.

Missing or approximate profile/rear geometry fails the sheet presentation contract while preserving the underlying frozen design state.
#### 5.17.14 Format gate

- exactly one continuous 16:9 landscape raster per sheet;
- five left-to-right character regions;
- no non-analytical page architecture;
- no vertically stacked identity regions; the subordinate side/rear row remains required;
- no auxiliary panels;
- no close-ups;
- no title/labels/icons/palette modules;
- all required full-body views visible and uncropped.

#### 5.17.15 Rendering plan gate and actual-image audit

Before generation, this gate evaluates whether the resolved design, Frozen Character Specification, Clean Renderer-Facing Description, and generation settings explicitly implement the 2D execution target. After generation, the same criteria are applied to the actual image and record pass or failure without a retry.

Required:

- unmistakably drawn 2D anime/manhwa artwork;
- clean controlled lineart;
- deliberate 2D shape language;
- clear macro structure;
- intricate but subordinate micro-detail;
- fully developed faces;
- main-character-grade finish for all five characters;
- coherent material illustration;
- deliberate character-specific colour architecture;
- readable body topology;
- readable appendages;
- readable apertures;
- neutral standing views;
- no visual quality collapse between character regions.

The planned image must explicitly satisfy these criteria before generation; the actual image must visibly satisfy them afterward. Attractiveness or detail alone cannot pass either evaluation.

The rendering family itself must correspond to the defined 2D requirement.

Any FALSE or INDETERMINATE pre-generation result blocks generation. A failed or indeterminate actual-image result prevents acceptance but does not authorize another call. The following gates are integral parts of certification and inspection.

##### 5.17.15A RENDERED POPULATION-COLLAPSE AUDIT — HARD / ABSOLUTE

After an initial five-character sheet returns a usable image, inspect the **actual principal-front pixels** for renderer-induced convergence even when the five frozen specifications were valid. This audit is an output-fidelity gate, not a redesign permission.

For all ten rendered character pairs, compare the visible bodies while mentally discounting palette, hair colour, markings, micro-detail, local ornament, and material patterning. The rendered pair must preserve the structural differences certified before submission. In particular inspect:

- stature and overall body regime;
- head-to-body relationship;
- shoulder/ribcage/torso architecture;
- chest/waist/hip-lower-torso construction;
- torso-to-leg and limb-thickness relationships;
- skeletal/musculature/mass distribution;
- major appendage count, root/origin zone, support logic, scale, form language, articulation and symmetry/asymmetry;
- central concept-fusion locus and visible material/topology organization;
- dominant silhouette mechanism independent of colour and surface motif.

**Hard rendered-pair floor.** Every rendered pair must visibly retain the same minimum macro-body divergence required by §5.11.13A: clear non-cosmetic divergence across at least four independent macro systems, with at least one difference in central torso/shoulder/hip-lower-torso/limb architecture rather than only head scale, colour, hair, horns, wings, tails, or surface treatment.

**Renderer-collapse failure.** If multiple columns visibly share one foundational torso/waist/hip/chest/leg chassis and differ mainly through wings, horns, tails, hair, colour, material pattern, feather/scale decoration, or local growths, the actual sheet FAILS even when those decorative systems are elaborate. Likewise, five similarly rooted paired back wings with altered patterns or materials fail whenever the frozen payloads required materially different appendage architectures.

**Flagship-output floor.** Each principal front must independently retain protagonist/flagship-grade identity: memorable face/head/hair, clearly authored body regime, a character-specific structural hook, and concept materialization strong enough that the character would not read as roster filler or an alternate skin of another column.

A failure here is recorded as **FIDELITY FAILURE — RENDERED POPULATION COLLAPSE**. Preserve the frozen specifications and the returned image separately. Do not silently accept the sheet, do not mutate the frozen designs to match the collapsed pixels, and do not retry without another explicit user `RENDER` authorization.

#### 5.17.16 Independent-aesthetic and taste gate — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Every character must have a complete, coherent, character-specific aesthetic and must pass an explicit premium-character desirability evaluation before rendering. Structural correctness is necessary but not sufficient.

The aesthetic must integrate:

- face and head identity;
- eye identity;
- hair architecture and silhouette;
- body proportions and mass distribution;
- dominant visual hook or hooks;
- concept-fusion architecture;
- materials and visibly differentiated textures;
- colour and value hierarchy;
- internal contrast;
- asymmetry where applicable;
- appendages/topology where applicable;
- identity-defining markings or cosmetic finishing where applicable;
- overall silhouette and focal hierarchy.

The gate must verify all of the following:

- the face/head remains one of the strongest identity regions unless a deliberate stronger hook temporarily leads the first read;
- hair contributes meaningful identity and silhouette rather than functioning as filler;
- concept manifestations have deliberate density variation rather than uniform coverage;
- the design contains quiet regions where needed to preserve hierarchy;
- concept completeness is achieved without maximum saturation;
- additional structures all strengthen the character rather than merely occupy unused space;
- useful internal contrasts are deliberate and coherent;
- the character remains beautiful, compelling, charismatic, or otherwise strongly desirable according to its own chosen appeal mode;
- the character does not depend on one universal generic pretty-anime female-character template;
- the design does not read as a procedural concept demonstration, anatomy experiment, repeated motif suit, or technically valid first draft;
- a stronger plausible implementation has not been ignored merely because the current one passes structural checks.

Any character that appears unfinished, generic, procedurally assembled, uniformly saturated, aesthetically weak, or technically valid but substantially inferior to a stronger available construction fails before rendering.

#### 5.17.17 Proportion-regime quality gate

Every body regime must pass as an intentionally authored protagonist-grade proportion system.

Verify that head size, neck, shoulders, ribcage, chest, waist, hip/lower-torsos, torso-to-leg ratio, thigh/calf relationship, arms, hands/feet, musculature/skeletal prominence, centre of mass, and appendage scale where present coherently support the same identity.

For compact/petite characters specifically:

- the body must not become a uniformly shrunken copy of another character;
- head/body ratio remains deliberately designed rather than caricaturally enlarged by default;
- face, limbs, hands, and feet remain fully developed;
- the silhouette remains deliberate and attractive.

For tall, broad, muscular, soft, full-figured, powerful, elongated, or other regimes, the corresponding proportional logic must likewise propagate through the whole body rather than changing only one measurement.

No body regime is a mandatory population slot. Every selected regime must remain attractive or compelling, fully developed, and main-character quality.

#### 5.17.18 Colour-architecture gate

Compare the five colour systems by:

- dominant/subordinate hue families;
- value range and light/dark mass placement;
- saturation range and focal saturation placement;
- warm/cool organization;
- distribution;
- material relationship;
- hair/body relationship;
- eye/body relationship;
- appendage/body relationship;
- interior/exterior treatment where applicable;
- focal hierarchy;
- approximate colour count only as descriptive evidence, never as a quota.

An individual character may be monochromatic; monochrome is permitted, not a mandatory population slot. Multiple characters may share a similar hue count if their colour architecture remains materially different. The population fails only when the actual palette logic converges, not because two characters happen to use the same number of colours.

#### 5.17.19 Style-activation gate

The embedded 2D grammar is always active:

- drawing parameters resolved;
- style serialized;
- positive 2D rendering language present;
- 3D simulation absent from the target rendering plan;
- defined style actively applied.

#### 5.17.20 Front-view geometry gate

Every principal front is direct, neutral, unposed, uncropped, and free of 3/4 camera substitution. The five-body comparison must be possible directly from these standardized fronts.

#### 5.17.21 Supporting-view gate

Exact profile and direct-rear views are mandatory on every initial sheet and must be consistent with the frozen body. Their omission is a Phase-1 presentation failure.

#### 5.17.22 Main-character parity gate

All five characters in each sheet group independently meet the same quality floor, and the same floor applies across all fifteen. No later, petite, unusually proportioned, heavily transformed, highly asymmetric, or materially difficult character may be downgraded to make the sheet easier.

#### 5.17.23 POST-RENDER 2D AND MATERIAL AUDIT — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Inspect the image for intentional 2D construction and material discriminability: line authority should remain visible, shadow masses should read as intentionally drawn groups, and complex materials should not depend on generalized smooth volume or CGI-like sheen.

- drawn linework and controlled contours;
- graphic value grouping and controlled gradients;
- material-specific edge, shadow, highlight, opacity, granularity, fracture/fiber/layering and thickness behavior;
- clear visual distinction between different body constituents;
- no universal glossy fantasy shader replacing the specified materials.

If materially different constituents all read as the same surface treatment, record failure even when the colours differ.
#### 5.17.24 POST-RENDER FORMAT / POSE / BODY-DIVERSITY AUDIT — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Reject as a compliant Phase-1 population if:

- canvas is not 16:9 landscape;
- five principal characters are not arranged left to right;
- any principal body is cropped materially;
- any principal front is 3/4, dynamically posed, arm-raised, hip-cocked, contrapposto, walking, kneeling, floating, or otherwise non-neutral;
- Phase-1 presentation is dominated by elaborate clothing/costume architecture rather than body-owned concept design;
- a mandatory major morphology class disappears from any character;
- body proportions collapse into one repeated mannequin family;
- faces/physiognomies collapse into one repeated fictional archetype;
- distinct materials collapse into one repeated rendering response;
- concept combination identity is carried by symbols, environment, or emitted phenomena rather than the character;
- a separated form appears that does not pass the body-bound detached identity-structure rule;
- extra studies or decorative modules materially reduce character readability.

Missing a required exact profile or direct-rear view fails the initial Phase-1 presentation contract, while the frozen character design remains separately preserved.

### 5.18 Certified generation package and evidence gate

#### 5.18.1 No invented or bypassed certification — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Generation eligibility depends on concrete frozen design evidence, not checklist assertion. Every required design fact must exist in the Frozen Character Specification and every render-critical fact must survive into the Clean Renderer-Facing Description.

#### 5.18.2 Complete generation-package schema — HARD

Maintain an internal package containing:

1. active phase and operation;
2. exact character / sheet revision IDs;
3. literal primary concept set;
4. complete concept-embodiment records;
5. pairwise and higher-order fusion records;
6. five-way differentiation evidence where applicable;
7. complete Frozen Character Specification(s);
8. concept-integrated intrinsic-coverage architecture and exact visible construction per character;
9. dominant-front geometry plus mandatory exact-profile/direct-rear geometry and cross-view consistency anchors;
10. exact canvas and region geometry;
11. embedded 2D rendering grammar;
12. Clean Renderer-Facing Description;
13. renderer preflight record;
14. immutable attempt record and actual outcome;
15. same-design serializer-correction record when used;
16. immutable Phase-1 sheet-attempt record tied to the five exact frozen character revisions.

The package remains internal unless the user requests inspection. Its purpose is exact preservation and traceability, not extra visible prose before the image. Internal preservation does not require public blueprint output. After each image attempt, preserve the attempt outcome separately from the frozen design state. A later revision never overwrites the earlier attempt record.
#### 5.18.3 Pairwise and population proof

Population proof remains mandatory before rendering. Structural independence, concept completeness, quality parity, material divergence, appendage/topology divergence where applicable, and all ten character-pair comparisons must be resolved before the handoff.

#### 5.18.4 Description integrity and faithful handoff

The Clean Renderer-Facing Description must preserve every macro and strong-meso identity-bearing system while remaining visually prioritized and non-repetitive. Its operative order is presentation envelope first, frozen character payload second, active 2D grammar last. The character payload is subordinate to the envelope and cannot change camera, stance, framing, background, page composition, or visible object classes. Remove duplicated reasoning, redundant negatives, repeated proof, abstract direction language, and scale-invisible enumeration before removing character design.

For Phase 1, intrinsic surface descriptions must preserve body membership through explicit origin, continuity, transition, attachment/root and termination information. If a render-critical fact is missing or ambiguous, or if a body-owned structure can reasonably be read as a separate worn system because continuity is underspecified, the handoff is unresolved and must be reserialized before submission. Do not silently omit the fact and expect inference to reconstruct it.
#### 5.18.5 Post-render result is a separate immutable outcome

The intended frozen design and the actual rendered image are separate records. Audit the actual pixels against the frozen specification and required geometry. A rendered deviation does not retroactively change the intended design.

A design may therefore remain valid while a particular image fails fidelity. Likewise, an image attempt may not execute even when the design package is complete. Record the actual outcome precisely.

Every returned image attempt remains a separate immutable outcome associated with the exact frozen revisions that produced it. A later attempt or revision does not redefine, replace, or erase an earlier outcome.

## 6 PHASE 2 — OUTFIT CREATION

Phase 2 creates the character's dedicated outfit after the Phase-1 body has been completely resolved, validated, certified, selected, and frozen. Phase 2 does not create a replacement character. It creates a wearable system for the exact character that already exists.

The governing dependency is:

FROZEN CHARACTER → OUTFIT DESIGN RESOLUTION → CHARACTER-SPECIFIC ADAPTATION → BADASS / OPEN-REGION FUSION → STRUCTURAL DIFFERENTIATION → MATERIAL AND MICRO-DETAIL RESOLUTION → PREMIUM-OUTFIT AESTHETIC PASS → FINAL REFINEMENT / RENDER-ROBUSTNESS CHECK → FREEZE INTERNAL OUTFIT SPECIFICATION → CLEAN RENDERER DESCRIPTION → RENDER PREFLIGHT → IMAGE GENERATION → INSPECTION → OUTCOME RECORD → USER SELECTION AND FREEZE

### 6.1 Phase-2 activation gate

Phase 2 may begin only on the user’s explicit instruction and when the relevant Phase-1 character has passed all mandatory Phase-1 gates and the body is selected and frozen. The complete frozen character state is an immutable input to Phase 2.

- The body cannot be redesigned because an outfit would otherwise be difficult to construct.

- The body's proportions cannot be normalized to fit a garment archetype.

- Concept-derived anatomy cannot be hidden, relocated, simplified, deleted, or converted into clothing.

- Appendages, apertures, unusual topology, unusual substrates, asymmetry, silhouette, face/head, eyes, hair, character-identity finishing, and body materials remain authoritative.

- Phase 2 may alter only the clothing system, including garment openings, clearances, edges, and attachments required for the frozen body. These accommodations never alter anatomy.

#### 6.1.1 Operational explanation

An outfit is designed only after the user starts Phase 2 and adopts a passing body. Eligibility is not automatic activation. Clothing accommodations modify garment edges and attachments, never the body.

#### 6.1.2 Worked application

A lateral torso aperture requires an opening in the garment at its actual location; the aperture cannot be moved toward the waist because a familiar bodice fits there.

#### 6.1.3 Failure and validation

Check the explicit phase command, frozen body ID/revision, and every preserved body feature. A body change or an automatic phase transition fails.

### 6.1.4 Premium-outfit designer mandate — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Phase 2 is not “put clothes on the body.” It must behave as top-level gacha/anime/manhwa costume design. Every outfit must elevate the exact frozen character, strengthen her silhouette and identity, preserve or frame her strongest anatomy, and feel sufficiently specific that it would be recognizable as belonging to her rather than transferable unchanged to another character. A technically fitted but generic outfit fails the design objective.

The outfit should increase the character's desirability, iconography, and franchise value while remaining subordinate to and compatible with the frozen body. Open-region architecture, layering, materials, asymmetry, ornament, construction, movement, and colour are design systems rather than checklist items.

### 6.2 Character-first outfit authorship

Every outfit must be authored for the actual frozen character. A generic costume subsequently placed over the character is invalid.

Before garment construction, Phase 2 must resolve how the frozen character constrains and informs the outfit through all applicable dimensions:

- height and total scale;

- shoulder width and shoulder topology;

- torso length and torso-to-leg ratio;

- chest architecture;

- waist architecture;

- hip/lower-torso region and hip architecture;

- limb length and limb thickness;

- musculature;

- appendage count, origin, direction, size, articulation, and occupied space;

- apertures, cavities, openings, protrusions, ridges, plates, or other unusual body topology;

- body-integrated materials and surface transitions;

- asymmetry;

- major silhouette;

- face, eye, hair, and character-identity finishing;

- the complete primary concept combination embodied by the body;

- the two established sub-concepts.

The outfit must visibly appear to have been designed after knowing these facts. If the same outfit could be transferred unchanged to an unrelated character, character-specific adaptation is insufficient.

#### 6.2.1 Operational explanation

Character-specific fit includes proportions, topology, appendage clearance, body material, and identity. It is more than scaling a generic costume.

#### 6.2.2 Worked application

For a short deep torso with an offset shoulder root and a flank channel, resolve an asymmetric upper attachment, a shortened waist section, and a channel-framing side boundary. A long-torso design needs its own different garment construction.

#### 6.2.3 Failure and validation

Compare garment anchors and coverage with the frozen anatomy. Transferability unchanged to an unrelated body indicates insufficient tailoring.

### 6.3 Complete clothing-space consideration

Phase 2 is not restricted to a predetermined fashion genre, era, culture, armor class, or costume archetype unless the active task explicitly imposes one. Concepts and sub-concepts influence the construction of the outfit, but they do not automatically dictate a stereotypical garment category.
