A targeted operation uses its explicit candidate count and the active phase’s presentation contract.

### 9.2 Detailed Phase-1 image audit

Reject immediately for any of:

- wrong canvas ratio or non-analytical layout;
- fewer or more than five identities;
- missing or duplicate identity;
- missing principal true-front full-body view;
- missing exact 90-degree profile or missing direct-rear view for any identity;
- a supposed profile that is three-quarter, or a supposed rear that is not directly rear-facing;
- front/profile/rear depictions that drift into different anatomy, proportions, appendage roots/counts, topology, materials, face/head/hair identity, markings, asymmetry, or palette instead of preserving the same frozen character;
- any principal front using three-quarter camera, raised-arm glamour posing, contrapposto, hip-cocking, crossed legs, walking, kneeling, floating, action, or another non-neutral stance;
- cropping of head, hair, feet, mandatory wings, horns, tails, or other identity-bearing extents;
- environment, scenery, decorative page architecture, symbolic concept prop, or emitted concept phenomenon carrying the design;
- a spatially detached structure that fails the body-bound identity-structure rule in §0.0;
- Phase-2-like costume design, elaborate armor systems, accessories, weapons, props, or fashion silhouettes replacing body analysis;
- any reintroduced analytical underlayer or separate coverage layer that becomes ornate, asymmetrical, concept-bearing, draped, decorative, silhouette-extending, or character-defining;
- unreadable body architecture or macro detail overload;
- 3D/PBR/photo drift;
- a primary concept that is weak, tokenized, absent, or recognizable only from colour, markings, micro-detail, labels, pose, a separate coverage layer, or external presentation;
- omission of any characteristic certified as major recognizable vocabulary;
- insufficient pairwise fusion or a higher-order interaction that is visually minor, decorative, or absent;
- concept fusion reduced to separate feature groups rather than symbiotic shared construction;
- generic shared mannequin or repeated glamour-body family;
- insufficient macro-body, proportion, or fictional physiognomic differentiation;
- concept-fusion architecture repeated between characters;
- same concept-to-body mapping repeated with only parameter/cosmetic changes;
- mandatory appendage classes retained only as near-identical templates;
- multiple characters effectively being the same body with surface changes;
- materially different substances rendered with effectively the same edge/shadow/highlight/gloss/texture response;
- colour/material treatment collapsing the population into one repeated visual recipe;
- any character substantially below the established main-character quality benchmark;
- a previous design or example copied as a character template instead of applying the construction discipline;
- any character no longer reading unambiguously as a female anime/manhwa character;
- any character becoming animal-first, creature-first, object-first, abstract-first, mascot-like, amorphous, or gender-ambiguous;
- female identity surviving only through face, label, colour, cosmetic styling, or one isolated feature while the underlying body becomes another form.

The mandatory exact profile and direct-rear views are audited for every identity on an initial Phase-1 sheet. Absence of either required view is a presentation failure.

#### 9.2.1 Failure ownership and structural interpretation — HARD

The following findings are structural, presentation, or realization failures, never defects that can be excused through cosmetic appeal. Assign responsibility from evidence: body/fusion construction for repeated anatomy; layout/camera for wrong views; serialization when an instruction is missing; renderer realization when the exact certified instruction was present but the image deviated.

- all five bodies use essentially the same physical construction;
- the same concept-to-body mapping is repeated across characters;
- appendage variation changes only count, size, or colour while the underlying origin, topology, construction, material logic, and silhouette role repeat;
- concept interaction is repeated with only parameter changes;
- the concepts are individually present but not jointly responsible for any meaningful major or strong meso-scale shared construction;
- a concept is carried by environmental, symbolic, or emitted presentation rather than by the character construction;
- the intact body remains a conventional template with the concepts limited to surface decoration or attached ornament;
- the body requires effects, environment, props, or presentation to identify the concept combination;
- a character is too similar to another after cosmetic removal;
- a character has a complex macro that prevents immediate silhouette/body reading;
- micro-detail is carrying primary concept identity that should already exist at macro or strong meso scale;
- a character has become accidentally/crudely bulky, caricaturally compressed, or amorphous in a way that reads as a degraded shortcut rather than an intentional attractive body regime;
- a character has become so creature-first or abstract-first that female-character identity is ambiguous;
- height variation is merely scaling rather than body-architecture divergence;
- chest architecture has converged despite otherwise superficial variation;
- body type/build has converged;
- appendage roots and origins have converged;
- material/substrate architecture has converged;
- cavities/topology have converged where those systems are relevant;
- asymmetry is repeated as the same mechanism;
- silhouette construction is repeated;
- a principal front is not truly frontal or is posed rather than neutrally standing;
- a required profile/rear support view is not exact or depicts a different body;
- a required profile/rear support view is composed so poorly that it competes with or shrinks the principal fronts below useful analytical scale;
- environment, symbolic concept objects, or emitted concept phenomena have been introduced as concept carriers;
- a detached structure lacks certified body ownership, fixed body-local relationship, or genuine morphological necessity;
- the rendering is not clean 2D anime/manhwa;
- rendering quality collapses for later characters;
- one or more characters fall below the established quality benchmark.

Before rendering, every such failure requires correction at the earliest violated stage. In an actual image, the same finding records failure only; no automatic correction follows.

The workflow must not repair a body-construction failure by adding effects, changing background presentation, changing pose, adding cosmetic detail, or changing labels.

A failed image is recorded as failed and kept unchanged. It is not certified, repaired, or automatically retried. Continue only with already-authorized remaining work in the active phase.

#### 9.2.2 Character-independence audit — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Reject if any pair of characters can be described as:

- the same body at another height;
- the same body with another body mass;
- the same body with another chest configuration;
- the same body with another aperture location;
- the same body with another wing count;
- the same body with another horn arrangement;
- the same body with another tail arrangement;
- the same body with another colour palette;
- the same body with another face;
- the same body with another hairstyle;
- the same body with additional micro-detail.

These are all parameterized variants rather than independent character identities when the underlying construction remains shared.

#### 9.2.3 Aperture / cavity / internal-topology audit — HARD

For any concept combination that produces or requires apertures, cavities, channels, breaches, missing volume, recessed chambers, or comparable topology, audit:

- anatomical locus;
- count;
- scale;
- depth;
- geometry;
- orientation;
- branching/connectivity;
- edge/rim architecture;
- relationship to skeleton/support;
- relationship to appendages;
- relationship to substrate/material boundaries;
- symmetry/asymmetry;
- relationship to every relevant supplied concept;
- silhouette consequence where applicable.

The same convenient body locus or cavity recipe must not be repeated across the population without a genuine structural reason. Variation must operate across the architecture of the system, not merely move one identical hole from limb to limb. If the topology is part of a concept's major recognizable vocabulary, it cannot be omitted from some characters for differentiation.

#### 9.2.4 Appendage and major-morphology audit — HARD

For every character, audit every appendage or major repeated morphology that is actually present or required by the concept's major recognizable vocabulary. Where applicable inspect:

- class/type;
- count and pair count;
- origin and attachment depth;
- direction/orientation;
- size and relative scale;
- form;
- length, width, and thickness;
- curvature/taper;
- branching;
- segmentation;
- articulation/jointure;
- material;
- visibly differentiated texture rendering;
- symmetry/asymmetry;
- internal construction/support;
- integration depth;
- interaction with topology/cavities;
- interaction with the other supplied concepts;
- silhouette role.

If multiple characters use the same architecture with only cosmetic, colour, or minor scale changes, record FAILED without correction or retry. If a concept's major recognizable vocabulary requires a specific appendage class, omitting it from some characters for differentiation is also a failure. Variation must occur through the architecture of the retained system.

#### 9.2.5 Face-quality audit — HARD

Reject any character whose face is visibly generic, underdeveloped, simplified relative to the body, or interchangeable with another character.

The face must have its own deliberate geometry and character-specific finishing.

#### 9.2.6 Colour/material audit — HARD

Reject if all five characters share essentially the same palette architecture or material hierarchy.

The audit must inspect the distribution of value, saturation, dominant hue, accent placement, substrate treatment, appendage material, hair relationship, eye relationship, and aperture interior treatment.

#### 9.2.7 2D-native audit — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Reject if the actual rendering reads as:

- three-dimensional asset rendering;
- CGI illustration;
- physically based material rendering;
- photographic imagery;
- game-engine character rendering;
- volumetric digital rendering.

The required output must read as intentionally drawn 2D anime/manhwa artwork.

This audit is independent of visual attractiveness.

#### 9.2.8 Macro saturation audit — HARD

Reject if major structures accumulate into a dense unreadable cluster that prevents immediate recognition of:

- silhouette;
- body proportions;
- major concept structures;
- appendages;
- apertures;
- major material regions.

Before rendering, correction means reorganizing body-derived structures while preserving their concept consequences. After rendering, record the defect without changing the output.

Do not compensate with background, effects, lighting, or pose.

#### 9.2.9 Main-character and premium-appeal audit — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Each character in every five-character sheet group must independently satisfy the following standard:

> **Would this character, without the other four, be sufficiently resolved, memorable, visually compelling, and desirable to serve as the principal visual character of a polished anime/manhwa production or a premium gacha release?**

Inspect not only finish but actual design quality: face/head identity, hair silhouette, body proportions, dominant hooks, visual hierarchy, density rhythm, material contrast, colour/value hierarchy, controlled internal contrast, concept completeness without oversaturation, and whether the character looks authored rather than procedurally assembled.

If the answer is no for any character, the sheet fails regardless of the quality of the strongest character. A technically accurate render does not pass this audit when the resulting character is aesthetically weak, generic, over-designed, uniformly saturated, or visually undesirable.

Do not evaluate all five against one beauty template. Cute, elegant, severe, muscular, full-figured, glamorous, eerie, monstrous-but-beautiful, regal, playful, dangerous, minimal, and other deliberately authored appeal modes may all pass. What is shared is premium design quality, not one face/body archetype.

#### 9.2.10 Submission and interpretation audit — HARD

Record every non-executed attempt or material renderer misinterpretation from actual evidence. A returned image that misses mandatory frozen design or geometry is a fidelity failure. An absent image is recorded as submission non-execution or technical failure according to available evidence.

A non-executed or serialization-layer outcome may use same-design serializer correction under §10.2 while preserving the frozen design. Any subsequent image call requires a new explicit user `RENDER` command. A usable but inaccurate image remains immutable attempt evidence and does not authorize silent character redesign or automatic resubmission.
#### 9.2.11 Independent-character audit

Compare every character pair across:

- body regime;
- fusion architecture;
- concept-carrier architecture;
- topology;
- appendage system;
- cavity system;
- face;
- material;
- colour;
- character-identity finishing, including optional cosmetics where selected;
- silhouette.

A failure in construction independence is sufficient to reject the sheet.

#### 9.2.12 Concept-materialization prominence audit

For every concept in every character:

- identify major body carriers;
- identify secondary body carriers;
- identify shared carriers;
- identify higher-order carriers;
- inspect macro prominence;
- inspect integration depth.

If the concept remains detectable only after zooming or explanation, reject.

#### 9.2.13 Character-family audit

Ask:

> Within this sheet group, would these five characters still look independently authored as designs if shown side-by-side without their names?

If a sheet group clearly looks like five versions of one character family, reject; the later global fifteen-character collision review additionally catches severe cross-sheet reuse.

#### 9.2.14 Embedded-quality audit

Compare every character to the embedded quality benchmark independently.

The benchmark comparison must not be averaged across the population.

A single weak character fails the population.

#### 9.2.15 Embedded-style audit

Compare the output to the embedded drawing grammar and the exact resolved 2D parameter record.

Verify:

- linework;
- face grammar;
- eye grammar;
- hair grammar;
- body grammar;
- rendering language;
- colour treatment;
- shadows;
- highlights;
- material depiction;
- texture;
- background behaviour.

A 3D-looking output fails regardless of attractiveness.

#### 9.2.16 Format audit

The exact required page geometry must be checked visually.

Do not accept a merely similar page.

The page must reproduce the certified architecture:

> **16:9 LANDSCAPE → FIVE REGIONS → FIVE DOMINANT TRUE FRONTS → OPTIONAL SMALL ANALYTICAL SUPPORT VIEWS → NO POSE DRIFT → NO UNREQUESTED MODULES**

#### 9.2.17 Final acceptance

A sheet is accepted only if:

- all five characters are independent;
- all five are high quality;
- every character materially fuses every concept in her own frozen Resolved Primary Concept Set;
- no concept is externalized;
- no character is caricaturally compressed;
- no character is an accidental bulky placeholder;
- petite characters remain attractive and fully resolved;
- palettes are materially differentiated;
- faces are independently developed;
- appendages are radically differentiated;
- aperture systems are radically differentiated where applicable;
- asymmetry is deliberate;
- macro readability is strong;
- micro-detail is subordinate;
- 2D style is correctly applied;
- all five principal views are truly frontal and strictly neutral;
- exact profile and direct-rear support is mandatory, exact, and consistent;
- no pose-based differentiation exists;
- no extra study modules compete with the bodies;
- each rendered sheet is one continuous 16:9 landscape sheet.

If any one condition fails before rendering, revise and revalidate. If the actual rendered image fails, record FAILED without correction or retry.

#### 9.2.18 Five-body independence audit

For every character, confirm independence in the systems that actually exist:

- body regime and full-body proportions;
- chest, torso, hip/lower-torsos, limbs, skeleton, and mass distribution;
- topology and cavity/aperture architecture where applicable;
- appendage architecture and roots where applicable;
- asymmetry mechanism where used;
- materials and material boundaries;
- colour/value architecture;
- cranial and face architecture;
- eye identity;
- hair architecture and silhouette;
- character-identity finishing, with conventional cosmetics optional;
- dominant visual hook and overall silhouette.

A conditionally absent aperture, appendage, marking, or cosmetic system is not a failure merely because another character uses one. Absence is valid only when the concept's major recognizable vocabulary and the resolved fusion do not require that system.

#### 9.2.19 Five-fusion independence audit

- independent primary fusion locus;
- independent secondary fusion loci;
- independent pairwise interactions;
- independent higher-order interaction;
- independent concept-carrier distribution;
- independent materialization mechanism;
- independent topology mechanism;
- independent appendage mechanism;
- independent aperture mechanism where applicable;
- independent silhouette mechanism.

#### 9.2.20 Whole-body concept audit

For every character, verify that the supplied concepts materially affect the complete body design.

Inspect:

- head;
- face;
- eyes;
- hair;
- neck;
- shoulders;
- torso;
- chest;
- waist;
- hip/lower-torso region;
- hips;
- limbs;
- joints;
- substrate;
- topology;
- appendages;
- cavities;
- silhouette.

The goal is not uniform treatment.

The goal is whole-body authorship.

#### 9.2.21 Concept-prominence audit

At normal viewing scale:

- the complete combination remains readable;
- each concept remains visible;
- the concepts appear physically integrated;
- major concept/fusion structures contribute to the macro or strong meso read, without requiring every concept to dominate the silhouette;
- no concept survives mainly through page presentation.

#### 9.2.22 External-field audit

The surrounding presentation remains analytically neutral.

All meaningful concept complexity is contained inside the characters and their directly body-derived systems.

#### 9.2.23 Non-repetition audit

Inspect all five characters in the applicable sheet group for repeated:

- body templates;
- face templates;
- eye templates;
- hair templates;
- body regimes;
- aperture loci;
- appendage counts;
- appendage origins;
- asymmetry patterns;
- material recipes;
- palette architectures;
- concept-fusion recipes.

A single repeated dominant recipe is sufficient to block a pre-render pass or fail a rendered result, as applicable.

#### 9.2.24 Colour architecture audit

Confirm that the population contains materially distinct **colour architectures**, not artificially different colour counts.

Inspect for each character:

- dominant and subordinate hue families where present;
- location of the darkest and lightest masses;
- value hierarchy;
- saturation hierarchy;
- warm/cool organization;
- material-specific colour behavior;
- hair/body relationship;
- eye/body relationship;
- appendage/body relationship where applicable;
- cavity/interior/exterior relationship where applicable;
- focal accent placement;
- distribution across the silhouette.

Monochrome, bichromatic, restrained, or highly polychromatic characters are all valid. Multiple characters may use the same approximate hue count. The population fails only when the **actual colour logic and distribution** converge into one repeated recipe.

#### 9.2.25 Face, head, hair, and character-identity audit

Each character must independently have:

- deliberately authored cranial/head geometry;
- distinct face length/width and feature relationships;
- developed eyes with character-specific geometry;
- developed hair with independent mass, roots, framing, and silhouette;
- coherent pigmentation/markings/material transitions where selected;
- complete identity finishing appropriate to that character;
- coherent relationship between face/head, body, materials, colour/value hierarchy, and dominant visual hook.

Conventional cosmetics are optional. Their absence is not a defect. If makeup or other cosmetics are used, they must reinforce an already distinct face rather than create the only difference between otherwise generic faces.

#### 9.2.26 Proportion-regime quality audit

Every character's proportion regime must remain:

- intentionally authored across the whole body;
- perceptually distinct from the others;
- coherent in head/neck/shoulder/ribcage/waist/hip/lower-torso region/limb/mass relationships;
- attractive or compelling in its chosen appeal mode;
- fully developed and main-character grade;
- fully concept-fused;
- professionally rendered.

Compact/petite bodies must not become miniature or caricaturally compressed. Tall, broad, muscular, soft, full-figured, powerful, elongated, or other regimes must not become one-parameter distortions of a shared mannequin. Extreme stature or mass is permitted but never required.

#### 9.2.27 Macro audit

- the body silhouette reads immediately;
- the five characters read as distinct;
- major concept structures read;
- concept structure hierarchy is clear;
- micro-detail remains subordinate.

#### 9.2.28 2D audit

Confirm:

- drawn linework;
- illustrated shape construction;
- anime/manhwa facial construction;
- controlled 2D shading;
- deliberate highlights;
- coherent illustrated materials;
- embedded-style correspondence;
- clean high-resolution finish.

The style must visibly remain 2D.

#### 9.2.29 Principal-view and pose audit

Confirm for each character:

- one complete uncropped dominant full-body true front;
- face and torso directly frontal with no intentional yaw;
- upright balanced neutral standing;
- arms naturally relaxed and not used as glamour or action posing;
- legs naturally supporting the body without crossed-leg or contrapposto presentation;
- all mandatory morphology and body-bound identity structures remain visibly attributable to the character;
- the principal front alone carries sufficient body, concept, fusion, material, and identity evidence.

Confirm all mandatory exact-profile/direct-rear views use exact analytical geometry and the same frozen body. Missing either required subordinate view fails the initial sheet presentation audit.

#### 9.2.30 Analytical-space audit

Confirm:

- one continuous 16:9 landscape sheet;
- exactly five left-to-right character regions;
- five principal full-body fronts dominate the usable image area;
- complete body and mandatory appendage extents are visible;
- no decorative lower row, face crop, palette strip, material board, concept icon, caption system, or other module consumes analytical character space;
- the mandatory exact profile and direct-rear views remain clearly subordinate and do not reduce front readability;
- the background remains a plain white analytical field;
- no analytical underlayer, studio/reference layer, or fallback coverage garment is present; body construction remains fully responsible for presentation coverage;
- no format drift into non-analytical page composition.

#### 9.2.30A Post-render template-collapse override — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The actual rendered principal fronts must be re-evaluated for structural convergence independently of the pre-generation certification. A pre-generation PASS never shields a rendered template collapse.

Perform a mental subtraction test on each pair: disregard hair styling, palette, markings, surface micro-detail, decorative material patterning, horns/wings/tails as isolated silhouettes, and other secondary identity cues, then compare the remaining body-core construction. The sheet fails if any pair resolves to substantially the same torso/waist/hip/chest/limb chassis with only secondary restyling.

The sheet also fails if **three or more** principal fronts visibly cluster around one common body regime/chassis strongly enough to read as variants of one underlying female template, even when their appendages or materials differ. Likewise, a mandatory appendage class fails if the rendered group collapses to one repeated root/support/planform architecture with only pattern/material variation.

This is an outcome-classification override: mark the image **FIDELITY FAILURE — TEMPLATE COLLAPSE**, preserve the returned image as immutable attempt evidence, preserve the frozen designs separately, and do not silently reinterpret the image as a successful representation of the certified population. No automatic retry is authorized.

#### 9.2.31 Main-character quality audit

Each character independently reaches the embedded quality benchmark.

A single weaker character rejects the population.

Quality cannot be averaged.

Each character must plausibly read as a flagship heroine rather than a variant/filler body. Confirm for every character:

- immediate body-level identity rather than mannequin-plus-restyling;
- a memorable face/eye/hair package with its own appeal mode;
- a body regime that is independently authored rather than one shared chassis with local edits;
- a strong primary visual hook that survives without relying on captions or decorative clutter;
- appendage and topology choices that feel specific to that character rather than borrowed from a common default;
- enough premium finish that the character could stand alone as key promotional roster art.

If one character reads as the obvious leftover, mildest solution, or weakest roster slot, the audit fails and the population returns to refinement.

#### 9.2.32 Final acceptance condition

The sheet is acceptable only when all of the following are simultaneously true:

> **FIVE INDEPENDENT FEMALE CHARACTER IDENTITIES**

+

> **FIVE INDEPENDENT BODY ARCHITECTURES**

> **FIVE INDEPENDENT CONCEPT-FUSION ARCHITECTURES**

> **WHOLE-BODY CONCEPT MATERIALIZATION**

> **VISIBLE CROSS-CONCEPT STRUCTURES**

> **USEFUL STRUCTURAL DIFFERENTIATION**

> **DELIBERATE FACE/HEAD/HAIR AND CHARACTER-IDENTITY AUTHORSHIP**

> **DIVERSE MATERIAL AND INTELLIGENT COLOUR ARCHITECTURES**

> **COMPLETE RECOGNIZABLE CONCEPT VOCABULARY WITHOUT DIFFERENTIATION-BY-OMISSION**

> **DELIBERATE VISUAL HIERARCHY + DENSITY RHYTHM + CONTROLLED INTERNAL CONTRAST**

> **CONCEPT COMPLETENESS WITHOUT MAXIMUM SATURATION**

> **AESTHETIC SEARCH / TASTE PASS FOR ALL FIVE**

> **FINAL-REFINED RENDER-READY CHARACTER QUALITY FOR ALL FIVE**

> **MAIN-CHARACTER QUALITY FOR ALL FIVE**

> **PROPORTION-REGIME QUALITY FOR ALL FIVE**

> **SIMPLE MACRO / INTRICATE MICRO**

> **NATIVE EMBEDDED-STYLE 2D RENDERING**

> **FIVE DOMINANT TRUE FRONTS + mandatory exact profile/direct-rear support**

> **STRICT NEUTRAL STANDING**

> **EACH SHEET IS ONE CONTINUOUS 16:9 LANDSCAPE SHEET**

+

> **THE BROAD PHASE-1 OUTPUT CONTAINS THREE INDEPENDENTLY AUDITED FIVE-CHARACTER SHEETS REPRESENTING ONE FIFTEEN-CHARACTER POPULATION**

=

> **ACCEPTABLE PHASE-1 POPULATION OUTPUT**

Each of the three five-character sheets must pass the complete Phase-1 visual contract for its own image to be called visually compliant; design certification of all fifteen precedes the first initial render. A failed sheet is recorded as failed and is not a compliant population or downstream frozen input; Phase 1 remains active until the user decides what to do next.

Do not patch a structural failure with decoration.

Do not patch a concept-fusion failure with external presentation.

Do not patch a differentiation failure with colour alone.

Do not patch a quality failure through indiscriminate generic simplification. If over-design, redundant structures, or motif saturation caused the quality failure, purposeful simplification and consolidation are required while preserving complete concept vocabulary, required interactions, and the character's strongest identity.

Do not patch a format failure by approximating the requested layout.

#### 9.2.33 Pre-generation gate additions — HARD / ABSOLUTE

Before each sheet is rendered, certify all of the following:

1. exactly five independently authored female identities are complete;
2. every character is fully body-resolved before rendering;
3. every active concept has major body-scale visibility;
4. every concept participates in pairwise and higher-order fusion where applicable;
5. no concept is dependent on external presentation;
6. all five fusion architectures are materially different;
7. aperture systems are independently authored where applicable;
8. appendage systems are independently authored where applicable;
9. body regimes are independently authored;
10. face/head/hair and character identity are independently authored; conventional cosmetics are optional;
11. colour architecture is intelligently differentiated without a numeric hue-count quota;
12. materially different substances receive visibly distinct 2D texture/shadow/highlight/edge treatment;
13. the complete major recognizable vocabulary of every primary concept is preserved without differentiation-by-omission;
14. all required pairwise and higher-order interactions physically exist;
15. concept completeness is achieved without maximum saturation or redundant structures;
16. visual hierarchy, density rhythm, quiet regions, and controlled internal contrast are deliberate;
17. every character passes the explicit aesthetic/taste rejection stage and its own premium appeal mode;
18. petite or other extreme proportion solutions remain fully developed where used;
19. simple macro hierarchy is secure before micro enrichment;
20. the active 2D style language is explicitly selected and serialized;
21. every principal front is geometrically true and strictly neutral;
22. any mandatory exact profile/direct-rear views is exact, consistent, and subordinate;
23. the sheet contains no presentation module outside the certified analytical architecture;
24. the Frozen Character Specification and Clean Renderer-Facing Description contain positive concrete construction language rather than a prohibition dump;
25. the execution gate in §1.9.1 is satisfied through complete frozen design state, renderer serialization, preflight, a SUPPORTED Renderer Capability Gate result, and READY TO RENDER state;
26. every character has passed the final-refinement barrier and the fifteen frozen specifications are render-ready final designs rather than structural drafts or placeholder states;
27. the Clean Renderer-Facing Description has been compiled by visual priority under §2.14, contains no audit/proof dumping, and preserves all render-critical visible decisions without burying the dominant silhouette, face/hair identity, fusion architecture, material/value hierarchy, or view contract in repetitive text;
28. every character passes the image-generation robustness gate in §2.3.3: identity-critical structures are legible at sheet scale, attachment/overlap ambiguities are resolved, and micro-detail is not carrying the primary design.
29. no major recognizable characteristic was downgraded to supporting/optional merely to create population variety; required morphology classes are retained and differentiated through architecture.
30. the fifteen principal higher-order fusion carriers do not share one repeated causal mechanism with only locus/shape/material substitutions.
31. every primary concept is immediately readable in the dominant front at sheet scale; subordinate profile/rear views supplement rather than rescue concept identity.
32. Sub-concept-1 assignments do not reveal a habitual source-family template within any sheet; repeats remain allowed when independently justified and physically distinct.
33. every claimed random Sub-concept-2 selection names an actual random mechanism in the written audit record; unrecorded model choice cannot pass as randomness.
34. the final Clean Renderer-Facing Description contains visual construction only and is free of file/citation/workflow/audit/randomization/non-execution metadata.

Any unresolved item blocks that sheet from generation.
#### 9.2.34 Phase-1 invariants and pre-render correction targeting — HARD / ABSOLUTE

The following are absolute invariants for every initial Broad Phase-1 generation:

1. **All fifteen characters are governed by the same Primary Input Specification; fixed slots remain fixed, while selector/wildcard slots resolve independently per candidate and then freeze.**
2. **Every concept materially constructs the body.**
3. **The same concepts produce materially different physical solutions.**
4. **No character derives from another independent CREATE character.**
5. **Female-character identity and complete concept fusion are simultaneous requirements.**
6. **Within each sheet, five full-body proportion/body-regime solutions are perceptually distinct; global review prevents severe chassis reuse across all fifteen.**
7. **Chest, torso, hip/lower-torso region, limbs, skeleton, mass, topology and silhouette remain active differentiation systems.**
8. **Required appendage classes remain present while their architecture varies radically.**
9. **Three-concept fusion creates at least one higher-order shared construction.**
10. **Every principal Phase-1 character view is strictly standing, neutral, and true-front.**
11. **Each initial sheet has exactly five dominant full-body true fronts, five exact profiles, and five direct rears.**
12. **The mandatory exact profile and direct-rear views depict the same frozen body and remain subordinate to the dominant front.**
13. **Each of the three initial sheets is one continuous exact 16:9 landscape image with five left-to-right regions and fifteen total full-body depictions.**
14. **Simple macro structure outranks intricate microdetail.**
15. **Every renderer-visible structural decision traces to frozen design state.**
16. **Every primary concept remains unmistakable at macro or strong-meso scale.**
17. **All fifteen characters remain independent physical identities; checkpoint-strength proof applies per sheet and a global collision review rejects severe reuse.**
18. **Material and colour architecture meaningfully differentiate the population.**
19. **Every character meets the same flagship/main-character-grade quality floor.**
20. **Concept completeness does not require maximum saturation; additional structures earn their place.**
21. **Different materials receive distinct material-specific 2D illustrated behavior.**
22. **A complete Frozen Character Specification and Clean Renderer-Facing Description exist before every image call.**
23. **Concept-integrated intrinsic coverage is resolved as body-owned design before freeze and follows the character's form.**
24. **Concept-integrated intrinsic coverage preserves high-intensity character presence, feminine silhouette readability, appendage roots, topology, and identity rather than redefining them.**
25. **The renderer preflight passes geometry, design completeness, intrinsic-coverage fidelity, serialization, and description-clarity checks before submission.**
26. **The renderer receives explicit female-character body architecture; a generic `humanoid` label is insufficient.**
27. **Only final-refined render-ready designs are image-eligible.**
28. **The Clean Renderer-Facing Description is a prioritized visual compilation rather than a transcript of internal analysis.**
29. **Identity is carried by macro and strong-meso design; microdetail enriches rather than substitutes.**
30. **A creation/development command stops at READY TO RENDER after internal certification; only an explicit user `RENDER` command authorizes one image-generation call.**
31. **A post-render image never retroactively changes the frozen design.**
32. **Any subsequent image call requires a new explicit user `RENDER` command; serializer correction may alter only renderer serialization or non-design generation parameters, never the Frozen Character Specification or intrinsic coverage.**
### 9.3 Static strict body-materialisation regression test — HARD / ABSOLUTE

This is a **text-only/static simulation test** and never authorizes image generation. At integration/audit time, test several representative primary combinations that collectively include concrete morphology, material, force/process/element/phenomenon, abstract/behavioural/psychological concepts, and at least one combination of three or more primary concepts.

For every simulated candidate verify all of the following:

1. every primary concept materially alters at least one macro or strong-meso character/body system;
2. every classified major recognizable characteristic remains present;
3. every required pair mutually deforms at least one genuine shared physical construction;
4. a 3+ concept set produces at least one unmistakable macro/strong-meso complete-set higher-order construction;
5. counterfactual removal of any primary concept forces consequential redesign;
6. no primary concept relies solely on colour, marking, hair, expression, pose, micro-detail, accessory/object, emitted effect, surrounding phenomenon, environment, semantic label, or renderer inference;
7. concept recognition survives suppression of labels, scene, props, emitted effects, pose/expression-only cues, colour-only cues, cosmetics, and micro-detail;
8. no realism normalization has weakened a valid concept construction;
9. the result remains a premium authored female anime/manhwa character rather than a specimen/object/monster template with cosmetic femininity;
10. canonical knowledge from `Concepts.md` is used as concept authority without treating one listed manifestation as a maximum or allowing it to lower §5.3/§5.4 embodiment requirements.

Any failure blocks integration/certification and returns to the responsible primary-materialization stage. Donor/companion wording can add concept knowledge but can never lower this test.


## 10 FAILURE / CORRECTION / RECOVERY

### 10.1 Unlimited correction before generation

**Rule.** Every unresolved or failed design requirement must be corrected before generation. There is no fixed pre-render revision limit.

**Explanation.** Revision may return to concept decomposition, whole-body construction, population comparison, subordinate selection, face/head/hair or identity finishing, material/colour resolution, aesthetic hierarchy, view planning, description materialization, or serialization according to the actual failing layer. Preserve genuinely unrelated passing decisions; recheck dependencies. A structurally passing system that is causally responsible for an aesthetic/taste failure is not protected from pre-render redesign. Repetition of the same weak description is not a revision.

**Application.** Two bodies share the same torso and aperture recipe. Return to independent complete construction. A new hair colour is insufficient. If the corrected torso changes the tail root, update that root and every required view before repeating the gate.

**Validation.** No FAIL or UNRESOLVED requirement remains. Every claimed pass has blueprint and certified renderer-description evidence. No stage delegates resolution to the renderer.

### 10.2 Explicit RENDER execution and same-design resubmission control

**Rule.** Final-refined frozen design state, serialization, preflight, and a SUPPORTED Renderer Capability Gate produce READY TO RENDER state only. They never authorize image generation by themselves. Only an explicit user `RENDER` command authorizes image generation. For the initial Broad Phase-1 three-sheet ready state, a bare `RENDER` authorizes the full unattempted READY initial-sheet set in numerical order, with exactly one image-generation call per targeted sheet. `RENDER SHEET 2` or another explicit targeted form authorizes only that target. Outside that initial three-sheet batch behavior, one `RENDER` authorizes exactly one image-generation call. CREATE / CONCEPT / ALTER / REFINE / FUSE never call image generation.

For the initial Broad Phase-1 population, Sheet 1, Sheet 2, and Sheet 3 may each be independently READY after all fifteen designs have been frozen and each sheet-local FINAL RENDER CONTEXT has passed preflight/capability checks. A bare `RENDER` targets all unattempted READY initial sheets in numerical order. `RENDER SHEET 2` targets only Sheet 2 when ready. There is no separate implicit `RENDER ALL` because bare `RENDER` already serves as the explicit all-ready initial-sheet command. No `n=3` or one-call alternative generation is permitted for the same sheet.

Immediately before each authorized submission:

1. confirm the target READY TO RENDER Revision-ID or sheet revision set still matches frozen current state;
2. rerun FINAL RENDER CONTEXT integrity for that exact target;
3. rerun the Renderer Capability Gate;
4. confirm all render-critical macro/strong-meso decisions and presentation facts remain present;
5. confirm the operative context is direct frozen-runtime serialization, not a mandatory file/capsule hydration path;
6. create the immutable attempt reservation containing the authorization, target revision set, FINAL RENDER CONTEXT identity, intended output geometry, and generation settings **before** the image call begins.

If any check fails or capability is NO/UNKNOWN, mark HANDOFF UNSUPPORTED, preserve state, and make no image call.

After the call, preservation precedes audit: capture any accessible returned image/reference immediately and bind it to the precommitted attempt reservation before performing visual inspection or writing an outcome summary. When the runtime supplies image bytes or a stable file/artifact path, preserve that exact asset and, **when a tool-supported copy/materialize/export action is actually available**, immediately create a durable conversation-owned copy before audit. When the runtime supplies only a stable opaque reference, preserve the reference and do not claim a durable pixel copy. If no copy/export capability exists, record that limitation rather than inventing persistence. Then inspect actual pixels/result and record success/failure as an immutable attempt outcome bound to the exact frozen revision/context. The frozen design remains separate from image outcome.

A failure never authorizes an automatic retry, automatic next-sheet call, or silent redesign. A failed Sheet-1 attempt leaves Sheet 2/3 frozen state and READY status unchanged. Another image call always requires another explicit user `RENDER`.

If a failure is purely serialization/handoff while the frozen design remains valid, repair only that non-design layer, rerun serialization/preflight/capability checks, return the exact same design to READY TO RENDER, and require another explicit `RENDER`. No identity-bearing frozen decision may be weakened or silently changed to obtain a pass. Never hide a retry behind a new ID.

**Validation.** Every image-attempt record points to one explicit `RENDER` authorization, one frozen revision target, one FINAL RENDER CONTEXT, and at most one image-generation call. There is no automatic retry branch and no multi-sheet call branch.
### 10.3 Attempt outcome classification

**Rule.** Record what actually happened and keep design state separate from image-attempt state.

Classify every unsuccessful outcome as one of:

1. **Design failure** — the frozen design itself is unresolved, contradictory, weak, or in conflict with the active character workflow.
2. **Serialization failure** — the design is resolved but the handoff omitted, contradicted, or ambiguously expressed render-critical information.
3. **Fidelity failure** — a usable image exists but materially deviates from the frozen design or required geometry.
4. **Submission non-execution** — no usable image is produced from an otherwise submitted package.
5. **Technical failure** — image generation fails independently of the resolved design and serialization.
6. **Interrupted / output unrecovered** — the image call was authorized or initiated, but execution ended before any returned image/reference became accessible to the assistant, so pixel persistence cannot be verified. Preserve the precommitted attempt reservation and all frozen inputs; do not invent an image result or infer whether the backend completed it.

Design and serialization failures are corrected at their responsible pre-render layers. Serialization failure, submission non-execution, or interrupted/output-unrecovered state may return the same frozen design to READY TO RENDER only after the attempt state is reconciled and normal recertification passes; every subsequent image call requires a new explicit user `RENDER` command under §10.2. Fidelity failure preserves both the frozen design and the actual returned image as separate records; it does not authorize silent redesign or automatic retry.

**Validation.** Outcome records describe observed evidence only. Do not invent a hidden technical cause or claim that a rendered image exists when none was returned.
### 10.4 Missing information and interrupted work

**Rule.** Resume from the last verified task state. Do not reconstruct missing source identity or frozen geometry from guesses.

**Explanation.** This document is self-contained as methodology, but a targeted alteration still requires the actual selected candidate state. Preserve completed blueprints, comparison records, Frozen Character Specifications, Clean Renderer-Facing Descriptions, submission records, outcome records, source relationships, and user phase decisions during extended work. A conversation interruption does not start a new phase or reset the concept set.

**Application.** If the selected outfit's rear attachment is absent from available state and materially affects an alteration, recover that state or ask the narrow missing-detail question. Do not substitute a generic rear fastening and call it preserved.

For an interrupted render, the precommitted attempt reservation remains valid evidence that a specific submission was authorized/initiated. If no returned image/reference became accessible before interruption, mark pixel outcome unknown rather than assuming persistence. If the user later supplies a copied screenshot or exported image of that attempt, preserve it as **recovered attempt evidence**, bind it to the most plausible precommitted attempt only when the correspondence is unambiguous, and audit the visible pixels normally. A user-supplied recovery copy does not recreate unavailable generator metadata and never becomes canonical frozen design state.

**Validation.** Confirm current phase, last explicit command, active IDs, frozen dependencies, outstanding work, and attempt reservation state before resuming. Mark unknown data as unknown. Do not retrieve or require an external methodology to operate this document.

### 10.5 Dependency invalidation and preservation

**Rule.** A user-authorized revision invalidates only affected certificates, while all genuinely dependent systems must be rechecked. Do not silently alter downstream systems.

**Explanation.** A body aperture change may invalidate clothing clearance. A sleeve change may invalidate the weapon grip/carrying fit. A hair colour change need not reopen body structure but can affect palette coherence. A Phase-4 assembly error cannot authorize changing the frozen weapon.

**Application.** The user changes a forearm's thickness in Phase 1. Record the new body revision and mark any previously adopted sleeve fit stale. Phase 2 cannot consume the outdated fit as though it still applied. The user still controls when Phase 2 begins or reopens.

**Validation.** Dependency records name exact upstream revisions and affected properties. Preserve unrelated geometry, identity, and accepted artifacts. No automatic redesign occurs outside the active authorized target.

### 10.6 Honest completion reporting

**Rule.** Report the status of the work actually completed. Distinguish processed attempts, compliant images, selected candidates, frozen systems, and phase completion.

**Application.** “One five-character Phase-1 sheet attempted; the image failed due to repeated anatomy and missing mandatory concept morphology; Phase 1 remains active” is coherent. “Phase 1 complete” is invalid unless the user explicitly ended it. “Five compliant characters” is invalid when the sheet failed.

**Validation.** No failed image is counted as a certified downstream input. No missing output is implied to exist. No retrospective audit observation is used to bypass a pre-generation requirement.

**Output-preservation rule.** Each Phase-1 sheet attempt is precommitted before submission and remains an immutable attempt record tied to the exact five frozen character specifications, sheet-local FINAL RENDER CONTEXT, explicit `RENDER` authorization, and generation settings used for it. Any returned image/reference that becomes accessible is attached to that record immediately before audit. When the runtime exposes bytes or a stable file/artifact path and a tool-supported copy/materialize/export action exists, create a durable conversation-owned copy as the first preservation action; otherwise retain the strongest actually available reference and record the persistence limitation. A later revision or explicitly authorized resubmission never silently replaces that attempt. If no returned image/reference becomes accessible because execution is interrupted, preserve the attempt reservation with pixel outcome marked unknown/unrecovered rather than fabricating persistence. Internal blueprints and renderer packages remain unshown unless the user explicitly requests inspection.
### 10.7 Preservation and complete-document maintenance

**Rule.** Maintain this document as one non-versioned, self-contained authority titled Character_Creation.md. Preserve every unique design requirement, construction method, boundary, failure condition, and example that materially improves execution. Exact or near-exact procedural repetition may be consolidated when the canonical replacement is equally explicit and all dependent references remain correct.

**Explanation.** Character creation is the core of the system and must retain its detailed physical decomposition, fusion, body architecture, identity, differentiation, sub-concept, refinement, and renderer-materialization logic. Outfit and weapon creation must likewise retain their substantive design methods. Simplification belongs primarily in duplicated routing, handoff, state, and audit wording. Never shorten a complex design rule into a slogan when doing so removes a decision the designer must actually make.

**Maintenance procedure.** When the user authorizes a rule change, identify its owning section and every dependent restatement; replace conflicting language at those locations; preserve unaffected clauses; expand examples and gates where needed; check phase scope, candidate identity, rendering, and state transitions; retain a private change/preservation record during editing. Do not add inactive historical alternatives to the active rules or leave contradictions to be resolved by an appended patch.

**Application.** Changing the sheet population count requires updating purpose, output contract, state procedure, serialization, validation, and execution formula. A single new paragraph saying “use the latest rule” is not a coherent repair.

**Validation.** Verify no empty operational sections, broken examples, conflicting the fixed fifteen-character / three-sheets-of-five Broad Phase-1 count, external methodological dependencies, document version labels, unsupported gate passes, or implicit/automatic render branches remain. Every substantive source provision must survive, be relocated without loss, or receive an explicitly authorized replacement.
### 10.8 Execution walkthroughs for consistency

**Rule.** Use the following specification walkthroughs when a relevant rule is edited. They test workflow coherence without generating images.

| Situation | Required behavior |
|---|---|
| Two concepts | Resolve both and their pair, with no invented third input |
| Four concepts | Resolve all six pairs and full-set fusion without separate decorative objects |
| Initial CREATE | One fifteen-character population; three exact 16:9 sheets of five; fifteen independent body constructions |
| Explicit `RENDER` | Allowed only for the current READY TO RENDER Revision-ID after FINAL RENDER CONTEXT integrity and Renderer Capability Gate both pass; authorizes exactly one image call |
| Initial five-character sheet before render | Pass five-way divergence matrix, all ten pairwise structural comparisons, distinct Concept-Fusion Architecture checks, and character-family audit on the complete frozen designs; if the group is one base character declined five ways, rebuild before any image call |
| Design operation reaches certified frozen state | Keep blueprints, Frozen Character Specifications, matrices, audit records, randomization records, and Clean Renderer-Facing Descriptions internal unless requested; run preflight and capability gate, then enter READY TO RENDER without submitting an image |
| The Phase-1 sheet returns a usable but nonconforming image | Run the rendered population-collapse audit as part of actual-image inspection; record fidelity failure when the renderer collapses distinct frozen bodies into one repeated chassis; preserve the image and frozen designs; do not silently redesign or auto-regenerate |
| The Phase-1 sheet produces no usable image | Record the outcome; correct only the responsible serialization/submission layer if appropriate; recertify to READY TO RENDER; require a new explicit `RENDER` command for any new image call |
| The render turn is interrupted after submission but before an image/reference becomes accessible | Preserve the precommitted attempt reservation; classify output as interrupted/unrecovered; keep frozen design/context unchanged; if the user later supplies a copy, bind it as recovered attempt evidence and audit it without inventing missing generator metadata |
| Structural similarity before render | Revise complete constructions until evidence supports independence |
| Identity finishing introduces a structural change | Recheck affected earlier structural certificates |
| No concept-required wings or apertures | Record absence only when they are not part of the major recognizable vocabulary and are not required by fusion; do not add optional systems to fill a checklist |
| Abstract concept | Author explicit visual interpretation without story, props, or semantic identity |
| Intrinsic flame-like hair | Resolve bounded rooted body matter; no emitted flame or sparks |
| Defining tail root hidden in front | Prove it in subordinate side/rear without rotating the front |
| Same material in two bodies | Evaluate actual complete construction, not material label alone |
| Same anatomy in two palettes | Fail structural independence |
| Intrinsic coverage conflicts with a defining aperture before freeze | Re-author the concept-derived coverage geometry so it follows the body and preserves the aperture, then re-run affected design gates before freezing |
| Weapon candidate becomes an unreadable mass | Simplify macro while preserving resolved subordinate detail |
| User FUSE within Phase 2 | Create one new integrated outfit candidate; retain source outfits and frozen body |
| User ALTER after rendering | Explicit new revision, source image unchanged, complete gate before a new render |
| Final render context is too noisy or omits certified content | Recompile by visual priority; block handoff until every render-critical fact is present |
| 2D illustration uses gloss | Judge drawn construction; gloss alone is not failure |
| Image looks CGI despite anime face | Fail the independent 2D rendering gate |
| Catalogue complete | Await user selection/development/phase-ending instruction |
| Phase 4 | Preserve chosen body, outfit, and weapon; exact 9:16 analytical true-front/profile/rear assembly sheet on white |
| RESET | Deactivate temporary state without deleting saved artifacts or changing methodology |
## 12 RECURSIVE SELECTOR / WILDCARD RUNTIME

### 12.1 Authority and activation — HARD / ABSOLUTE

`Concepts.md` §4 owns the recursive canonical tree, branch/sub-branch descriptions, aliases, cross-cutting views, and canonical leaf membership. This section owns execution only. SC leaves alone are selectable concepts. BC/SF containers, aliases, mechanism/topology records, and local variants/reference forms never materialize instead of a resolved SC leaf. SC names and IDs remain immutable.

### 12.2 Slot syntax and discovery

Fixed canonical names resolve to their exact SC identity. Fixed user literals absent from the roster continue through the TLCR process and are never autonomous wildcard outcomes. An unqualified named canonical branch/sub-branch remains a constrained selector for compatibility.

`X` draws from the complete eligible canonical SC pool. `X{query}` accepts an ordinary natural-language selector constraint. `X?` or `show branches` may list broad categories, descendant branches, concise descriptions, and descendant counts; discovery performs no draw, freeze, or render.

### 12.3 Query resolution priority — HARD

Inside `X{...}`, resolve the pool constraint in this order:
1. exact canonical branch/sub-branch name;
2. registered branch/sub-branch alias or obvious singular/plural equivalent;
3. unambiguous natural-language branch intent against canonical descriptions and descendants;
4. exact selectable leaf as a singleton pool **only when no applicable branch interpretation exists**. A registered leaf-name synonym or obvious inflection is the same singleton fallback.

Trim whitespace and normalize case without creating a new identity. Match complete compound names and preserve qualifiers; a shared word is not sufficient to reroute a leaf into another family.

At each applicable stage, compare materially plausible interpretations. If multiple valid interpretations remain, present the small relevant candidate set and require clarification before drawing. Do not silently choose a lower-priority leaf or broaden the request to unrestricted `X`. Unknown or empty constrained pools also require clarification. A broad-category node and its identically named sole child denote the same pool rather than two meanings.

### 12.4 Eligibility and uniform leaf sampling — HARD

For a resolved node, recursively collect all descendant SC leaves. For a registered alias/view, union the descendants of its targets. Deduplicate by SC ID, then apply only explicit governing eligibility constraints. The resulting finite set `E` is the complete eligible pool for that slot.

For unrestricted `X`, begin with the complete current canonical SC registry supplied by `Concepts.md`. No branch-depth weighting, family-first lottery, aesthetic ranking, compatibility preference, novelty preference, historical-frequency weighting, population-diversity pressure, or unused-concept preference may change probabilities inside `E`.

Each eligible leaf has probability **1/|E|**. Use a genuine uniform random draw and record the pool and method internally. An authored choice is never represented as a random draw.

A difficult or aesthetically unfamiliar combination is not ineligible merely because it is difficult. Do not filter previously used concepts to manufacture diversity. Later construction weakness is repaired under the same frozen inputs.

### 12.5 Independent draws and body authorship — HARD

Resolve every autonomous slot independently for every candidate. Sampling is with replacement across candidates: a leaf or complete Resolved Primary Concept Set may repeat across a sheet or across the initial fifteen. Input repetition is not itself a diversity failure.

Every candidate still requires independently authored complete concept fusion, body regime, proportions, topology, material distribution, face/eyes/hair, colour, appendage construction, silhouette, and identity consequences. Repeated complete body solutions fail; repeated input identities do not.

### 12.6 Domain and category views

Consume domain/category views from `Concepts.md` alongside roots, branches, descriptions, and aliases. A view is a search pool over canonical nodes/leaves, not a second canonical membership. Resolve every view to its deduplicated eligible descendant SC set before uniform sampling.

### 12.7 Recursive branch registry

Consume `Concepts.md` §4 directly. The registry permits leaves and child branches at the same node and arbitrary future depth. Every SC must have exactly one canonical path; all ancestor selectors resolve to that same leaf identity. Branches and sub-branches are containers only and never materialize as character concepts.

### 12.8 Local variants / reference forms — NON-SELECTABLE

`Concepts.md` §§6 and 8 own variant/reference disposition. Such records may refine their canonical leaf after resolution but cannot become autonomous SC draws.

### 12.9 Topology-only and mechanism exclusion — HARD

Mechanism/topology knowledge may explain construction but never enters a selectable pool unless separately admitted as a canonical SC leaf. Similar names do not convert non-selectable knowledge into concepts.

### 12.10 Resolution-ledger certification

Before Phase-1 concept decomposition, record each candidate's original slot, interpretation route, canonical pool or fixed/TLCR identity, any explicit eligibility constraint, uniform draw method where applicable, resolved leaf, and freeze state. The ledger is internal evidence and never enters renderer-facing documents.

### 12.11 Post-resolution equivalence

Every resolved canonical leaf consumes its complete canonical entry exactly as a fixed literal would. A branch/sub-branch contributes no generic anatomy, materialization shortcut, or concept label to the character; it only defines the selection pool.

### 12.12 User control and freeze

A user-fixed literal always outranks autonomous selection. Unqualified exact canonical leaf names resolve literally; branch-first precedence applies specifically inside constrained `X{...}` and explicit selector-family use.

Freeze each candidate's resolved inputs before body construction. Construction weakness does not authorize substitution or reroll. Reopening requires explicit user change/UNFREEZE authorization and normal dependency invalidation.

## 11 CONCLUSION / FINAL EXECUTION FORMULA

The complete workflow is:

> **PRIMARY INPUT SPECIFICATION → FIXED / TLCR / SELECTOR-FAMILY / X / X{query} RESOLUTION → FIFTEEN FROZEN RESOLVED PRIMARY SETS → ZERO-INFERENCE EXTRACTION + CANONICAL CONCEPT-KNOWLEDGE LOADING → GENERALIZED PHYSICAL-PROPERTY DECOMPOSITION → MAJOR / SUPPORTING / OPTIONAL RECOGNIZABLE-VOCABULARY CLASSIFICATION → MAXIMAL-INTENSITY COMPLETE CONCEPT FUSION → FIFTEEN INDEPENDENT BODY-SOLUTION SEARCHES → COMPLETE CONCEPT MATERIALIZATION PER CHARACTER → PAIRWISE + COMPLETE-SET HIGHER-ORDER FUSION PROOF PER CHARACTER → THREE CHECKPOINT-STRENGTH FIVE-WAY STRUCTURAL DIFFERENTIATION PROOFS + ALL TEN PAIRWISE COMPARISONS PER SHEET → GLOBAL FIFTEEN-CHARACTER COLLISION REVIEW → BODY / PROPORTION / TOPOLOGY / APPENDAGE / APERTURE DIVERGENCE WHERE APPLICABLE → STRUCTURAL CERTIFICATION → AESTHETIC SEARCH / TASTE REJECTION → SUB-CONCEPT 1 REASONED ACTUAL ERA/CULTURE/CIVILIZATION SELECTION + SUB-CONCEPT 2 GENUINE RANDOM OPEN-RESERVOIR DRAW → SUBORDINATE INTEGRATION → RECHECK AFFECTED STRUCTURE → REASONED OR EXPLICIT DESIGN-DIRECTION ROUTING WITHIN CONCEPT-VALID SPACE → FACE / HEAD / EYE / HAIR + CHARACTER-IDENTITY AUTHORSHIP → MATERIAL-SPECIFIC 2D TEXTURE DIFFERENTIATION → INTELLIGENT COLOUR ARCHITECTURE → CONCEPT-INTEGRATED INTRINSIC-COVERAGE RESOLUTION → VISUAL HIERARCHY / DENSITY RHYTHM / CONTROLLED INTERNAL CONTRAST → CONCEPT COMPLETENESS WITHOUT MAXIMUM SATURATION → INTERNAL ALTERNATIVE-DESIGN SEARCH → FOUR-PASS FINAL CHARACTER REFINEMENT → PREMIUM CHARACTER-APPEAL PASS → MAIN-CHARACTER QUALITY PARITY → EXTREME-PROPORTION QUALITY WHERE USED → SIMPLE MACRO / INTRICATE MICRO → WEAKEST-CANDIDATE REJECTION / QUALITY PARITY → IMAGE-GENERATION ROBUSTNESS GATE → FREEZE ALL FIFTEEN COMPLETE CHARACTER SPECIFICATIONS → COMPILE THREE SHEET REVISION SETS → MANDATORY FRONT + EXACT 90-DEGREE PROFILE + DIRECT REAR FOR EVERY IDENTITY → THREE 16:9 LANDSCAPE SHEETS / FIVE REGIONS EACH → COMPILE THREE SHEET-LOCAL SHORTEST-LOSSLESS FINAL RENDER CONTEXTS → RENDER PREFLIGHT + RENDERER CAPABILITY GATE PER SHEET → INDEPENDENT READY TO RENDER STATES → EXPLICIT USER `RENDER` → TARGET-SHEET FINAL CONTEXT INTEGRITY + CAPABILITY RECHECK → EXACTLY ONE TARGET-SHEET IMAGE GENERATION CALL → PRESERVE ACCESSIBLE IMAGE/REFERENCE → RECORD IMMUTABLE ATTEMPT → ACTUAL-IMAGE AUDIT → OUTCOME CLASSIFICATION → USER-CONTROLLED NEXT ACTION → USER-CONTROLLED PHASE CONTINUATION**

The design system remains exhaustive; the renderer handoff remains compact and concrete. The internal specification owns character identity and geometry. Concept-integrated intrinsic coverage is authored as part of the body before freeze and follows the character rather than overriding her form. The renderer receives only the resulting visible structures and required camera/layout/rendering facts, never unresolved choices or workflow objectives. Public written specifications are optional inspection outputs. Certified design ends at READY TO RENDER; image generation occurs only after an explicit user `RENDER` command and a current SUPPORTED handoff.
